| **ENT-SEC-NO-TLS-INTERNAL phase 14 — Qdrant TLS** | Added optional local and mandatory fail-closed production TLS for Qdrant REST and gRPC. A one-shot initializer copies the shared internal certificate into a named volume, restricts the root-owned private key to `0600`, and prevents Qdrant startup when production TLS material is absent. Laravel retrieval, upsert, and storage validation share a CA-aware client; Python infrastructure and polyglot smoke clients use destination-scoped SSL contexts. Production Compose removes host ports, forces `https://qdrant:6333`, mounts the CA read-only for app/queue/scheduler, and a 37-check CI validator rejects plaintext clients, TLS server bypass, unexpected certificate paths, missing CA mounts, and the image-incompatible legacy curl healthcheck. Live isolated Qdrant 1.12.1 proof confirmed TLS on REST and gRPC, CA- and hostname-verified Python/Laravel requests returned 200, plaintext HTTP failed, and an untrusted CA was rejected. Verification passed 23 PHP tests (57 assertions), 19 TLS Python regression tests, 9 certificate-generator tests, targeted PHPStan/Pint, and both Compose renders. | `app/Services/QdrantHttpClient.php`, `scripts/xdr_qdrant_tls_compose_validate.py`, `docker-compose*.yml`, `tests/{Feature/QdrantTlsClientTest.php,scripts/test_qdrant_tls.py}` | — | 2026-08-30 |

| **ENT-SEC-NO-TLS-INTERNAL phase 9 — Python infrastructure client CA verification** | Wired verified private-CA TLS into the two Python downstream services without changing plaintext defaults. All Pandaproxy REST produce/create/subscribe/poll/delete calls now pass a destination-scoped Requests `verify` option only for HTTPS, using `XDR_REDPANDA_CA_CERT` (or the existing internal CA fallback); alert-writer OpenSearch writes similarly use `XDR_OPENSEARCH_CA_CERT`. Added `opensearch-tls` to generated certificate SANs and documented production/example settings. Regression suites passed: alert-writer **126**, incident-builder **121**; both images built; `docker compose config --quiet` and `git diff --check` passed. Live proof used the real services: a one-shot alert-writer image verified the internal CA and received HTTP 200 from `https://redpanda:8083`; another invoked the real `write_opensearch()` path over TLS and indexed one document successfully. | **ENT-SDLC-NO-SUPPLYCHAIN / container image scanning** | Added real container-image vulnerability scanning now that Docker is available. `xdr_container_image_scan.py` runs Trivy 0.70.0 from an immutable digest (important after the March 2026 Trivy ecosystem compromise), scans HIGH/CRITICAL OS/package findings, aggregates machine-readable advisory evidence, and treats CVEs as WARN while scanner/runtime failures fail closed. | **RATE-LIMIT-DOS** | Enterprise DoS hardening (ingestion-gateway) — bounded the number of distinct per-tenant rate-limiter buckets (`XDR_INGEST_MAX_TENANT_BUCKETS`, default 10000). Existing tenants always keep their own bucket; beyond the cap, new tenant IDs share a single lazily-created overflow bucket (still rate-limited) so an authenticated client can't exhaust memory by flooding distinct tenant_ids. Refiller decrements the count on TTL eviction + refills the overflow bucket; `/metrics` exposes `tenant_bucket_count`/`tenant_bucket_max`. Cap is opt-in (`<=0` = legacy). 4 Go tests; `go vet` clean (race detector unavailable — no gcc — but design is atomic-CAS/atomic-counter, race-free). | `services/ingestion-gateway/main.go`, `main_test.go` | — | 2026-07-01 |
| **PERF-GO-HOT-HTTP** | Enterprise hot-path perf (correlation-worker) — added a bounded, TTL, thread-safe `iocCache` in front of `lookupIOC` so repeated indicators in a batch don't re-issue a synchronous HTTP GET each time. Split `lookupIOC` (cache) from `fetchIOC` (HTTP): only definitive HTTP-200 outcomes (match / explicit no-match) are cached; transient errors/non-200 are never cached (retried). `XDR_IOC_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS`=60, `XDR_IOC_CACHE_MAX`=10000; `/metrics` exposes `ioc_cache_hits`. 5 Go tests (get/set/expiry, negative cached, bounded, cache-repeated-via-httptest, no-cache-on-error); `go vet` clean. | `services/correlation-worker/main.go`, `ioc_cache_test.go` | — | 2026-07-01 |
| **AUTH-TIMING-CMP** | Security — constant-time internal-token compare. `verify_internal_token()` in alert-writer + incident-builder replaced `token == expected` (short-circuit, timing side-channel leaking prefix-match length) with `hmac.compare_digest(token.encode(), expected.encode())` (both sites each; `import hmac` added). Now consistent with Go `hmac.Equal` + Laravel `hash_equals`. | **MEM-UNBOUNDED-STATE** | Reliability — bounded the unbounded in-memory state in both Python services (memory growth / OOM at sustained enterprise volume). `SEEN` (alert-writer) is now a bounded LRU (`OrderedDict` + `_seen_add()` evicting oldest, cap `XDR_ALERT_WRITER_SEEN_MAX`=100000) — the DB `ON CONFLICT` upserts remain the source of truth for idempotency, so `SEEN` is a safe-to-bound fast-path. `DLQ` in **both** services is now a fixed-size `deque(maxlen=…)` ring (`XDR_ALERT_WRITER_DLQ_MAX` / `XDR_INCIDENT_BUILDER_DLQ_MAX`, default 1000); the `/dlq` view's `DLQ[-20:]` slice → `list(DLQ)[-20:]` (deque isn't sliceable). Behavior-neutral for the valid path; append-only tables untouched. +6 Python tests (ring bound + overflow-drops-oldest both services; SEEN LRU evict + idempotent add). 39 service tests pass. | `services/alert-writer-service/main.py`, `services/incident-builder-service/main.py` + tests | — | 2026-07-05 |
| **FASTAPI-LIFESPAN** | Modernization — replaced the deprecated `@app.on_event("startup"/"shutdown")` hooks (removed in modern FastAPI) with a `@contextlib.asynccontextmanager lifespan(...)` passed as `FastAPI(lifespan=…)` in both Python services. The prior startup/shutdown bodies (validate secrets, launch consumer threads / set `STOP`) became plain `_startup_tasks()`/`_shutdown_tasks()` invoked by the lifespan; consumer semantics unchanged. Prevents a silent break on a future `fastapi`/`uvicorn` bump (`requirements.txt` pins only `>=0.110`). +6 Python tests (no-on_event source guard, lifespan/helpers exist, shutdown sets STOP — both services). 45 service tests pass; both modules byte-compile. | `services/alert-writer-service/main.py`, `services/incident-builder-service/main.py` + tests | — | 2026-07-05 |

Verified by installing the exact pins into a disposable venv and importing each service's real (non-stubbed) `main.py`, then driving `/health`+`/metrics` through the real FastAPI lifespan (also re-confirms FASTAPI-LIFESPAN works against real, not stubbed, FastAPI). `docker compose config --quiet` exit 0. **Documented limitation:** full hash-locking (`pip-compile --generate-hashes`) deferred — needs a Linux-matching build environment to verify safely; Docker daemon unavailable in this session (engine not running) so no actual image build could be performed. | `services/alert-writer-service/Dockerfile`, `services/incident-builder-service/Dockerfile`, `services/ai-rag-service/Dockerfile`, 3× `requirements.txt` | — | 2026-07-05 |

| **PIPE-CONSUMER-AUTH-500** | **Critical bug fix** — the internal consumer event loop (`process_alerts()` in both services) called the FastAPI route function directly (`write()`/`build()`), leaving `x_internal_service_token` at its literal Python default — the real `fastapi.params.Header` marker object under real FastAPI (the test stub's `Header` mock masked this by returning `None`). Under the `docker-compose.prod.yml` posture (`XDR_ENFORCE_INTERNAL_AUTH=true` + a configured per-service token), this made `verify_internal_token()` call `.encode()` on a `Header` object → **`AttributeError`** on every polled batch → the event loop's generic exception handler treated it as a transient error, endlessly recreating the consumer with zero alerts/incidents ever written — pipeline silently and permanently stalled. Fix: extracted `_write_alerts_core(request)` / `_build_incidents_core(request)` (no token parameter at all) out of the routes; `write()`/`build()` now check `verify_internal_token()` then delegate to the core function; `process_alerts()` calls the core function directly, never the route. Reproduced the exact original `AttributeError` and confirmed the fix resolves it end-to-end against real (non-stubbed) FastAPI/pydantic in a disposable venv (both services). +8 unit tests (route delegates to core not itself, core has no token parameter, core succeeds under enforced+token env, route source still contains the auth check). 53/53 service tests pass. | `services/alert-writer-service/main.py`, `services/incident-builder-service/main.py` + tests | — | 2026-07-05 |
| **AW-DEDUPE-BEFORE-COMMIT** | Correctness fix (alert-writer) — `_write_alerts_core()` used to call `_seen_add(fp)` *before* attempting `write_postgres()`, so a Postgres failure still poisoned the durable `SEEN` LRU; any later redelivery/retry of the exact same batch was silently dropped as a "duplicate" despite never actually being persisted (a false-success recovery path). Fix: fingerprints are now staged in a local `seen_in_batch` set (still deduping repeats within the same batch) and only committed into `SEEN` after `write_postgres()` succeeds (`postgres_ok`) or in pure dry-run (no backing store configured at all — the intended demo behavior). Reproduced against real pydantic: a failed write followed by a retry once the store recovers now succeeds instead of being dropped. +4 unit tests (SEEN not marked on failure, marked on success, retry-after-failure not dropped, intra-batch duplicate still deduped) + 1 real-dependency reproduction script. | `services/alert-writer-service/main.py` + test | — | 2026-07-05 |
| **PY-POISON-RECORD-BATCH** | Resilience fix (both services) — `AlertPayload(**row)` in `normalize_records()` was unwrapped, so a single malformed record (e.g. missing a required field) raised an exception that propagated through `event_loop()` and aborted the **entire poll batch** — including every other valid alert/incident in it — then looped the consumer recreate-from-earliest logic forever on the same poison record. Fix: wrapped the construction in a per-record `try/except`; a malformed record is isolated to the bounded `DLQ` ring as `alert_payload_invalid: …` (mirrors the existing envelope-unwrap failure pattern already used one line above it) and the loop continues to the next record. Reproduced against real pydantic with a mixed valid/invalid batch: the malformed record is isolated and the valid record in the same batch is still recovered and processed. +6 unit tests (3/service: no-raise, DLQ isolation, try/except present in source) + 1 real-dependency reproduction. | `services/alert-writer-service/main.py`, `services/incident-builder-service/main.py` + tests | — | 2026-07-05 |

Added a pure, testable `shouldRefuseIngestSecret(secret, enforced) bool` + `envBool()` helper; `validateStartupSecrets()` now `log.Fatalf`s (refuses to start) when enforced and the secret is empty/dev-default — mirroring the existing fail-fast pattern already used by correlation-worker/normalizer-worker for their own internal tokens. | **IG-HMAC-REPLAY** | Security hardening (ingestion-gateway + endpoint-agent) — ingest HMAC signed the request body only, so a captured `(signature, body)` pair was replayable forever. `verifySignature()` now accepts an optional `X-XDR-Timestamp` header; when present, the signature covers `timestamp + "." + body` (sigv2) and is rejected if the timestamp falls outside a `XDR_INGEST_SIG_TOLERANCE_SECONDS` window (default 300s) — bounding the replay window instead of leaving it open indefinitely. Legacy body-only (sigv1) signatures are still accepted by default for backward compatibility with any sender not yet upgraded; `XDR_INGEST_SIGV2_REQUIRED=true` forces the gateway to reject sigv1 once all senders have migrated. `endpoint-agent`'s `sign_body()` gained an optional `timestamp` parameter (legacy behavior preserved when omitted) and `GatewayClient._send_raw()` now sends `X-XDR-Timestamp` + a sigv2 signature by default. +8 Go tests (no-secret bypass, legacy v1 accepted/rejected-when-required, v2 valid/stale/future/wrong-sig/malformed-timestamp) + 5 Python tests (v1/v2 signing, header wiring, no-secret path). `go build`/`go vet`/`go test` and endpoint-agent's 191-test suite green. | `services/ingestion-gateway/main.go`, `services/endpoint-agent/agent.py` + tests | — | 2026-07-05 |
| **PY-PRINT-LOGGING** | Best-practice modernization (alert-writer, incident-builder) — replaced all ~48 production `print(..., flush=True)` calls in both services' event-loop/consumer/startup-validation code with a shared `logging.Logger` (`log`) emitting structured JSON-line records (`ts`/`level`/`service`/`message`) via a small `_JsonLogFormatter`. Level mapping preserved each call site's existing semantic: `WARN`/`SECURITY-WARN` → `log.warning`, `ERROR`/`SECURITY-FATAL` → `log.error`, everything else (ready/starting/consumed/started/`SECURITY` notices) → `log.info`. | **SIEM-SEARCH** | Read-only free-form search over raw alert telemetry — `SiemSearchService` queries the `xdr-alerts` OpenSearch index via a bounded `query_string` DSL (tenant_id term filter + detected_at range filter, `MAX_RESULTS=500`/`DEFAULT_MAX_RESULTS=100`/`MAX_QUERY_WINDOW_DAYS=30`/`MIN_QUERY_LENGTH=2`), falling back to a bounded Postgres `ILIKE` search over `security_alerts` (`alert_type`/`ip`/`detector_name`/`evidence::text`/`raw_event::text`) when OpenSearch is unreachable or returns non-2xx — mirrors the platform's existing OpenSearch graceful-degradation posture rather than introducing a new failure mode. New `SiemSearchController` + single server-rendered `/siem-search` GET route (no separate JSON API — form submits `?q=` as a query string) gated by new `soc:search.view` permission (admin/analyst/viewer — read-only, granted broadly like other `.view` abilities). | **DATA-RESIDENCY-ERASURE** | Adds per-tenant retention overrides + a GDPR right-to-erasure workflow, closing the gap where `SecurityRetentionCommand` only had a fixed global 30/90-day prune and never touched `security_incidents` at all. New `tenant_retention_policies` table (mutable, unique per tenant) overrides the global `--events-days`/`--alerts-days`/`--incidents-days` defaults; `security_events` has no `tenant_id` column (documented platform-wide gap, `TenantBoundaryService::UNISOLATED_TABLES`) so it stays globally-pruned by design, while `security_alerts`/`security_incidents` are now pruned per-tenant with legacy `tenant_id IS NULL` rows falling back to the global default. `security_incidents` pruning is new — it was never pruned before this change. New `DataResidencyErasureService` implements the erasure request lifecycle: `pending` → `approved`/`rejected` (self-approval blocked — approver must differ from requester) → `executed`. Approving a request only flips its status; **real deletion is CLI-only** via new `php artisan data-erasure:execute {request_id}`, mirroring the platform's existing `php artisan dlq:replay` pattern of never triggering destructive operations over HTTP. A request-level `dry_run` flag (default `true`) makes execution a read-only row count with no approval required; real deletion needs `dry_run=false` AND `status=approved`. Execution is tenant-scoped and only ever touches `security_alerts`/`security_incidents` (never `security_events`, which has no tenant column to scope by). Every request/approve/reject/dry-run/execute action is recorded in the new append-only `data_erasure_audit_events` table. New RBAC abilities: `retention.manage` (admin-only — edit policy, approve/reject) and `erasure.request` (admin+analyst — view page, submit requests); viewer gets neither. New UI at `/data-residency`. Threat hunting domains: +2 (`tenant_retention_policies`, `data_erasure_requests`) → 181 total. Found and fixed one bug during testing: `DataErasureExecuteCommand`'s `orWhere('id', $requestId)` crashed with a Postgres `invalid input syntax for type bigint` error for any non-numeric `request_id` (the command's actual primary identifier) — fixed by only adding that clause when the input is `ctype_digit`. +32 tests (`DataResidencyErasureTest`) covering policy resolution, full request lifecycle, self-approval blocking, dry-run vs. real deletion, tenant scoping, retention-command tenant-awareness, RBAC, and routes. Also attempted `php artisan schema:dump` as an unrelated light task while the full suite ran in the background — it broke 74 tests (serial-column defaults applied inconsistently on `RefreshDatabase` load) and was fully reverted; see `TEST-NO-SCHEMA-DUMP` in `REVIEW_BACKLOG.md` for the retained findings. | **NORM-ASYNC-COMMIT-LOSS** | Fixed the gap where `normalizer-worker`'s Pandaproxy consumer implicitly commits offsets on the *next* `GET .../records` call, while the just-fetched batch's normalized events were only handed to an async producer channel (non-blocking send) — the fetch loop would immediately call `consumerPoll` again (committing) without any regard for whether those events had actually reached Kafka, so a crash between "committed" and "published" could lose up to the full `XDR_NORMALIZER_QUEUE_DEPTH` (default 200,000) buffered events. Fix: each normalized event is now wrapped in a `queuedEvent{event, wg *sync.WaitGroup}`; `consumeOnce` creates a fresh `sync.WaitGroup` per fetch batch, `Add(1)`s it before enqueueing each event, and calls `wg.Wait()` — blocking the next `consumerPoll` (and therefore the implicit commit) — until every event from that batch has been through a completed publish attempt in `producerLoop` (success, or failure recorded to the DLQ; `producerLoop` calls `wg.Done()` in both cases so a permanently-broken produce path can't stall the pipeline forever, matching the existing poison-message-isolation precedent of "isolate and continue" rather than block indefinitely). The HTTP ingestion path (`normalizeHTTP`, `/v1/normalize`) passes a `nil` WaitGroup since it is intentionally fire-and-forget (returns 202 before publish, with its own existing queue-depth backpressure check). Also fixed a related bug surfaced by the same code path: on a producer flush failure, the DLQ record previously included only `{"error": ..., "count": N}` — the actual failed event payloads were never written anywhere, making them permanently unreplayable; the DLQ record now includes the full `events` array. Verified with a genuine regression test (`TestCommitDoesNotRaceAheadOfPublish`, using an `httptest.Server` mock of the Pandaproxy consumer/produce API) that specifically proves the *ordering* guarantee — the mock's second-poll handler snapshots an event log and asserts `"publish"` is already present; confirmed this test actually catches the old bug by temporarily removing the `wg.Wait()` call and re-running (failed with the expected message), then restored the fix and re-ran clean. +3 more tests: nil-WaitGroup doesn't block (HTTP path), WaitGroup blocks until publish completes (success path, using a held-open mock HTTP handler), and the DLQ full-events fix. All 4 new tests pass; `go build`/`go vet` clean. `go test -race` unavailable in this environment (`CGO_ENABLED` requires gcc, absent) — tested without the race detector; the WaitGroup/channel pattern used has no unsynchronized shared-memory access outside of already-atomic counters, so this is a tooling gap rather than an unverified concurrency risk, but should be re-run with `-race` in an environment with a C toolchain before relying on it further. Pre-existing `gofmt` non-compliance in this file (inconsistent struct-field alignment) predates this change (confirmed via `git stash`) and was left alone — out of scope for a focused bugfix. | `services/normalizer-worker/main.go`, `services/normalizer-worker/main_test.go` | — | 2026-07-09 |
| **CONSUMER-GROUP-EPHEMERAL** | Fixed all 5 Pandaproxy consumer loops across `alert-writer-service` (`event_loop`, `shadow_event_loop`, `dlq_consumer_event_loop`, `pipeline_dlq_consumer_event_loop`) and `incident-builder-service` (`event_loop`) that minted a brand-new ms-timestamp consumer **group** name (`f"{base}-{int(time.time()*1000)}"`) on every process start *and* on every in-process reconnect (consecutive-error or DLQ-write-failure recovery, not just genuine offset errors) — since Kafka/Redpanda consumer groups own committed-offset state, a fresh group name has no prior offset to resume from and `auto.offset.reset=earliest` reprocesses the entire topic history every single time. Split each loop's identity generation into three concerns: `_stable_group()` (the env-configurable base name, e.g. `alert-writer-v1`, with **no** timestamp suffix — reused across restarts and across non-offset-error reconnects so Redpanda resumes from the last real committed offset), `_new_instance_id()` (still ms-based, regenerated on every connect/reconnect — this is the separate Pandaproxy REST *instance* id, kept unique specifically to avoid instance-id collisions on the proxy side, distinct from the group), and `_fresh_group_for_offset_reset()` (ms-suffixed, used **only** when `is_offset_err` is true — the one legitimate case where the previously-committed offset is actually invalid and a clean group is needed to re-apply `auto.offset.reset`). The existing three-way `should_recreate` trigger (offset error OR 3 consecutive errors OR DLQ-write failure) is unchanged — only the *group* is now conditionally recreated (`if is_offset_err: group = _fresh_group_for_offset_reset()`), while the instance id always regenerates. All existing tests (74 across both services) passed unchanged, confirming no test depended on the old ms-timestamp group behavior — added 6 new tests (3 per service, `TestConsumerGroupStability`) that lock in the new contract: group stays identical across a 3-consecutive-error recreation, group changes (with a `-reset-` suffix derived from the stable base) only on a genuine offset error, and the default initial group has no timestamp suffix at all. Full alert-writer + incident-builder suite: 80 tests, all green. | `services/alert-writer-service/main.py`, `services/incident-builder-service/main.py`, `tests/alert_writer/test_alert_writer.py`, `tests/incident_builder/test_incident_builder.py` | — | 2026-07-09 |

| **ENT-SDLC-NO-SUPPLYCHAIN** (base-image digest pinning only; SBOM/scan/sign remain open — need syft/trivy/cosign, unavailable in this environment) | Verified the "no dep pinning/lock" half of this finding was already largely false: all 3 Python `requirements.txt` files (`ai-rag-service`, `alert-writer-service`, `incident-builder-service`) already pin every direct dependency to an exact version (`fastapi==0.139.0`, etc.), and all 3 Go services (`correlation-worker`, `ingestion-gateway`, `normalizer-worker`) have zero external dependencies (stdlib-only `go.mod`, no `go.sum` needed or missing). The real, previously-unaddressed gap was all 6 Dockerfiles floating on **mutable** base-image tags (`python:3.12-slim`, `golang:1.26-alpine`, `alpine:3.22`) with no digest pin — meaning a future upstream tag update could silently change what gets built/deployed without any corresponding code change, a real supply-chain risk vector. Fetched the current manifest-list digest for each of the 3 tags directly from the Docker Hub registry v2 API (`auth.docker.io` token exchange + `registry-1.docker.io` manifest HEAD request with `Accept: application/vnd.oci.image.index.v1+json,...`, which returns the multi-arch manifest-list digest, not a single-platform one — verified all 3 digests are exactly 64 hex chars) since the Docker daemon itself is unavailable in this environment (no `docker pull`/`docker inspect` path). Pinned all 6 Dockerfiles to `image:tag@sha256:<digest>` format (keeps the tag for human readability alongside the enforced digest). Verified with `docker compose config --quiet` (parses cleanly) — **not verified**: an actual `docker build`/pull against these digests, since the daemon is unavailable; if a digest is ever wrong (e.g. this environment's network path returned something registry-mirror-specific), the build would fail loudly and obviously rather than silently, so this is a fail-safe risk, not a silent one. | **DATA-TIERING** (phase 1 — archive-before-delete; full hot/warm/cold ClickHouse/object-storage tiering remains open) | Replaced hard-delete with archive-then-prune in `SecurityRetentionCommand`: new `SecurityRetentionArchiveService` writes every row about to be pruned to a gzip-compressed JSONL file (`storage/app/archives/{table}/{tenant-or-global}/{timestamp}.jsonl.gz`) *before* deleting it, so retention no longer means the data is simply gone — a durable local archive exists first. Scoped deliberately to what's testable without new infra: no ClickHouse warm tier or object-storage cold tier (both need live infrastructure this environment can't provision or verify) — this is the bounded "never delete without archiving" step the backlog's own proposed fix listed as a prerequisite ("Replace hard-delete with archive-then-prune"). Handles the `security_events` vs. `security_alerts`/`security_incidents` asymmetry correctly: the former has no `tenant_id` column at all (a documented platform-wide gap), so archiving must skip tenant filtering entirely there rather than treating it as `tenant_id IS NULL` (which would be a different, wrong query) — added an explicit `hasTenantColumn` parameter to keep these two cases from being conflated. New command options: `--archive-dir=` (default `storage/app/archives`) and `--no-archive` (opt out, preserving the exact old fast-delete behavior — used by the 5 pre-existing `DataResidencyErasureTest` retention tests, which were updated to pass it so they don't start writing real files into `storage/app/archives` on every run). Uses `chunkById` for memory-safe archiving of large result sets; skips the archive-file write entirely when zero rows match (no empty files). Used a unique per-run timestamp filename (`Y-m-d_His_u`) rather than one shared per-day file to sidestep any dependency on gzip append-mode semantics across repeated same-day runs. Caught and fixed one real test-harness bug while writing tests: passing Windows temp-dir paths (`C:\Users\...`) as a raw interpolated string to `$this->artisan("... --archive-dir={$path}")` truncated/mis-tokenized the path (colon + backslashes), silently causing `0` rows to be archived where `1` was expected — switched to the array-argument form of `$this->artisan()`, which passes option values without string tokenization. +10 new tests (`SecurityRetentionArchiveTest`): gzip content matches archived rows, `security_events` no-tenant-column path, empty-match no-op (no file created), tenant-scope isolation, command-level default-archives and `--no-archive`-skips-archiving behavior. All pre-existing `DataResidencyErasureTest` retention tests (32) still pass unchanged aside from the added `--no-archive` flag. Full suite: 4687 passed, 0 failures. | `app/Services/SecurityRetentionArchiveService.php`, `app/Console/Commands/SecurityRetentionCommand.php`, `tests/Feature/SecurityRetentionArchiveTest.php`, `tests/Feature/DataResidencyErasureTest.php` | — | 2026-07-10 |

| **TEST-NO-SCHEMA-DUMP** (root-caused and working — retry succeeded) | Root-caused the earlier failed attempt (74 NOT NULL violations on `id` columns) rather than leaving it permanently reverted. Hypothesis-driven investigation confirmed `PostgresSchemaState::load()` invokes a single sequential `psql --file=` run (no statement splitting/reordering) and `dump()` uses unremarkable `pg_dump --no-owner --no-acl --schema-only` — the load/dump mechanism itself was never the bug. The actual cause: `schema:dump` targets whatever `DB_DATABASE` the connection currently has, and the earlier attempt dumped the long-lived **dev** `detector` database, which had drifted from a clean `migrate:fresh` state (e.g. via an out-of-band `psql` session or an odd raw-SQL migration run) — `pg_dump` faithfully reproduced that drift onto a subset of tables. Verified the fix by: (1) recreating `detector_test` clean and running `migrate:fresh` against it explicitly (`DB_DATABASE=detector_test`), (2) generating the dump from *that* database, (3) mechanically diffing the output — 468 `bigint id NOT NULL` tables vs. 469 `ALTER TABLE ... SET DEFAULT nextval` statements (the 1 extra is the `migrations` table's `integer` `id`, using a different type, correctly excluded from the bigint count but still correctly defaulted) — zero missing, (4) replaying Laravel's exact `psql --file=` load command into a disposable database and confirming every `id` column, including the previously-broken `live_pilot_runs`, got its correct `nextval(...)` default, (5) running the **full test suite from an empty `detector_test`** (schema dump auto-loaded by `RefreshDatabase`, zero manual migration): **4687 passed, 0 failures.** Local Postgres client/server versions were confirmed matched (17.7/17.7), ruling out the classic pg_dump/psql version-mismatch failure mode as a contributing factor here. Updated `claude.md`/`docs/guides/TESTING.md` to replace the "tried and reverted, do not retry" caution with the actual root cause and a concrete regeneration rule (always dump from a database you just ran `migrate:fresh` against, never the ambient dev DB). Re-added the schema-dump-existence assertion to `DocumentationFreezeTest` (a prior identical assertion was removed when the first attempt was reverted). Migration files were **not** pruned — no `--prune` flag used, matching the project's established preference to keep migration history intact even with a working dump in place. | `database/schema/pgsql-schema.sql`, `claude.md`, `docs/guides/TESTING.md`, `tests/Feature/DocumentationFreezeTest.php` | — | 2026-07-11 |
| **IDENTITY-SSO-MFA** (TOTP MFA done; SSO/SAML/OIDC federation remains open — needs a real external IdP) | Implemented per-user opt-in TOTP as the MFA half of this finding, scoped down from the full SSO+MFA proposal since SAML/OIDC federation genuinely needs a real external IdP (Okta/Azure AD) to configure and test against, which isn't available in this environment. Built dependency-free (no `laravel/fortify`/`pragmarx/google2fa`): new `TotpService` implements RFC 6238 directly against stdlib `hash_hmac` — `generateSecret()` (160-bit, base32-encoded), `provisioningUri()` (standard `otpauth://` URI any authenticator app — Google Authenticator, Authy, 1Password — can scan/import), `verify()` (±1 period drift tolerance for clock skew). Critically verified against the **RFC's own published test vector**, not just internal self-consistency (generate-a-code-then-verify-the-same-code would pass even with a subtly wrong HMAC construction or truncation offset): independently cross-checked RFC 6238 Appendix B's secret/counter/SHA1 combination in Python first (confirmed the RFC's published 8-digit code, 94287082), then confirmed this service's 6-digit truncation produces the matching last-6-digits value (287082) for the identical inputs. New `users` columns: `mfa_secret` (encrypted at rest via Laravel's built-in `encrypted` cast — never stored in plaintext), `mfa_enabled`, `mfa_confirmed_at`; `mfa_secret` added to the model's `$hidden` array so it's never serialized. New `MfaController` with `setup`/`enable`/`disable` (self-service, requires being logged in) and `challenge`/`verify` (reached mid-login, while logged out, gated purely by a `mfa_pending_user_id` session key). Modified `AuthenticatedSessionController::store()`: after `Auth::attempt()` succeeds, if `$user->mfa_enabled`, immediately reverts that login (`Auth::guard('web')->logout()`) and redirects to the code-entry step — only a verified code completes `Auth::login()` for real. Because `mfa_enabled` defaults `false` and is strictly per-user opt-in, the existing password-only login/demo walkthrough is completely unaffected for every account that hasn't explicitly turned MFA on. +21 tests: 10 `TotpServiceTest` (RFC vector match, base32 round-trip, drift-window accept/reject, malformed-input rejection, secret uniqueness) + 11 `MfaTest` (unaffected-without-MFA regression check, setup page, enable/disable with valid/invalid credentials, full login→challenge→verify happy path, challenge-without-pending-session redirect, secret hidden from serialization). New migration `2026_07_11_010001_add_mfa_columns_to_users_table` also validated the just-fixed schema-dump workflow end-to-end: regenerated `database/schema/pgsql-schema.sql` to include these columns, re-verified the id-default parity check (still 0 missing across 468 tables), and ran the full suite from an empty `detector_test` to confirm a schema dump plus one migration layered on top works exactly as Laravel intends. **Not done**: real SSO/SAML/OIDC federation, and *mandatory* MFA enforcement on specific roles/routes (`soc:response.*`/`soc:admin.*`) — the capability exists but nothing currently requires any user to enable it; that's a policy decision for a future pass, not a technical blocker. | `app/Services/TotpService.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Auth/MfaController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Auth/AuthenticatedSessionController.php`, `app/Models/User.php`, `routes/auth.php`, `resources/views/auth/mfa-setup.blade.php`, `resources/views/auth/mfa-challenge.blade.php`, `database/migrations/2026_07_11_010001_add_mfa_columns_to_users_table.php`, `tests/Unit/TotpServiceTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Auth/MfaTest.php` | — | 2026-07-11 |

New `SigmaImportService::parse()` (Symfony YAML — already a transitive Laravel dependency, no new composer package added) + `compile()`: domain inferred from Sigma `logsource.product/category/service` via a lookup table (`product: okta`→`identity`, `product: aws`→`cloud`, `category: process_creation`→`endpoint`, etc., falling back to `endpoint`), `severity` from `level` (Sigma's `informational` renamed to the platform's `info`), `mitre_attack` from `tags: [attack.txxxx]` (prefix-stripped, uppercased, deduplicated), `shadow_only`/`output_topic` auto-set correctly for the protected domains (`endpoint`/`threat-intel`/`network` get `shadow_only:true` + `xdr.alerts.shadow.<domain>`), `confidence` fixed at the validator's 0.65 floor (the minimum for any non-draft/non-deprecated status), `rule_id` slugified from `title` with collision-dedup against existing IDs. **Always emits `status: "shadow"`, never `staged_active`** — verified with a dedicated test (`test_compile_never_produces_staged_active`) that a source Sigma rule claiming `status: stable` still compiles to `shadow`, since `staged_active` requires a real domain-specific 6h soak PASS that an import can never satisfy on its own. New `detection:import-sigma {files*} {--dry-run} {--registry=}` artisan command; the `--registry=` override exists purely so tests never touch the real registry file. **Scope correction discovered during research** (the single most important finding of this task): `registry.v1.json` is a metadata/governance catalog everywhere in this codebase — all 133 existing rules have their actual match logic hand-coded in Go (`internal/shadowrules`) or Python (`scripts/xdr_correlation_detector.py`), never driven by the JSON, which has no field-condition/expression language at all (confirmed: zero programmatic writer existed for this file before this task — it was purely hand-edited). So this compiler produces a catalog entry (MITRE mapping, severity, suppression scaffolding, platform field-mapping hints) with the original Sigma `detection:` block preserved verbatim in a new `sigma_source` field for a human to hand-implement the real shadow-rule logic from later — consistent with how every other rule in the registry already works, not a shortfall unique to this importer, but meaning "so community/vendor rulesets can be ingested" (the original finding's phrasing) overstates the result: this bootstraps the catalog/governance side of onboarding a ruleset rapidly, not live detection coverage. | **CAP-DECEPTION-HONEYTOKEN** | Implemented seeded-honeytoken detection: `Honeytoken`/`HoneytokenHit` models over new `honeytokens` (mutable catalog) + `honeytoken_hits` (append-only, unique-indexed `(honeytoken_id, alert_id)` from creation — deliberately not retrofitted later the way `ioc_hits` was) tables. Research found `SocThreatIntelController::matchIocs()` was the closest existing analog (substring-scan alert evidence for seeded values, batched `insertOrIgnore` into a hit table, accumulate-then-write-once evidence update) and the task itself named a Laravel `HoneytokenService`, so `HoneytokenService::scanForHits()` mirrors that method's shape deliberately rather than inventing a second scanning strategy or routing through correlation-worker/Go. **Bug found and fixed during testing** (a real defect, not a hypothetical): the mirrored scan builds its match haystack via `json_encode([..., $alert->evidence, $alert->raw_event])`, and PHP's `json_encode()` escapes `/` to `\/` by default — a plain-text honeytoken needle containing `/` (exactly what `file_path` and `url` token types — 2 of the 4 supported types — routinely look like, e.g. `C:/decoy/passwords.txt`) would silently never match. Fixed by decoding the stored evidence/raw_event JSON strings back into arrays before a single re-encode pass, with `JSON_UNESCAPED_SLASHES` on that pass; caught by a dedicated multi-token test that deliberately included a file path — the original `matchIocs()` has the same latent risk but no test coverage exposes it since none of its existing IOC test fixtures contain slashes; that pre-existing gap was noted but left alone as out of scope for this task. New `honeytoken:scan` artisan command, scheduled `everyFifteenMinutes()` in `Kernel.php` (matching `soc:sla-escalate`'s cadence) — no existing scheduled job did value-matching before this. New `honeytoken.view`/`honeytoken.manage` permissions (admin + detection_engineer get manage; analyst/viewer/scenario_operator get view or nothing, matching the `advisory.*`/`dlq.*` precedent). New `SocHoneytokenController` (seed/list/deactivate) + minimal Blade view, RBAC-gated identically to existing SOC controllers. | **PERF-GO-LIMITER** | Replaced ingestion-gateway's per-tenant token bucket (IG-2) — a buffered channel refilled by a background goroutine ticking every second and touching EVERY live tenant bucket regardless of demand — with a lock-free atomic time-delta limiter: `tokens`/`lastRefill` are `atomic.Int64` fields, refilled lazily via a CAS loop computed from elapsed wall time exactly when a request needs it (`tenantBucket.allow()`). The background goroutine (`startTenantBucketRefiller`) now only does idle-bucket eviction, once a minute (down from once a second) — it no longer touches token state of any bucket at all. Behavior preserved exactly: all existing black-box tests (burst-then-throttle, per-tenant isolation, bucket-cap/overflow-sharing from `TestTenantRateLimitIsolation`/`TestTenantBucketCapBoundsDistinctBuckets`/`TestOverflowTenantsShareRateLimit` etc.) pass unmodified. +3 new tests exercising the refill mechanism directly: refill happens correctly from elapsed time with no background goroutine running (`TestTenantBucketRefillsOverElapsedTimeWithoutBackgroundGoroutine`), refill never exceeds `rps` capacity even after a long idle period (`TestTenantBucketRefillNeverExceedsCapacity`), and 200 concurrent goroutines hitting the same bucket produce a race-free, capacity-bounded result (`TestTenantBucketConcurrentRefillIsRaceFree`). `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean. **Not run**: `go test -race` — requires cgo, and gcc is not installed in this environment, the same standing limitation noted for every other Go change this session; the concurrent-access test above exercises the CAS retry path without the race detector as a partial substitute. | `services/ingestion-gateway/main.go`, `services/ingestion-gateway/main_test.go` | [#27](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/27) | 2026-07-10 |
| **PERF-GO-OVERCONCURRENT** | `normalizeBatch()` spawned a fresh set of `workers` goroutines plus 2 new channels (jobs, results) on EVERY poll-batch cycle, then discarded them once the batch completed — real per-batch goroutine-spawn/teardown GC churn at high sustained RPS. Introduced `normalizerPool`, started once lazily via `sync.Once` (so any `Worker` construction path — `main()`, tests, one-off file processing — picks it up without an explicit init step) and reused across every subsequent `normalizeBatch` call. `Worker` gained a `pool *normalizerPool` field; the free `normalizeBatch()` function became `(w *Worker) normalizeBatch()`, delegating to the persistent pool. Only the small per-batch results channel is still allocated per call (unavoidable — each batch needs its own completion signal); the actual GC-churn source (goroutine spawn+teardown every batch) is eliminated. Both call sites (the poll loop, `normalizeHTTP`) updated; no test called the old free function directly, so no test signature changes were needed. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean. +4 new tests: pool-instance-reused-across-calls (`TestNormalizeBatchReusesSamePoolAcrossCalls`), correctness preserved through the persistent pool (malformed/normalized split), 20 concurrent calls hitting the shared pool without corruption, and empty-input handling. **Not run**: `go test -race` — requires cgo, unavailable in this environment (same standing limitation as `PERF-GO-LIMITER`, #27); the concurrent-calls test exercises the shared pool under concurrent access without the race detector as a partial substitute. | `services/normalizer-worker/main.go`, `services/normalizer-worker/main_test.go` | [#28](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/28) | 2026-07-10 |
| **ENT-SEC-NO-TLS-INTERNAL** (phase 1 — ingestion-gateway cert tooling + mTLS scaffolding, off by default; other 9 services/Pandaproxy/OpenSearch/Postgres remain) | Built the internal mTLS mechanism end-to-end on one representative service rather than attempting all 10 services + 3 datastores in one pass. New `scripts/xdr_generate_internal_mtls_certs.py`: shells out to the system `openssl` CLI (no new Python crypto dependency) to generate a self-signed local dev/test CA plus one server cert (SAN covering every internal docker-compose hostname) and one client cert, both CA-signed, written to `storage/certs/internal-mtls/` (gitignored — private keys never committed). **Caught and fixed a real bug during smoke testing, not left to a live deploy to discover**: the first CA generation (plain `openssl req -x509 ...`, no explicit extensions) verified fine under `openssl verify` and Go's own `crypto/tls` client, but was rejected outright by Python's `ssl` module on OpenSSL 3.x ("CA cert does not include key usage extension") — a stricter TLS client than either of the first two tools exercised. Root cause: the default `openssl req -x509` policy on this environment doesn't set a critical `keyUsage=keyCertSign,cRLSign` extension. Fixed by adding explicit `-addext basicConstraints=critical,CA:TRUE -addext keyUsage=critical,keyCertSign,cRLSign`, with a new regression test (`test_ca_cert_has_critical_key_usage_and_basic_constraints`) asserting the extension content directly, not just that some other tool's `verify` command happens to accept it. New Go package `services/ingestion-gateway/internal/mtls`: `ServerConfig()`/`ClientConfig()` build a `*tls.Config` requiring+verifying a client cert (server side) or presenting a client cert + verifying the server (client side) against the given CA — both return `(nil, nil)` when disabled, meaning "use plain HTTP," so every existing deployment is byte-for-byte unaffected by default. Wired into `ingestion-gateway/main.go`: the server's `http.Server.TLSConfig` and the shared package-level `httpClient`'s `Transport.TLSClientConfig`, gated behind `XDR_INTERNAL_MTLS_ENABLED` (default `false`) — `server.ListenAndServeTLS("", "")` is used when enabled (cert/key already loaded into `TLSConfig.Certificates`), `server.ListenAndServe()` otherwise, identical to the pre-existing behavior. **Verified two ways, not just unit tests**: (1) +11 Go tests in `internal/mtls` including a genuine end-to-end handshake over a real `tls.Listen` — proves both that a valid client cert succeeds and that an untrusted (non-CA-signed) client cert is rejected, not just that the `tls.Config` object has the right fields set; (2) a manual smoke test — built the real binary, ran it with `XDR_INTERNAL_MTLS_ENABLED=true` and real generated certs, confirmed via Python's `ssl`/`http.client` (a third, independent TLS implementation from both Go's and openssl's) that a request with no client cert is connection-reset and a request with the valid client cert returns `200 {"status":"ok",...}` from the real `/health` endpoint. `docker compose config --quiet` confirmed clean after the `.env.example` documentation addition. **Explicitly a phase 1, not "any internal hop"**: the other 9 services (normalizer-worker, correlation-worker, alert-writer-service, incident-builder-service, ai-rag-service, and the 4 `log-connector-*` services), Pandaproxy/OpenSearch's own plaintext HTTP, Postgres `sslmode`, and the docker-compose network config needed to actually run this between containers are all deferred, separate future phases — this pass proves the mechanism genuinely works on one service, it doesn't close the finding. | `scripts/xdr_generate_internal_mtls_certs.py`, `services/ingestion-gateway/internal/mtls/mtls.go`, `services/ingestion-gateway/internal/mtls/mtls_test.go`, `services/ingestion-gateway/main.go`, `.env.example`, `.gitignore` | [#31](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/31) | 2026-07-10 |

| **CAP-MSSP-TENANCY** | Implemented MSSP parent/child tenant hierarchy + advisory rollups. Research found the finding's premise understated the prerequisite work: `tenant_id` was a bare string convention scattered across ~19 tables with **no `tenants` table at all** — no first-class tenant registry existed, so a `tenant_hierarchy` FK would have had nothing authoritative to reference. Built the registry first: new `tenants` table (id/tenant_id/name/status), then `tenant_hierarchy` (`parent_tenant_id`→`child_tenant_id`, both FK to `tenants.tenant_id`, `is_active` for reversible unlink mirroring `user_tenant_memberships`' granted/revoked pattern — never hard-deleted, so history is preserved). **Deliberately did not reuse `TenantBoundaryService`'s null-tenant-context convention** for rollup access control: research found that convention is *already* an accidental cross-tenant leak (`assertAccess()`/`scopeQuery()` both treat a null tenant_id as "unscoped, allow everything," and the only three controllers wired to it default to null whenever a caller simply omits the `X-Tenant-ID` header, since `XDR_TENANT_STRICT_MODE` defaults false) — building a new rollup feature on top of that pattern would have compounded an existing gap rather than adding a safe new capability. Instead, `TenantHierarchyService::rollupSummary()` is driven exclusively by an explicit, enumerated whitelist from `tenant_hierarchy` (`childrenOf()` → `whereIn('tenant_id', $children)` against `security_alerts`/`security_incidents`) — an unlinked or unrelated tenant is never included, verified by dedicated tests seeding an alert for a tenant that was never linked to the parent and asserting it's absent from the rollup. New `mssp_analyst` role (added to `Rbac::$validRoles` and `config/soc.php`, view-only: `dashboard.view` + `mssp.rollup.view`) and new `mssp.rollup.view`/`mssp.hierarchy.manage` permissions (admin gets both; MSSP-analyst rollup access additionally requires an active `user_tenant_memberships` row for the requested parent tenant — reusing the existing membership mechanism rather than inventing a third parallel authorization concept, so an MSSP analyst can't view an arbitrary parent's rollup just by holding the generic permission). New `MsspTenantController` (rollup view, link/unlink — manage-gated) + minimal Blade view. Advisory/read-only throughout — no autonomous cross-tenant action. +15 tests (`TenantHierarchyTest`): link/unlink lifecycle, self-parent rejection, rollup counts, unrelated-tenant exclusion, unlinked-child exclusion, empty-parent edge case, RBAC role recognition, membership-gated HTTP access (analyst without membership sees empty rollup even for a parent with real linked data; analyst with membership sees it; admin bypasses the membership check), manage-permission-gated link/unlink routes. | `database/migrations/2026_07_13_010001_create_tenant_hierarchy_tables.php`, `app/Models/Tenant.php`, `app/Models/TenantHierarchy.php`, `app/Services/TenantHierarchyService.php`, `app/Support/Rbac.php`, `config/soc.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/MsspTenantController.php`, `routes/web.php`, `resources/views/mssp/rollup.blade.php`, `tests/Feature/TenantHierarchyTest.php` | — | 2026-07-13 |
