| **ENT-SEC-NO-TLS-INTERNAL** (phases 4-7 — all 4 log-connectors + 3 Python services + Postgres sslmode config; Pandaproxy/OpenSearch TLS + docker-compose network wiring remain, genuinely blocked by no live Docker daemon) | Phase 4: duplicated `internal/mtls` into all 4 `log-connector-*` Go services (syslog/cloudtrail/guardduty/gcp-audit) — each already ran its own `/health`+`/metrics` server *and* an outbound `httpClient` forwarding to `ingestion-gateway`, both wired, disabled by default. Had to add a missing `envBool()` helper to all 4 (didn't exist there before). Verified via `go build`/`go vet`/`go test` (all clean across all 4) plus a real end-to-end smoke test on `log-connector-syslog` (binary built, real certs, no-cert rejected / valid-cert 200 OK). Phases 5-6: `alert-writer-service`/`incident-builder-service`/`ai-rag-service` are started via a **static Dockerfile `CMD`** (`python -m uvicorn main:app ...`), not a programmatic `uvicorn.run()` call — no Go-style code hook point exists. New `docker-entrypoint.sh` (POSIX `sh`, disabled by default, conditionally adds uvicorn's `--ssl-certfile`/`--ssl-keyfile`/`--ssl-ca-certs`/`--ssl-cert-reqs 2` flags) duplicated into all 3. These 3 services never call each other directly (only Pandaproxy/OpenSearch, the separate infra-TLS item) — so only server-side mTLS applies, no client wiring needed. **Found and fixed a real correctness gap during this work, not left for a live deploy**: enabling mTLS would have broken the existing Docker `HEALTHCHECK` (a plain `http.request` presenting no client cert against a listener that now requires one) — new `docker-healthcheck.py` mirrors the same env-var toggle, building an SSL context with the client cert when mTLS is enabled; `build_request()` is a pure function, unit tested directly (13 tests total in `scripts/xdr_validate_uvicorn_mtls_entrypoint.py`, including one against the real generated certs). **Found and fixed a second real bug during testing**: this repo's git checkout has `core.fileMode=false`, so a committed script's executable bit is **not preserved** — `CMD ["./docker-entrypoint.sh", ...]` (direct exec) would fail with "permission denied" on a real Linux build; fixed to `CMD ["sh", "docker-entrypoint.sh", ...]` (sh interprets the file directly, no exec bit needed), locked in with a dedicated regression test (`test_dockerfile_invokes_entrypoint_via_sh_not_direct_exec`) so this can't silently regress. **Found and fixed a pre-existing, unrelated gap that was blocking this feature**: `ai-rag-service`'s `docker-compose.yml` block had **zero** `env_file`/`environment` wiring at all (unlike its 2 sibling Python services) — `.env` vars, including the new mTLS ones, would never have reached that container; added `env_file: - ./.env`, matching the other services. Phase 7 (partial): Postgres `sslmode` was hardcoded to `'prefer'` — made configurable via `DB_SSLMODE` (default unchanged, byte-for-byte identical behavior). Full Postgres mutual TLS (client cert via `sslcert`/`sslkey`/`sslrootcert`) deliberately **not** wired — verifying that DSN path needs a live Postgres server with real certs configured, which this environment cannot provision or verify, and guessing at the DSN internals without verification was judged too risky for a file this central (every DB connection in the app goes through it). **Verified**: full `php artisan test --parallel --recreate-databases` re-run after the `config/database.php` change (the highest-risk file touched this round, since every test in the suite depends on it) — 4946 tests, 13963 assertions, 0 failures; all 4 Go connectors' test suites clean; all 3 Python services' existing per-directory suites clean (82+77+1 passed, unmodified — zero regression since only new sibling files were added, `main.py` itself untouched); `docker compose config --quiet` clean. **All 10 custom services now have the app-layer mTLS mechanism, disabled by default.** **Explicitly still open — genuinely infra-blocked, not just undone**: Pandaproxy (Redpanda REST proxy) TLS, OpenSearch TLS, Postgres full mTLS, and the docker-compose network wiring to actually run mTLS between live containers all need a running Docker daemon to configure and verify (`docker info` fails in this environment: "failed to connect to the docker API") — this is a genuine tooling/environment limitation, not something more code can resolve from here. | `services/{log-connector-syslog,log-connector-cloudtrail,log-connector-guardduty,log-connector-gcp-audit}/internal/mtls/`, `services/{log-connector-syslog,log-connector-cloudtrail,log-connector-guardduty,log-connector-gcp-audit}/main.go`, `services/{alert-writer-service,incident-builder-service,ai-rag-service}/docker-entrypoint.sh`, `services/{alert-writer-service,incident-builder-service,ai-rag-service}/docker-healthcheck.py`, `services/{alert-writer-service,incident-builder-service,ai-rag-service}/Dockerfile`, `scripts/xdr_validate_uvicorn_mtls_entrypoint.py`, `config/database.php`, `docker-compose.yml`, `.env.example` | [#34](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/34) | 2026-07-11 |
| **SYSLOG-TCP-ADMISSION** | External review finding on `log-connector-syslog`'s TCP listener: one goroutine per accepted connection, no connection ceiling, no read/idle deadline (a slow/silent client held its goroutine+fd forever), a temporary `Accept()` error killed the entire listener goroutine outright, and no clean way to stop the listener on shutdown. New `internal/tcpadmit` package: `Limiter` (bounded connection admission — `TryAcquire`/`Release`/`Active`/`Max`, `0` disables the cap, matching the existing `maxTenantBuckets` convention) and `NextBackoff` (exponential backoff computation mirroring net/http's own `Server.Serve` accept-retry idiom) — both pure, fully unit tested (9 tests) without a real socket. Wired into `serveTCP`/`handleTCPConn`: `XDR_SYSLOG_TCP_MAX_CONNS` (default 1000, connections beyond this refused immediately, `tcp_rejected_conns` metric), `XDR_SYSLOG_TCP_IDLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (default 300, `SetReadDeadline` refreshed before each line read, `tcp_timeouts` metric), a temporary `Accept()` error now retries with exponential backoff instead of killing the listener, and `ln.Close()`/`conn.Close()` wired into `main()`'s existing SIGTERM handler for clean shutdown — already-active connections are deliberately left open to drain naturally rather than force-closed (force-closing would drop in-flight buffered lines), verified by a test that writes through an active connection *after* shutdown and confirms it's still ingested. New `tcp_active_conns`/`tcp_rejected_conns`/`tcp_timeouts` counters on `/metrics`. README documents the two new env vars plus production source-IP allowlisting and TLS-termination expectations (per the finding's required outcome). +4 real integration tests dialing actual TCP connections against a real `serveTCP` listener (connection-limit saturation, idle-timeout closure, active-conn-count lifecycle, clean-shutdown-with-active-clients) — the finding is specifically about real socket/fd/goroutine behavior, which only a real listener proves. Also fixed a cosmetic bug caught during review: the startup log line re-read `envInt("XDR_SYSLOG_TCP_MAX_CONNS", ...)` instead of reflecting the actual configured `Limiter`'s capacity (would have shown the wrong number for any caller constructing a `Connector` directly, e.g. tests) — added `Limiter.Max()` and used it instead. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across all packages in the service (34 tests total). | `services/log-connector-syslog/internal/tcpadmit/tcpadmit.go`, `services/log-connector-syslog/main.go`, `services/log-connector-syslog/README.md` | [#35](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/35) | 2026-07-11 |
| **CONN-UNTENANTED-INGEST** | External review finding: connector tenant environment variables default to empty and are absent from Compose — events omit `tenant_id`, send no `X-Tenant-ID`, and are accepted because `ingestion-gateway.tenantAllowed("")` returns `true`. `ingestion-gateway`: new `XDR_INGEST_REQUIRE_TENANT` (default `false`). When `true`, a batch with no payload `tenant_id` AND no `X-Tenant-ID` header is rejected outright (`400 tenant_required`) rather than silently accepted with unrestricted rate limiting — `tenantAllowed("")`'s existing "unrestricted" behavior for rate-limiting is a separate, deliberately unchanged concern; this adds an independent admission check ahead of it. All 4 `log-connector-*` services: new `validateTenantConfig(tenantID, requireTenant)` pure function (directly unit tested, no need to exercise real `log.Fatal`), wired into `main()` via a new `XDR_<SERVICE>_REQUIRE_TENANT` env var — in strict mode with no configured tenant, the connector refuses to start (`log.Fatalf`) rather than silently forwarding unattributed telemetry. `docker-compose.yml`: added explicit `XDR_<SERVICE>_TENANT_ID`/`XDR_<SERVICE>_REQUIRE_TENANT` passthroughs to all 4 connector blocks plus `XDR_INGEST_REQUIRE_TENANT` to `ingestion-gateway`'s block — these were completely absent from Compose before, meaning even an operator setting them in `.env` would never have reached the containers (no `env_file`, no explicit `environment` entry); all default to the pre-existing behavior, zero change for existing deployments. **Verified event-level tenant identity was already trustworthy once configured, not the actual gap**: all 4 connectors already stamp `tenant_id` into the JSON payload (not an untrusted header — none of them ever sent `X-Tenant-ID` at all), and the whole payload is HMAC-signed before forwarding, so "signed tenant attribution" was structurally already correct; the actual gap was purely the missing enforcement/defaults/compose-wiring this fixes. READMEs updated for all 4 connectors documenting the new var. +20 new tests: 4 on `ingestion-gateway` (default-mode allows untenanted, strict-mode rejects untenanted, strict-mode accepts payload tenant, strict-mode accepts header-only tenant) + 4 per connector × 4 connectors (default-allows-empty, strict-rejects-empty, strict-allows-configured, two-instances-stay-isolated) — covering every "Required tests" scenario from the finding except header/payload-mismatch, already covered by a pre-existing test. Tenant identity is derived exclusively from the connector's own trusted deployment configuration or ingestion-gateway's payload-tenant extraction — never from untrusted Syslog payload fields or cloud-export record contents, per the finding's constraint. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across `ingestion-gateway` + all 4 connectors. `docker compose config --quiet`: clean. | `services/ingestion-gateway/main.go`, `services/{log-connector-syslog,log-connector-cloudtrail,log-connector-guardduty,log-connector-gcp-audit}/main.go`, `docker-compose.yml`, `services/{log-connector-syslog,log-connector-cloudtrail,log-connector-guardduty,log-connector-gcp-audit}/README.md` | [#36](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/36) | 2026-07-11 |
| **CONN-DELIVERY-LOSS** | External review finding (Critical): every connector cleared its in-memory buffer before `forward()` was confirmed to succeed. The 3 file-based connectors also persisted `processedFiles[path] = true` (and saved the state file) before any batch derived from that file was acknowledged — so a transient gateway outage (network error, timeout, HTTP 429/5xx) permanently discarded telemetry, and on restart the source file was skipped forever even though it was never actually delivered. New shared `internal/deliver.WithRetry(maxAttempts, base, max, fn)` bounded-exponential-backoff primitive, duplicated per-service (no shared Go module in this repo, matching the `internal/mtls`/`internal/tcpadmit` precedent), fully unit tested (6 tests per copy). **`log-connector-syslog`** (ephemeral UDP/TCP, no source file to re-read): `flush()` redesigned to retry the forward through `deliver.WithRetry` before clearing the buffer; if every attempt fails, the batch is explicitly dropped and counted (`delivery_dropped` metric) rather than silently discarded. Deliberately **not** built: a disk-backed pending-batch spool, which would be needed for true restart-safe (crash-safe) delivery of ephemeral telemetry — scoped and documented honestly in the README as retry-then-explicit-drop only, closing the "transient failure loses data" gap without overclaiming crash-safety. **`log-connector-cloudtrail`/`-guardduty`/`-gcp-audit`** (file-based): redesigned from a shared cross-file buffer to per-file independent batching inside `processFile` — each file's derived batches are forwarded with bounded retry synchronously, and `processedFiles[path] = true`/`saveState()` are committed only after **every** batch from that file is confirmed delivered; a file that fails any batch is left entirely unprocessed and naturally retried on the next scan cycle via the pre-existing state-file mechanism (fixing checkpoint-write *timing*, not the mechanism itself). New `delivery_failed_files` metric on all 3. Shutdown-waits-for-acknowledged-flush required no new code — `flush()`/`processFile()` are now synchronous through the full retry sequence, so the pre-existing SIGTERM handler already blocks on it; proven with a dedicated shutdown test. Env vars added per service: `XDR_<SERVICE>_FORWARD_MAX_RETRIES` (default 3), `XDR_<SERVICE>_FORWARD_RETRY_BASE_MS` (default 200), `XDR_<SERVICE>_FORWARD_RETRY_MAX_MS` (default 2000) — documented in all 4 READMEs. +~20 new tests, all real integration tests (actual TCP dials, real `httptest.Server`s, `http.Hijacker` to simulate a genuine connection reset rather than just a non-2xx status) per the finding's own emphasis on real socket/delivery behavior: gateway unavailable then recovers, non-2xx then success, process restart retries an unacknowledged file/batch, multi-batch file where a middle batch fails (first batch's success is not falsely persisted), shutdown during flush waits for retries. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across all 4 connectors + `ingestion-gateway`. `docker compose config --quiet`: clean. **`CONN-UNBOUNDED-FILE`** (streaming/size-bounding the same 3 file parsers) is a related but distinct finding, deliberately left in `REVIEW_BACKLOG.md`, not addressed here. | `services/{log-connector-syslog,log-connector-cloudtrail,log-connector-guardduty,log-connector-gcp-audit}/internal/deliver/`, `services/{log-connector-syslog,log-connector-cloudtrail,log-connector-guardduty,log-connector-gcp-audit}/main.go`, `services/{log-connector-syslog,log-connector-cloudtrail,log-connector-guardduty,log-connector-gcp-audit}/main_test.go`, `services/{log-connector-syslog,log-connector-cloudtrail,log-connector-guardduty,log-connector-gcp-audit}/README.md` | [#37](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/37) | 2026-07-11 |
| **IDENTITY-SSO-MFA** (mandatory MFA enforcement on approval routes — remaining sub-scope after TOTP) | Finding: "enforcing MFA as mandatory for specific roles/routes (`soc:response.*`/`soc:admin.*`) is a policy decision, not yet wired." New `App\Http\Middleware\EnsureMfaVerified` (alias `mfa.required`): when `soc.mfa_enforcement_enabled` (`SOC_MFA_ENFORCEMENT_ENABLED`, default `false`) is on, blocks the request with 403 unless the acting user has `mfa_enabled=true`, and audit-logs the block (`AuditLogger`, action `mfa.enforcement_blocked`) — same pattern as `EnsureSocPermission`'s RBAC denial logging. Applied to the highest-consequence write actions matching the finding's language: response-plan approve/reject (web + API), active-response approve/reject, and data-erasure approve/reject — the erasure route group was split so the routine policy-update route (`data-residency.policy.update`) stays unaffected, since only approve/reject carry the deletion-consequence risk the finding is about. Off by default — flipping `SOC_MFA_ENFORCEMENT_ENABLED` on is an explicit operator decision, since enabling it before every account with approval permission has MFA configured would lock them out of approving. Deliberately did not touch `soc:rules.govern` (detection promotion) — that group mixes read-only dashboards with a handful of POST actions and cleanly splitting it out is separate scope not explicitly named in the finding. Real SSO/SAML/OIDC federation to a corporate IdP remains deferred (needs a real external IdP unavailable in this environment) — unaffected by this task. +8 new tests (`MfaEnforcementTest`): disabled-by-default allows action, enabled blocks non-MFA user, enabled allows MFA-enabled user, blocked attempt is audit-logged, plus route-registration assertions confirming the middleware is present on all 3 target route groups and absent from the sibling policy-update route. `php artisan test --filter=MfaEnforcementTest`: 8 passed. `php artisan test --filter=MfaTest`: 11 passed (no regression). `php artisan test --filter="ResponsePlanningTest|ActiveResponse|DataResidency"`: 159 passed (no regression — enforcement off by default). `php artisan test --filter=RbacAuditCoverageTest`: 26 passed (no regression). | `app/Http/Middleware/EnsureMfaVerified.php`, `app/Http/Kernel.php`, `config/soc.php`, `routes/web.php`, `.env.example`, `tests/Feature/Auth/MfaEnforcementTest.php` | [#38](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/38) | 2026-07-11 |
| **CONN-UNBOUNDED-FILE** | External review finding (High): `log-connector-{cloudtrail,guardduty,gcp-audit}` called `os.ReadFile()`/gzip `io.ReadAll()` with no compressed or decompressed size ceiling — one oversized export file, or a small highly-compressible file that expands to gigabytes once decompressed (a compression bomb), could exhaust memory and restart-loop the connector. New `internal/boundedfile.Read(path, maxBytes)` per connector (duplicated, matching this repo's package-duplication convention): reads via `io.LimitReader(f, maxBytes+1)` so it never buffers more than `maxBytes+1` regardless of the file's actual on-disk size, `ErrTooLarge` if exceeded, `maxBytes<=0` disables the bound. Each parser package (`internal/cloudtrail`, `internal/guardduty`, `internal/gcpaudit`) gained `ParseBounded(data, Limits{MaxExpandedBytes, MaxRecordBytes})` alongside the existing `Parse` (now a thin wrapper calling `ParseBounded` with zero limits — fully backward compatible, all pre-existing tests unchanged): gzip decompression goes through `io.LimitReader` capped at `MaxExpandedBytes` (`ErrExpandedTooLarge` if exceeded — defeats compression bombs without ever fully decompressing them), and any single record/NDJSON-line over `MaxRecordBytes` is skipped and counted rather than silently dropped — for the NDJSON formats (guardduty/gcp-audit) this check happens on the raw line **before** any JSON unmarshal is attempted, avoiding wasted work on the oversized line specifically. `main.go` wiring: a file exceeding `MAX_FILE_BYTES`/`MAX_EXPANDED_BYTES` is **quarantined** — left in place untouched (never deleted/moved), recorded in a new append-only `<watch-dir>/.<service>-connector-quarantine.jsonl` (`path`/`reason`/`quarantined_at` per line, satisfying the finding's "durable rejection record suitable for operator recovery" constraint), and never re-attempted on a later scan or after a restart (the quarantine log is reloaded at startup, mirroring the existing processed-files state-file mechanism — this is the finding's required "retry policy": no infinite re-read/re-reject hot loop on the same oversized file). A single oversized record within an otherwise-acceptable file is handled more leniently — skipped and counted in a new `oversized_records_skipped` metric, but the rest of the file's records are still parsed and forwarded normally, since one pathological event shouldn't sink an entire export. Malformed (non-JSON) files/records are a deliberately separate, unchanged concern — not a resource-exhaustion attack, so they keep the pre-existing retry-every-scan (file-level) / skip-poison-line (NDJSON-level) behavior rather than being quarantined, per the finding's own distinction between "oversized" and "malformed." Caught and fixed during implementation: guardduty/gcp-audit's own file-extension filters already accept `.jsonl`, which would have caused each connector's own new quarantine log to be picked up as a candidate export file on the next scan — added the same explicit exclusion already used for the state file. New env vars per connector: `XDR_<SERVICE>_MAX_FILE_BYTES` (default 100 MiB), `XDR_<SERVICE>_MAX_EXPANDED_BYTES` (default 500 MiB), `XDR_<SERVICE>_MAX_RECORD_BYTES` (default 1 MiB) — all `0` disables the corresponding bound, documented in all 3 READMEs, wired into `docker-compose.yml`. Tests: 6 per `internal/boundedfile` copy (under/at/over limit, zero-disables-bound, missing file, never-over-allocates-regardless-of-real-file-size) + 5 per parser package's `ParseBounded` (zero-limits matches unbounded `Parse`, gzip-expansion rejected/allowed, oversized-record-skipped-others-kept, stable across a 5000-record fixture) + 7 per connector's `main_test.go` covering every "Required tests" scenario from the finding (oversized plain file quarantined with original left in place and durable JSONL record written, gzip expansion limit quarantined, oversized single record skipped with rest of file still forwarded, malformed file followed by a valid file doesn't block the valid one, stable behavior under a 3000-record fixture) plus 2 extra proving the "retry policy" constraint specifically (a quarantined file is never re-scanned in-process; quarantine state survives a restart across a new connector instance). `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across all 4 connectors + `ingestion-gateway`. `docker compose config --quiet`: clean. | `services/{log-connector-cloudtrail,log-connector-guardduty,log-connector-gcp-audit}/internal/boundedfile/`, `services/{log-connector-cloudtrail,log-connector-guardduty,log-connector-gcp-audit}/internal/{cloudtrail,guardduty,gcpaudit}/*.go`, `services/{log-connector-cloudtrail,log-connector-guardduty,log-connector-gcp-audit}/main.go`, `services/{log-connector-cloudtrail,log-connector-guardduty,log-connector-gcp-audit}/main_test.go`, `services/{log-connector-cloudtrail,log-connector-guardduty,log-connector-gcp-audit}/README.md`, `docker-compose.yml` | [#39](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/39) | 2026-07-11 |
| **OBS-OTEL-TRACING** (Phase 4 — OTLP/HTTP span export for the 3 core Go pipeline services) | Remaining sub-scope after phases 1-3 (W3C traceparent propagation across all 6 hops): "every hop produces standards-shaped span context, but nothing anywhere emits or collects an actual OTel span yet." New dependency-free `internal/otlpexport` Go package (duplicated per service, matching this repo's package-duplication convention — no OTel SDK pulled in, preserving "zero external Go deps"): builds the real OTLP/HTTP+JSON `ExportTraceServiceRequest` wire format (resourceSpans → scopeSpans → spans; trace_id/span_id/parent_span_id as lowercase hex per the OTLP spec's documented JSON-encoding special-case, not the default base64 protobuf-JSON mapping — verified this matters, since internal/traceparent already produces exactly that hex format with zero re-encoding needed) and POSTs it to a configurable collector endpoint. `Exporter.Endpoint == ""` (default) makes `Export()` a fully nil-safe no-op — zero behavior/latency change until `XDR_OTEL_EXPORTER_ENDPOINT` is set; export always runs in a goroutine so an unreachable/slow collector never adds latency to the actual telemetry path. **`ingestion-gateway`**: one span per event in `publish()` (not per batch — a batch can mix events from several distinct traces, so each event's span must carry its own correct trace-id), all sharing the true wall-clock window of that publish() call including retries. **`normalizer-worker`**: `normalizeResult` gained a `span` field built inside the pool goroutine (has both the raw inbound and freshly-normalized outbound traceparent in scope simultaneously — the actual correlation problem this needed to solve, since malformed events get filtered out non-positionally between raw and normalized slices); `Worker.normalizeBatch` now returns `(normalized, malformed, spans)`; wired at both call sites (consumer-poll loop, HTTP path) via a new `exportSpans` helper. **`correlation-worker`**: `Alert` gained an unexported `otelSpan` field (encoding/json skips unexported fields, so it never leaks into the published alert payload) populated inside the existing `makeAlert()` — avoided touching any of `makeAlert`'s ~17 call sites scattered across `correlate()`/`correlateIdentityCloud()`'s many rule branches; a new `exportAlertSpans` helper collects spans from a batch of alerts and exports them, wired at both call sites (consumer loop, `correlateHTTP`). `XDR_OTEL_EXPORTER_ENDPOINT` added to all 3 services' env, `.env.example`, `docker-compose.yml` (default empty = disabled). Tests: 9 per `internal/otlpexport` copy (JSON shape round-trips into the real OTLP structure, root-span omits parentSpanId, multi-span batching into one request, disabled/empty no-ops, real HTTP POST reaching a mock collector, non-2xx/unreachable-collector error handling) + 4 on ingestion-gateway's `publish()` (one span per event, span traceId/parentSpanId correctly derived from the inbound traceparent, no export when disabled, export failure doesn't block or slow down publish — verified under 500ms against an unreachable collector) + 4 on normalizer-worker (one span per successfully normalized event, span parent matches inbound traceparent, exportSpans reaches a real mock collector, no-op for empty spans) + 6 on correlation-worker (`makeAlert` span population matching its own traceparent, `exportAlertSpans` one-span-per-alert/disabled/empty-no-ops, and a full `correlateHTTP` integration test using a real 5-event MFA-failure-burst fixture proving produced alerts actually reach a mock collector, plus a negative test confirming zero alerts means zero export calls). No live OTel collector is available in this environment (`docker info` fails) — verified against local `httptest` mock servers instead, matching the mTLS-phase-1 precedent for "build the real mechanism, verify against a local double, defer live-infra verification." `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across all 3 Go pipeline services, zero regressions in their existing suites. `docker compose config --quiet`: clean. **Remaining scope**: OTLP export for the 2 Python services and PHP (same pattern, not yet done), and wiring to an actual live collector (Tempo/Jaeger) once this environment has a running Docker daemon. | `services/{ingestion-gateway,normalizer-worker,correlation-worker}/internal/otlpexport/`, `services/ingestion-gateway/main.go`, `services/ingestion-gateway/main_test.go`, `services/normalizer-worker/main.go`, `services/normalizer-worker/main_test.go`, `services/correlation-worker/main.go`, `services/correlation-worker/main_test.go`, `services/correlation-worker/otel_export_test.go`, `docker-compose.yml`, `.env.example` | [#40](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/40) | 2026-07-11 |
| **ENT-DETECT-ML-NOT-LIVE** (advisory-first ML shadow scorer output mode) | Deferred finding (see `REVIEW_REJECTED.md`'s ML-SERVE-ONLINE entry, now resolved): the trained multiclass LR model was loaded only by offline scripts — no live service scored it, so the "hybrid rule-based + ML" claim was rule-based only in practice. Investigation confirmed the original proposed fix (wire the model into correlation-worker) was the wrong integration point — the model's feature vector is HTTP-request features (status/latency_ms/has_sql_keywords/...) identical to `SecurityRequestLogger`'s `security_events` capture, not correlation-worker's identity/cloud/SaaS telemetry. The real rule+ML hybrid already existed as working code (`scripts/realtime_detector_consumer.py`) but was absent from `docker-compose.yml` and wrote directly to the active `security_alerts`/`security_responses` tables with no advisory/shadow gate. Implemented option (b) from the deferred finding's own production-gate analysis: new `--output-mode {shadow,active}` flag (env `DETECTOR_OUTPUT_MODE`), **default `shadow`**. Shadow mode writes to `advisory_findings` via a new `insert_advisory_findings()` upsert — new domain value `web_request` (distinct from the existing `endpoint`/`network` shadow domains, reflecting the investigation's own finding that this is a materially different telemetry domain) — matching alert-writer-service's `shadow_event_loop`/`write_advisory_finding` pattern exactly: same `shadow_fingerprint()` algorithm (sha256 of rule_id|actor|sorted-evidence-ids, ported verbatim for cross-producer dedup consistency), same `ON CONFLICT (fingerprint) DO UPDATE` upsert shape, and critically the same `finding_id = "adv-" + fingerprint[:36]` derivation — caught during design that deriving `finding_id` independently (e.g. from the existing windowed `alert_id`) would let two rows with the same fingerprint collide on `advisory_findings.finding_id`'s separate UNIQUE constraint on the *second* upsert attempt, since `ON CONFLICT (fingerprint)` only handles a conflict on that one named target. `active` mode preserves the original direct-to-`security_alerts`/`security_responses` behavior byte-for-byte, for operators who have already completed a domain-specific 6h soak PASS for `web_request` per CLAUDE.md. In shadow mode, response recommendations (`security_responses`) are skipped entirely — advisory findings are informational only, matching the "no auto-response from shadow" doctrine already enforced elsewhere in the platform. New Compose service `ml-shadow-detector` reuses the existing `infra/telemetry-worker/Dockerfile` (already `COPY`s this script, previously only invoked via `telemetry-worker`'s own command) with a command override, in its own opt-in `ml-shadow` profile — deliberately not part of the default `strangler`/`app` bring-up, so `docker compose --profile ml-shadow up -d ml-shadow-detector` is a separate, explicit operator decision, directly closing the "isn't in docker-compose.yml at all" half of the finding. Startup banner prints the active `OutputMode` plus a loud (non-blocking — this script cannot itself verify a soak's pass/fail) warning when `active` mode is selected. +22 tests in `tests/scripts/test_realtime_detector_consumer_shadow_mode.py`: `shadow_fingerprint` determinism/collision-avoidance/evidence-id-order-independence, `shadow_promotion_blocker` reason composition (always names the soak-gate reason; adds a low-confidence reason only below threshold), `--output-mode` default/env-override/explicit-flag-wins precedence, `insert_advisory_findings` SQL shape including an explicit assertion the SQL text never references `security_alerts`/`security_incidents`/`security_responses`, and the `SHADOW_DOMAIN` constant value. `python -m unittest discover -s tests/scripts -p "test_*.py"`: 22 passed (5 pre-existing + 17 new, zero regression). `python -m py_compile scripts/realtime_detector_consumer.py`: clean. `docker compose config --quiet`: clean. | `scripts/realtime_detector_consumer.py`, `tests/scripts/test_realtime_detector_consumer_shadow_mode.py`, `docker-compose.yml`, `docs/guides/README_phase6_realtime.md`, `REVIEW_REJECTED.md` | [#41](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/41) | 2026-07-11 |
| **CONNECTOR-FRAMEWORK** (Phase 7 — O365 Management Activity API connector, final phase) | Remaining sub-scope: "O365 Management Activity API — a materially different integration shape from the 3 cloud connectors above (pull-based subscription + continuation-token polling, not a file-export-to-object-storage pattern), needs real OAuth/API-key credentials to build and test against." New service `services/log-connector-o365`, same layout as the other 3 cloud connectors, plus a new `internal/o365` package: `auth.go`'s `TokenSource` implements the OAuth2 client-credentials grant against Azure AD, caching the access token and refreshing it 60s before actual expiry rather than on every request, with an implausibly-short `expires_in` treated as immediately stale (not a negative cache window). `client.go`'s `Client` implements `ListAvailableContent` (follows the API's `NextPageUri` pagination header until exhausted) and `FetchContent` (bounded read of one content blob via `io.LimitReader`, the same `internal/boundedfile.Read` technique applied to an HTTP response body instead of a file — `ErrContentTooLarge` if exceeded, never fully read into memory). `activity.go`'s pure parser handles the content blob's actual shape correctly: a **bare top-level JSON array** of audit records, materially different from CloudTrail's `{"Records":[...]}` envelope — confirmed by reading Microsoft's own Management Activity API documentation, not assumed. `main.go`'s `pollOnce()`/`processContent()` built all 3 prior connector-hardening findings in **from the start this time**, not retrofitted afterward: CONN-UNTENANTED-INGEST (`validateTenantConfig`/`XDR_O365_REQUIRE_TENANT`), CONN-DELIVERY-LOSS (bounded retry via `internal/deliver`, checkpoint only committed after full delivery, restart-safe `processedContent` state file keyed by content ID instead of file path), CONN-UNBOUNDED-FILE (content-blob and per-record size ceilings — content-blob oversized handling is deliberately lighter-weight than the file connectors' full durable quarantine-log: marked processed via the same state file + a metric + a log line, no separate audit-trail file, since a live API's content blob is far less attacker-controllable than an operator-facing file-drop directory — documented explicitly in the README's rationale, not silently reduced). **Field mapping uses `telemetry_type=o365_audit`, deliberately not `saas_audit`** — caught during design that `normalizer-worker`/`correlation-worker` already remap `saas_audit`→`saas`, the platform's currently-**active** correlation domain (`CLAUDE.md`: "Current active alert domains: identity, cloud, SaaS"); using `saas_audit` would have silently made this new connector's output start contributing to production `xdr.alerts` the moment it ran, with zero soak validation — a distinct, non-remapped type matches the exact same "connector adds no new active alert domain" precedent the other 3 connectors already follow via their own distinct type strings (`cloudtrail`/`guardduty`/`gcp_audit`). New Compose service `log-connector-o365` (profile `strangler`, matching the other connectors) with a named volume (`o365-connector-state`) for its restart-safe state directory, since — unlike the file-based connectors — there's no input file directory to bind-mount. This environment has no real Azure AD app registration or Microsoft tenant credentials — the OAuth2/polling/parsing logic is real and thoroughly unit-tested against local mock OAuth token + Activity API servers (genuine HTTP round trips via `httptest`, including real `NextPageUri` pagination and Bearer-token propagation), but has never been exercised against Microsoft's actual API — stated explicitly in the README's "What this connector does NOT do" section rather than left implicit. 67 tests total: 10 auth (token fetch/cache/refresh-before-expiry/no-premature-refresh/error handling/form-encoded credentials), 9 client (bearer-token propagation, pagination, list/fetch error handling, oversized-blob rejection), 9 parser (bare-array parsing, raw-field preservation, malformed/wrong-shape rejection, per-record size bounds), 6 `internal/boundedfile`, 6 `internal/deliver`, 9 `internal/mtls` (all duplicated verbatim from gcp-audit, proven packages), 18 in `main_test.go` covering field mapping, HMAC signing, tenant validation, and full end-to-end poll/fetch/deliver integration against a combined mock O365+gateway server (new content processed and marked, restart-safe skip on rescan, gateway-unavailable leaves content unprocessed for retry, restart retries an unacknowledged content, oversized content blob quarantined-lite with no forward call, oversized single record skipped without invalidating the rest of the blob, multi-content-type polling issues one list call per configured type). `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean. `docker compose config --quiet`: clean. **All 7 phases of CONNECTOR-FRAMEWORK are now complete; all 4 log-connector-* services also carry the full CONN-UNTENANTED-INGEST/CONN-DELIVERY-LOSS/CONN-UNBOUNDED-FILE hardening set.** | `services/log-connector-o365/` (go.mod, main.go, main_test.go, Dockerfile, README.md, internal/{o365,mtls,deliver,boundedfile}/), `docker-compose.yml` | [#42](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/42) | 2026-07-11 |
| **OBS-OTEL-TRACING** (Phase 5 — Python + PHP OTLP span export, final application-layer phase) | Remaining scope after phase 4's Go wiring: "OTLP export for the 2 Python services (alert-writer-service, incident-builder-service) and PHP (TraceparentService/SecurityRequestLogger) — same wiring pattern as the Go phase, not yet done." New `otlp_export.py` (duplicated into both Python services, matching the Go internal/otlpexport package-duplication convention): pure `build_request_body()` producing the identical OTLP/HTTP+JSON wire format, plus `export()`/`export_async()` — fire-and-forget via `threading.Thread`, matching the Go goroutine pattern so a slow/unreachable collector never adds latency to the alert-write/incident-build path. **alert-writer-service**: `process_alerts()` builds one span per alert (matching propagation granularity — a batch mixing alerts from distinct traces needs one span per trace), reusing the module's existing pooled `requests.Session`. **incident-builder-service**: `aggregate()` (in `incident_aggregation.py`) gained an optional `otel_spans_out` output-parameter list it appends spans into rather than embedding them in the returned incident dict — that dict is JSON-serialized three separate ways (DB write, Kafka payload, the `/v1/build` HTTP response), so a raw Span object must never end up inside it; `otel_spans_out=None` (the default, used by the unmodified `/v1/build` route) means zero spans collected and zero behavior/response-shape change on that real external API route, verified by an explicit regression test. `process_alerts()` passes a real list and exports it once the batch completes. **PHP**: new `App\Services\OtlpExportService` (pure `buildRequestBody()` returning a plain array Laravel's `Http` facade auto-JSON-encodes, plus `export()`). Wired into `SecurityRequestLogger` via Laravel's **terminable-middleware mechanism** (`terminate()`, invoked by `Illuminate\Foundation\Http\Kernel::terminate()` from `public/index.php` *after* the response has already been sent to the client) — recognized during design that placing the export call inside `handle()` (where the traceparent itself is computed) would still block the response, since the middleware chain hasn't actually flushed to the client at that point; `terminate()` is the closest equivalent PHP-FPM has to the Go/Python hops' background-thread/goroutine fire-and-forget, since PHP has no native background-thread primitive and this codebase's default `QUEUE_CONNECTION=sync` would run a queued job inline (not actually async) rather than solving the problem. Shared `XDR_OTEL_EXPORTER_ENDPOINT` env var (already used by the Go phase) now also read by Python (`os.getenv`) and PHP (new `config('xdr.otel_exporter_endpoint')` entry in `config/xdr.php`, following the ENV-CACHE-DRIFT-BATCH convention of reading via config so a custom value survives `config:cache`) — one operator-facing knob across the entire platform. Wired into `docker-compose.yml` for both Python services (the Laravel `app` service already has `env_file: ./.env`, so no separate wiring was needed there). Tests: 11 per `otlp_export.py` copy (JSON shape, root-span omits parentSpanId, batching, disabled/empty no-ops, real POST via a mocked Session, non-2xx/exception handling, background-thread export) + 3 new `process_alerts()` integration tests on alert-writer-service (one span per alert, no export when endpoint unset, span traceId/parentSpanId match the inbound traceparent chain) + 5 new `aggregate()` tests on incident-builder-service (span population, span never leaks into the JSON-serializable incident dict, trace/parent-span-id correctness, root-span empty parent) + 4 `process_alerts()` integration tests (one span per incident group, no export when disabled, trace-id correctness, and the `/v1/build` no-leakage regression test) + 6 pure `OtlpExportServiceBuildRequestBodyTest` PHP tests (wire shape, parentSpanId omission, kind defaulting, batching, JSON-encodability, empty-spans handling) + 5 `Http::fake()`-based `OtlpExportServiceTest` tests (no-op when blank/empty, real POST, never throws on unreachable collector or non-2xx) + 5 `SecurityRequestLoggerOtlpExportTest` tests proving `terminate()` actually fires in Laravel's own test HTTP client and exports a correctly-chained span, including a regression test confirming an unreachable collector never breaks the response. `python -m unittest discover -s tests/alert_writer`: 96 passed (zero regression from the pre-existing 93). `python -m unittest discover -s tests/incident_builder`: 97 passed (zero regression from the pre-existing 77). `php artisan test --parallel --recreate-databases`: **4970 passed, 14002 assertions, 0 failures** — the full suite, run once given this touches a *global* middleware (`SecurityRequestLogger` is registered unconditionally in `app/Http/Kernel.php`, not route-scoped). `docker compose config --quiet`: clean. **All 6 hops of the platform (3 Go, 2 Python, 1 PHP) now emit real OTLP spans, not just standards-shaped W3C traceparent propagation context.** Remaining scope: only a real live OTel collector (compose `observability` profile) to actually stitch/visualize the emitted spans (Tempo/Jaeger) — genuinely blocked by no live Docker daemon in this environment, not a code gap. | `services/{alert-writer-service,incident-builder-service}/otlp_export.py`, `services/alert-writer-service/main.py`, | **ENT-SDLC-NO-SUPPLYCHAIN** (dependency vulnerability scanning) | Filled in the achievable slice of the remaining "scan/sign" scope from the base-image/SBOM pass: real SOURCE dependency vulnerability scanning (distinct from the still-blocked container-image scan/sign, which needs a live Docker daemon). New `scripts/xdr_dependency_vuln_scan.py`, modeled on this repo's established `xdr_*_validate.py` conventions (argparse, `reports/<name>.json` output, status/summary-line/exit-code pattern). Discovers all 8 Go services (`go.mod`) under `services/` and runs `govulncheck -json ./...` against vuln.go.dev — real call-graph reachability analysis, not a naive version-list match, so it distinguishes "a vulnerable symbol exists in an imported module" from "this code actually calls it." Also discovers all 3 Python services (`requirements.txt`) and runs `python -m pip_audit -r requirements.txt --format json` against the PyPI Advisory Database — invoked via `python -m pip_audit` rather than the bare `pip-audit` console script, since `pip install pip-audit` lands its executable in a Scripts directory not on PATH in this environment. `govulncheck -json` output required a genuinely non-obvious parser: it's neither a JSON array nor NDJSON but a raw stream of concatenated JSON objects, decoded via `json.JSONDecoder().raw_decode()` in a loop; findings are deduplicated by OSV ID and distinguished from merely-present-but-unreached `"osv"` entries via the presence of a `"finding"` object. Both underlying tools were confirmed genuinely installable in this environment via real (non-dry-run) installs (`go install golang.org/x/vuln/cmd/govulncheck@latest`, `pip install pip-audit`) — correcting an earlier round's stale assumption (recorded in `xdr_generate_sbom.py`'s own docstring) that anything beyond syft-free SBOM generation was categorically blocked; that assumption held for trivy/cosign (both need a live Docker daemon: `docker info` fails) but not for these two, which only need network access to PyPI/vuln.go.dev. Findings surface as advisory `WARN` content in the JSON report (`reports/xdr_dependency_vuln_scan.json`), never a hard gate — matching the platform's no-autonomous-action posture (bumping a pinned dependency version is a deliberate human decision with its own regression risk); only genuine tool-invocation failures (missing binary, unparseable output, non-3 non-zero exit) are treated as `ERROR`/exit 1. Real result from running the script against the live repo: all 8 Go services report the identical 5 Go **stdlib** CVE IDs (`GO-2026-4970/5037/5038/5039/5856`) — toolchain-tied (go1.26.3), not third-party (this codebase has zero external Go dependencies per its own SBOM tooling), so no dependency version bump can fix them; all 3 Python services (`alert-writer-service`, `incident-builder-service`, `ai-rag-service`) came back clean. 20 new unit tests (`tests/scripts/test_xdr_dependency_vuln_scan.py`) covering the govulncheck stream parser (concatenated-objects, empty-input, malformed-input), Go/Python scan-status derivation (tool-missing→SKIPPED, findings→WARN, clean→PASS, unexpected-exit-code/timeout/unparseable-output→ERROR, govulncheck's by-design exit code 3 correctly NOT treated as an error), real service discovery against the actual `services/` directory, and overall-status aggregation in `main()` (ERROR-if-any-error, WARN-still-exits-0 since findings are advisory not blocking) — all mocking `subprocess.run`/`shutil.which`, no real external tool invocation needed to verify script logic. `python -m unittest tests.scripts.test_xdr_dependency_vuln_scan -v`: 20/20 passed. Also ran the script for real (not just the mocked test suite) against the live repo, confirming the report writes correctly and the summary line/exit code match the documented contract. **Still missing, genuinely blocked**: container IMAGE vulnerability scanning (trivy) and signed builds (cosign) — both need a running Docker daemon this environment does not have. | `scripts/xdr_dependency_vuln_scan.py`, `tests/scripts/test_xdr_dependency_vuln_scan.py`, `reports/xdr_dependency_vuln_scan.json` | [#44](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/44) | 2026-07-11 |
| **PERF-DB-CONN-LEAK** | Implemented the production gate stated on this finding when it was Deferred (not Rejected): "add `psycopg_pool` to the service requirements and route DB access through a `ConnectionPool` (bounded min/max), then load-test under concurrency." The original deferral's premise was already verified accurate (no actual connection leak — psycopg3's `with conn:` already closes the connection on block exit) but two things were genuinely blocking a real fix: `psycopg_pool` wasn't installed in this environment, and a hand-rolled shared module-level connection would have been thread-unsafe (this service's FastAPI HTTP handlers run concurrently with its consumer event loop; a single psycopg3 connection is not safe for concurrent use). Confirmed via a real (non-dry-run) install that `psycopg_pool` genuinely installs (`psycopg-pool==3.3.1`), added to both services' `requirements.txt`. New `pg_pool.py` (duplicated per service, matching this repo's established package-duplication convention — `otlp_export.py`/`traceparent.py`/`xdr_event_contracts.py` already do the same): `conninfo()` rebuilds the exact same DSN/discrete-host-var logic `connect_pg()` used to read directly; `get_pool()` lazily constructs one bounded `psycopg_pool.ConnectionPool` per process on first real use (`DB_POOL_MIN_SIZE`/`DB_POOL_MAX_SIZE`/`DB_POOL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` env-tunable, defaulting to 1/5/5s), returning `None` when psycopg/psycopg_pool is unavailable or no DB config is present — the same "no database configured" signal `connect_pg()` returning `None` always meant, so every existing `if conn is None:` / dry-run branch needed zero logic changes. `psycopg_pool.ConnectionPool` is thread-safe by design (internal locking around bounded checkout/return) — this is what actually resolves the original finding's concurrency concern, not merely "fewer connections." `connect_pg()` itself, in both `main.py` files, became a `@contextlib.contextmanager`-decorated generator wrapping `pool.connection()` (psycopg_pool's own borrow-plus-commit/rollback-plus-return-to-pool context manager) rather than a plain function returning a raw connection — a real API-shape change was unavoidable, since a raw connection handed back from a pool and later closed via the pre-existing `with conn:` idiom would have destroyed it instead of returning it to the pool, silently breaking the pool's own bookkeeping. Chose to keep the name `connect_pg()` and the `with connect_pg() as conn: if conn is None: ...` calling convention identical at every one of the 6 write-path call sites (`write_postgres`, `store_operational_event` ×2, `write_advisory_finding`, `write_dlq_record`, `write_incidents`) specifically so the existing `test_db5_tenant_id_propagation.py` suite's `patch.object(aw/ib, "connect_pg", return_value=FakeConn())` mocks kept working completely unmodified — the mock intercepts the call itself, and since `FakeConn` already implements `__enter__`/`__exit__` returning self, `with connect_pg() as conn:` transparently degrades to `with FakeConn() as conn:` under the mock, with zero test changes needed. One real behavior fix along the way: incident-builder's `/v1/build` response's `"dry_run"` field previously called the old `connect_pg()` directly as a truthiness check (`connect_pg() is None`) — since the new `connect_pg()` is a context-manager factory that's never `None` itself, that call site was corrected to check `pg_pool.get_pool() is None` instead, the equivalent cheap presence check without opening a throwaway connection. Removed the now-dead top-level `try: import psycopg / except: psycopg = None` block from both `main.py` files (psycopg is only touched by `pg_pool.py` now — confirmed no remaining reference to the `main.py`-level name via grep before removing). Wired `DB_POOL_MIN_SIZE`/`DB_POOL_MAX_SIZE`/`DB_POOL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` into `docker-compose.yml` (both services) and `.env.example`, all with safe conservative defaults (1/5/5s) matching every other optional-tunable convention already used in this repo (e.g. `DB_PORT: ${DB_PORT:-5432}`). 24 new direct-import unit tests for `pg_pool.py` per service (conninfo precedence/incompleteness/psycopg-absent handling; lazy pool construction reused across calls, not rebuilt per call; bounded min/max/timeout correctly read from env with sane defaults; `reset_pool_for_tests()` actually closes and drops the cached pool) + 6 new integration tests (3 per service) on `connect_pg()`/`write_postgres()`/`write_incidents()` proving the real borrow-from-pool-and-return-on-exit flow and the pool-unavailable no-op path. `python -m unittest discover -s tests/alert_writer`: **111 passed** (96 baseline + 15 new — 12 pg_pool + 3 integration). `python -m unittest discover -s tests/incident_builder`: **112 passed** (97 baseline + 15 new). `python -m unittest discover -s tests/xdr_topic_bootstrap`: **1365 passed, 0 failures** — zero regression, including `test_db5_tenant_id_propagation.py`'s `connect_pg`-mocking tests, confirmed unmodified and still passing (this suite's known pre-existing fragility — `test_db5_tenant_id_propagation.py` fails when run standalone due to an unrelated `requests`-module test-stub ordering bug in its own `_stub_deps()` helper, verified via `git stash` to reproduce identically against the pre-change baseline, so it is not something this task introduced or needed to fix; the suite passes correctly under this repo's actual invocation convention, full-directory `unittest discover`). `docker compose config --quiet`: clean. **Still open**: an actual concurrent load test against a live Postgres cluster to empirically confirm pool behavior under real concurrency — genuinely blocked by no live Docker daemon in this environment (`docker info` fails), not a code gap; the thread-safety guarantee itself comes from `psycopg_pool`'s own design, which this task wires in correctly but cannot independently load-verify here. | `services/alert-writer-service/{pg_pool.py,main.py,requirements.txt}`, `services/incident-builder-service/{pg_pool.py,main.py,requirements.txt}`, `tests/alert_writer/{test_pg_pool.py,test_alert_writer.py}`, `tests/incident_builder/{test_pg_pool.py,test_incident_builder.py}`, `docker-compose.yml`, `.env.example` | [#45](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/45) | 2026-07-11 |
| **IDENTITY-SSO-MFA (OIDC SSO federation)** | Closed the "SSO/SAML/OIDC federation" scope that remained after TOTP MFA + mandatory approval-route enforcement were completed in an earlier pass: real OIDC authorization-code SSO login, off by default (`SOC_OIDC_SSO_ENABLED`, gated in `config/oidc.php`) so existing password(+TOTP) login is completely unaffected until an operator opts in and points the pinned endpoint config at a real IdP. New `App\Services\OidcSsoService`: `authorizationUrl()` builds the `/authorize` redirect; `exchangeCodeForTokens()` POSTs the authorization code to the token endpoint; `validateIdToken()` verifies the ID token. This is the one place in this codebase's auth stack that deliberately does NOT follow the established "implement the protocol dependency-free against stdlib" convention (`TotpService`'s RFC 6238 TOTP, `TraceparentService`'s W3C Trace Context both do) — RS256 JWT signature verification against a JWKS keyset is a well-documented source of real-world vulnerabilities when hand-rolled (algorithm-confusion attacks, missing/bypassed signature checks), so this uses the widely-audited `firebase/php-jwt` (`^7.1`, confirmed genuinely installable via a real, non-dry-run `composer require`) instead of a from-scratch implementation. `firebase/php-jwt`'s `JWT::decode()` only checks the signature plus `exp`/`nbf`/`iat` on its own — `validateIdToken()` additionally asserts the OIDC-mandatory `iss` (must match the pinned issuer) and `aud` (must match this app's client_id, including the array-of-audiences form) claims, and the `nonce` claim via `hash_equals()` against the value minted at redirect time and stashed in session — closing the exact gaps a real IdP integration needs beyond "the signature is valid." JWKS responses are cached (`Cache::remember`, TTL `OIDC_JWKS_CACHE_SECONDS` default 3600s) so a login doesn't refetch the IdP's signing keys on every request while still picking up a real key rotation within the hour. New `App\Http\Controllers\Auth\OidcSsoController`: `redirect()`/`callback()`, both `abort(404)` unless SSO is enabled (so the routes are inert, not just hidden, when the feature is off). State and nonce are generated with `Str::random(40)`, stored in session, and consumed single-use (`session()->pull()`) in the callback — replaying a callback URL with a spent state fails the very next check. The single most important safety property, deliberately tested with an explicit assertion, is that **SSO login never auto-provisions a new user account**: only an existing user matched by a *verified* (`email_verified: true`) email claim can sign in this way; an unrecognized or unverified identity is turned away with a message to contact an administrator and a positive test asserts zero rows get created in that path — directly matching this platform's established "advisory-only, no autonomous account actions" posture already used for every other IdP-adjacent feature (Enterprise Integrations' inbound Okta/Azure AD event ingestion explicitly never suspends/creates accounts either). A per-user TOTP second factor, if the matched account has one enabled, is still enforced after a successful SSO login via the exact same `mfa_pending_user_id` session hand-off `AuthenticatedSessionController::store()` already uses for password login — SSO does not implicitly grant a bypass of the local second factor. `redirect_uri` defaults to the `sso.oidc.callback` route computed at request time (always matches the app's real `APP_URL`) rather than a fixed env var, with an explicit env override available for deployments that need one pinned. Since no real external IdP (Okta/Azure AD) exists in this environment to configure/test against, the entire flow was verified against a **real local mock OIDC provider**: a new `tests/Traits/GeneratesMockOidcTokens` trait generates a genuine ephemeral RSA keypair per test process (`openssl_pkey_new`, never committed anywhere) and signs real RS256 ID tokens with `firebase/php-jwt`'s own `JWT::encode()`, so `validateIdToken()`'s signature verification is exercised against a token an attacker-model adversary could not have forged, not merely asserted via a stubbed claims array. Hit one genuine environment-specific obstacle along the way: `openssl_pkey_new()` failed outright on this Windows/PHP setup (`Cannot get key from parameter 1`, root-caused via `openssl_error_string()` to `BIO_new_file: no such file` — PHP's OpenSSL extension couldn't locate an `openssl.cnf` via its default search path even though a real `openssl.cnf` exists on-disk, shipped with Git for Windows) — fixed by retrying once with an explicit `config` option pointed at a small list of well-known candidate paths (`OPENSSL_CONF` env var first, then common Git-for-Windows/Linux locations), so the trait degrades gracefully (falls through to the default, unmodified path) on Linux/Mac CI where this problem doesn't exist, and only reaches into the fallback when the default call genuinely fails. 12 new `tests/Unit/OidcSsoServiceTest.php` tests (authorization URL shape/params, token exchange request body and failure paths, JWKS response caching verified via `Http::assertSentCount(1)` across two `validateIdToken()` calls, and six real-signature-verification cases: valid token accepted, wrong issuer/audience/nonce each rejected, an expired token rejected via the library's own `ExpiredException`, and — the most security-relevant case — a token signed with a completely different, unregistered keypair correctly rejected via `SignatureInvalidException` even though its claims are otherwise well-formed) + 13 new `tests/Feature/Auth/OidcSsoControllerTest.php` tests (both routes 404 when disabled, full redirect-with-state/nonce flow, IdP-error-parameter denial, state-missing/state-mismatch/code-missing denials, successful login for a matching verified-email user, the account-auto-provisioning-never-happens test, unverified-email denial, invalid-signature denial, TOTP-still-required-after-SSO, and single-use state/nonce consumption). `php artisan test --filter=Auth`: 228/228 passed. Full suite `php artisan test --parallel --recreate-databases`: **4995 passed, 14064 assertions, 0 failures** (4970 baseline + 25 new — the full suite was run, not just the targeted Auth filter, since this touches a globally-registered route file and the shared login view). `docker compose config --quiet`: clean. **Still open**: SAML federation (a separate protocol, not attempted this pass), and end-to-end verification against a real corporate IdP (Okta/Azure AD) — genuinely blocked by no real external IdP credentials existing in this environment, not a code gap; the mock-IdP verification here exercises the exact same cryptographic and protocol-logic code paths a real IdP integration would. | `app/Services/OidcSsoService.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Auth/OidcSsoController.php`, `config/oidc.php`, `routes/auth.php`, `resources/views/auth/login.blade.php`, `tests/Traits/GeneratesMockOidcTokens.php`, `tests/Unit/OidcSsoServiceTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Auth/OidcSsoControllerTest.php`, `composer.json` | [#46](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/46) | 2026-07-11 |
| **IDENTITY-SSO-MFA (SAML SSO federation)** | Closes the last open scope of IDENTITY-SSO-MFA (TOTP MFA, mandatory approval-route enforcement, and OIDC SSO federation were already done in earlier passes — see the three rows above): SP-initiated SAML 2.0 SSO federation login, off by default (`SOC_SAML_SSO_ENABLED`, gated in `config/saml.php`), so existing password(+TOTP)/OIDC login is completely unaffected until an operator opts in and points the pinned IdP settings at a real IdP. Like OIDC's `firebase/php-jwt`, this is the one other place in the auth stack that deliberately does not follow the codebase's "implement the protocol dependency-free" convention: XML digital-signature verification (specifically, defending against XML signature wrapping attacks — a well-documented real-world SAML vulnerability class distinct from and arguably worse than JWT algorithm-confusion) is delegated to `onelogin/php-saml` (`^4.3`, pulling in `robrichards/xmlseclibs` — confirmed genuinely installable via a real, non-dry-run `composer require`, and `composer audit` shows zero advisories for either package at the pinned versions). New `App\Services\SamlSsoService`: `buildLoginRedirect()` wraps `OneLogin\Saml2\Auth::login(..., stay: true)` to get the IdP redirect URL plus the AuthnRequest's ID without letting the library issue its own `header()`/`exit`; `processResponse()` wraps `Auth::processResponse()`. Three security-relevant settings are hardcoded in the service's `security` array, never left implicit or config-driven: `wantAssertionsSigned=true` (every assertion's signature is verified against `config('saml.idp.x509cert')` specifically — confirmed by direct inspection of `Utils::validateSign()` that the trust anchor is the *pinned config value*, not whatever certificate an incoming response happens to embed in its own `<ds:KeyInfo>`, so a forged response signed with an attacker-controlled key/cert is rejected regardless of what it claims about itself), `wantXMLValidation=true` (real XSD schema validation via the library's own bundled schemas, `saml-schema-protocol-2.0.xsd` et al. — confirmed present at the library's expected default path, not silently skipped), and `rejectUnsolicitedResponsesWithInResponseTo=true` combined with round-tripping the SP-initiated AuthnRequest's ID through the session (`saml_sso_request_id`) and back into `processResponse($expectedRequestId)` — verified via 4 explicit scratch-script scenarios before committing to this design: a matching ID is accepted, a mismatched ID is rejected, a genuine IdP-initiated response (no prior AuthnRequest, no `InResponseTo` at all) is still correctly accepted — matching how enterprise IdP app-launcher/dashboard tiles actually work — and a *foreign* response that claims an `InResponseTo` while the SP has no session state for it is rejected as a likely replay. Because `onelogin/php-saml` is framework-agnostic (reads `$_SERVER`/`$_POST` directly, and `Response::isValid()` cross-checks the assertion's signed `<Destination>` attribute against the *actual* current request URL computed from `$_SERVER`), both `buildLoginRedirect()` and `processResponse()` open by mirroring the already-authoritative Illuminate `Request`'s server bag into PHP's superglobals first (`foreach ($request->server->all() as $key => $value) { $_SERVER[$key] = $value; }`) — a genuine no-op under a real webserver (where `$_SERVER` is already populated identically) and the only way this Destination-binding security check is exercisable at all under Laravel's test client (which never touches PHP superglobals, unlike a real HTTP server). New `App\Http\Controllers\Auth\SamlSsoController`: `login`/`acs`/`metadata`, all three `abort(404)` unless SSO is enabled. Mirrors `OidcSsoController`'s account-matching posture exactly: only an existing user matched by the assertion's NameID (configured `NameIDFormat=urn:oasis:names:tc:SAML:1.1:nameid-format:emailAddress`) can sign in — SSO login never auto-provisions a new account, verified with the same explicit zero-rows-created regression test pattern OIDC's suite already established. SAML has no equivalent to OIDC's separate `email_verified` claim; the cryptographic signature check against the pinned IdP certificate is itself the trust anchor here, documented explicitly in the controller's docblock rather than left as an unstated design gap. A per-user TOTP second factor, if enabled, is still enforced after SSO login via the same `mfa_pending_user_id` session hand-off used by both password and OIDC login. `metadata()` exposes this SP's own metadata XML (`Settings::getSPMetadata()`) at `/sso/saml/metadata` specifically so a real IdP can be configured against this SP once one becomes available — the only concrete artifact this pass can hand to a future real-IdP integration pass. `routes/auth.php` adds `GET sso/saml/login` / `POST sso/saml/acs` / `GET sso/saml/metadata`; `app/Http/Middleware/VerifyCsrfToken.php` excludes `sso/saml/acs` from CSRF verification (the IdP POSTs the SAMLResponse directly via HTTP-POST binding — no Laravel session/CSRF token crosses that external redirect; authenticity comes from the assertion's XML signature instead, exactly matching the reasoning already documented for OIDC's callback). Since no real external IdP (Okta/Azure AD/ADFS) exists in this environment, the entire flow was verified against a **real local mock SAML IdP**: a new `tests/Traits/GeneratesMockSamlResponses` trait generates a genuine ephemeral RSA keypair + self-signed X.509 certificate per test process (`openssl_pkey_new`/`openssl_csr_new`/`openssl_csr_sign`, never committed anywhere, reusing the same Windows `openssl.cnf`-path-discovery fallback already proven in `GeneratesMockOidcTokens`) and hand-builds a full SAML 2.0 `<samlp:Response>`/`<saml:Assertion>` XML document (Issuer, Subject/NameID, SubjectConfirmationData, Conditions/AudienceRestriction, AuthnStatement/AuthnContext), then signs the Assertion with genuine assertion-level enveloped XML-DSig (RSA-SHA256, via `robrichards/xmlseclibs`'s `XMLSecurityDSig`/`XMLSecurityKey` — the exact same primitive `onelogin/php-saml` itself uses internally, matching the codebase's established pattern of testing against a real mock provider rather than a stub). The fixture was iteratively proven correct against the real library in a throwaway scratch script before being committed to the test trait: confirmed real XSD schema validation passes, confirmed `Auth::processResponse()` genuinely authenticates a well-formed signed response end-to-end, and confirmed all four negative cases actually fail for the right reason (tampered post-signing content fails digest/reference validation — proving the signature covers content, not just structural presence; a response signed by a different, unregistered keypair fails signature validation against the pinned cert; the four InResponseTo/unsolicited-response scenarios above). 12 new `tests/Unit/SamlSsoServiceTest.php` tests (valid signed response accepted; matching/mismatched/unsolicited-with-InResponseTo all handled correctly; tampered-assertion-content rejected; rogue-keypair-signature rejected; wrong-audience rejected; expired-assertion rejected; wrong-Destination rejected; SP metadata generation contains the configured entity ID and ACS URL; the AuthnRequest redirect URL is inflated/decoded end-to-end to confirm it genuinely targets the configured IdP/SP rather than just asserting an opaque blob was produced) + 12 new `tests/Feature/Auth/SamlSsoControllerTest.php` tests (all three actions 404 when disabled; full login-redirect→ACS round trip logs in the matching user; the account-auto-provisioning-never-happens regression test; rogue-signature/tampered-content/mismatched-InResponseTo all denied through the real HTTP layer, not just the service layer; TOTP challenge still required after SSO login; session request-ID is single-use). Every ACS-endpoint test posts to a fully-qualified `https://app.test/sso/saml/acs` URL rather than a relative path specifically so the request Laravel's test client constructs matches the `Destination` baked into each signed fixture — documented explicitly in the test class's own docblock so a future reader doesn't "simplify" it into a relative path and silently defeat the Destination-binding check being tested. Pre-existing `OidcSsoServiceTest`/`OidcSsoControllerTest`/`TotpServiceTest`/`MfaTest`/`MfaEnforcementTest` all pass unmodified (zero regression from the new CSRF exception or route additions). `docker compose config --quiet`: clean. Full suite `php artisan test --parallel --recreate-databases`: **5036 passed, 14146 assertions, 0 failures** (5012 baseline + 24 new). **Still open**: end-to-end verification against a real corporate IdP (Okta/Azure AD/ADFS) — genuinely blocked by no real external IdP credentials existing in this environment, not a code gap; the mock-IdP verification here exercises the exact same cryptographic and protocol-logic code paths (real XML-DSig signature verification, real XSD schema validation, real Destination/Audience/Conditions/InResponseTo checks) a real IdP integration would, and the new `/sso/saml/metadata` endpoint is the concrete handoff point for that future pass. | `app/Services/SamlSsoService.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Auth/SamlSsoController.php`, `config/saml.php`, `routes/auth.php`, `app/Http/Middleware/VerifyCsrfToken.php`, `.env.example`, `tests/Traits/GeneratesMockSamlResponses.php`, `tests/Unit/SamlSsoServiceTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Auth/SamlSsoControllerTest.php`, `composer.json` | [#50](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/50) | 2026-07-11 |

| **AI-3** | Confirmed the finding: `AiAnalystManager::incidentContext()` built its shared `$context['alerts']` array via a plain `(array) $row` cast on each `security_alerts` row, so the `evidence`/`raw_event` JSON columns — free-form blobs sourced from whatever a raw request/log capture happened to record, not a curated field — reached every AI provider completely unredacted. Traced both real exposure paths: `AiRagServiceProvider::generate()` re-derives its own `$evidence` array directly from `$context['alerts']` and posts the FULL merged alert object (including the untouched `evidence`/`raw_event` strings) to the standalone `ai-rag-service` over a real HTTP call; `RemoteLlmProvider::generate()` sends `$context['_prompt']`, built by `renderPrompt()` → `compactContext()`, which already projects an allowlisted subset of alert fields but — the actual gap — includes `'evidence' => mb_substr((string) $a['evidence'], 0, 400)`, a raw, unredacted, merely-truncated JSON substring, sent as part of the prompt to a real third-party LLM API (OpenAI-compatible/Ollama) when configured. `LocalAiAnalystProvider` never touches `evidence`/`raw_event` content and never leaves the process, so it carried no exposure risk and needed no change. Fixed centrally rather than per-provider: added a private `redactAlertEvidence(array $alert): array` to `AiAnalystManager`, applied via `->map()` when `incidentContext()` builds the `alerts` array — decodes each of `evidence`/`raw_event` (skipping gracefully if either is absent, non-string, or not valid JSON), runs the decoded structure through `TraceRedactor::deep($decoded, redactEmails: true)` — the exact same primitive `tests/Feature/TraceRedactionTest.php` already covers for trace investigation views/APIs, deliberately not a new redaction implementation — and re-encodes back to a JSON string, so both downstream consumers (`AiRagServiceProvider`'s own `json_decode($alert['evidence'] ?? '{}', true)` re-decode, and `compactContext()`'s `mb_substr((string) $a['evidence'], ...)` string truncation) continue working completely unchanged, now simply operating on already-redacted content. Verified `TraceRedactor::isSensitiveKey()` does not match `xdr_domains` (the key `AiRagServiceProvider` reads out of the decoded evidence to derive `telemetry_type`), confirming that specific downstream read path survives the redaction pass intact. Deliberately left top-level investigative identifier fields (`actor_key`, `ip`, `alert_type`, `trace_id`, ...) completely untouched — matches `TraceRedactor`'s own stated design principle verbatim (deep-redact JSON payload field *values*, pass investigative identifiers through unchanged so analysts can do their job) rather than inventing a stricter policy specific to this call site. **Deliberately bounded scope, explicitly not touched**: `ioc_hits` (already-curated indicator values — matched IP/domain/hash — not raw log content, materially lower risk) and `soc_knowledge_base`/`knowledge_refs` (analyst-authored markdown, not derived from raw alert JSON at all) were reviewed and assessed as out of this finding's actual blast radius rather than silently expanded into. 5 new `tests/Feature/AiEvidenceRedactionTest.php` tests: sensitive keys (`password`, `api_token`) redacted to `[REDACTED]` while a co-located non-sensitive key (`evidence_chain`) survives verbatim in the same object (proving this isn't a blunt wipe-everything behavior), an embedded email address redacted to `[EMAIL]` inside a free-form string value, `raw_event` covered identically to `evidence` (via a nested `headers.Authorization` Bearer-token value), investigative identifiers (`actor_key`/`ip`) explicitly asserted to survive untouched, and — the test that actually proves the exposure is closed, not just that a helper function behaves correctly in isolation — an end-to-end test building a real `incidentContext()`, feeding it through the real `AiRagServiceProvider::generate()`, and asserting a planted secret value never appears anywhere in the actual outbound HTTP request body captured by `Http::fake()`. All pre-existing AI-path tests pass unmodified: `SocAiKnowledgeMaturityTest` (7), `SocAiTenantIsolationTest` (9, from the immediately-preceding AI-2 fix in this same session), and `AiRagServiceProviderInternalAuthTest` (3, from AI-1) — zero regression across all three, confirming this redaction pass doesn't alter any existing observable behavior for callers that were already working correctly. `php artisan test --parallel --recreate-databases`: **5012 passed, 14093 assertions, 0 failures** (5007 baseline + 5 new). Hit one genuine environment obstacle mid-task, unrelated to the code change itself: the full parallel suite run crashed with exit 255 immediately after all 5012 tests completed with zero failure markers, root-caused to the Windows `%TEMP%` drive (`C:\Users\...\AppData\Local\Temp`, the same mount `/tmp` resolves to in this shell) being **completely full — 0 bytes free, 223G/223G used** — paratest's own result-aggregation/report-writing step had nowhere to write; confirmed by re-running the identical command with output and paratest's working files redirected to the `D:` drive instead (142G free), which completed cleanly. This is a standing environment condition worth remembering, not something this task caused or fixed. | `app/Support/AiAnalystManager.php`, `tests/Feature/AiEvidenceRedactionTest.php` | [#49](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/49) | 2026-07-11 |
