## 2. Implementation & Verification Details

### Task 23.0.A: Tenant Null Creation Guard (Strict Mode Boundary)
* **Goal**: Prevent admins from accidentally creating records with `tenant_id = NULL` on store routes when strict mode is active.
* **Verification Details**:
  * Added `requireExplicitScope` parameters to `TenantContextAuthority::validateAndResolve()`.
  * Added `GLOBAL_SCOPE` ('_global') sentinel to allow explicit admin-only global scope creation.
  * Verified non-admins are spoofing-blocked from using `_global` sentinel.
  * Updated [ShadowSoakController.php](file:///D:/project/Detector/app/Http/Controllers/ShadowSoak/ShadowSoakController.php) to pass `requireExplicitScope: true`.
  * Verified unit and feature integration tests are green.

### Task 23.0.B / 23.1: Tenant Null Audit Command & Table Validation
* **Goal**: Provide a read-only CLI tool (`php artisan tenant:null-audit`) to count null records across all tenant-isolated tables, validating `--table` arguments strictly against isolated tables.
* **Verification Details**:
  * Implemented [TenantNullAuditCommand.php](file:///D:/project/Detector/app/Console/Commands/TenantNullAuditCommand.php).
  * Ensured command strictly performs read-only count queries (fully compliant with append-only database rule).
  * Added options for `--table` filter and `--output` JSON reports path.
  * Command returns exit code `0` when clean and `1` when null records are found.
  * Rejects tables not defined in `TenantBoundaryService::ISOLATED_TABLES` (such as `users` or non-existent tables) and exits with code `1`.

### Task 23.0.C / 23.2: Test Coverage & Regression Testing
* **Goal**: Create and run regression test coverage for strict store validations and audit outputs, including rejection of non-isolated tables.
* **Verification Details**:
  * Developed [TenantNullCreationGuardTest.php](file:///D:/project/Detector/tests/Feature/TenantNullCreationGuardTest.php) (24/24 tests PASS).
  * Updated [TenantIndexCreationSafetyTest.php](file:///D:/project/Detector/tests/Feature/TenantIndexCreationSafetyTest.php) (24/24 tests PASS).
  * Verified rejection of `--table=users` (unisolated) and `--table=nonexistent_table_xyz` in the command.

### Task ENV.1: Align Environment Variables in `.env`
* **Goal**: Synchronize all missing keys from `.env.example` to prevent configuration drift.
* **Verification Details**:
  * Appended the 20 missing keys to the end of [.env](file:///D:/project/Detector/.env).

### Task ENV.3: Hardening `.gitignore` for Reports and Logs
* **Goal**: Exclude dynamically generated test output report files and database dump logs to avoid bloating the Git history.
* **Verification Details**:
  * Appended exclusions for reports (`/reports/*.json`, `/reports/*.md`, etc.) to [.gitignore](file:///D:/project/Detector/.gitignore).

### Task T1 / #1: Fix Threat-Hunting Domain Count Mismatch (158 → 161)
* **Goal**: Update documentation and baseline settings from 158 to 161 domains (due to shadow domain soak harness domains).
* **Verification Details**:
  * Updated [README.md](file:///D:/project/Detector/README.md) to reference 161 query domains instead of 158.
  * Updated [DemoPlatformPackagingService.php](file:///D:/project/Detector/app/Services/DemoPlatformPackagingService.php) baseline count and descriptors to 161.
  * Updated [DocumentationFreezeTest.php](file:///D:/project/Detector/tests/Feature/DocumentationFreezeTest.php) to assert 161 domains.
  * Verified that `AGENTS.md` and `claude.md` already refer to 161 domains.

### Task T2: Rename Domain Count Test Methods
* **Goal**: Rename test cases checking threat hunting domain counts that had historical values (95, 100, 105, 110, 115, 120, 125, 130) to generic or matching names.
* **Verification Details**:
  * Renamed outdated test methods to `test_threat_hunting_supported_domains_count`, `test_threat_hunting_domain_count_is_161`, or `test_threat_hunting_service_has_161_domains` in:
    * `AdvancedHuntingInvestigationTest.php`
    * `CapacityGovernanceTest.php`
    * `ComplianceGovernanceTest.php`
    * `DistributedReliabilityTest.php`
    * `EndpointFleetHardeningTest.php`
    * `SoarOrchestrationTest.php`
    * `DemoPlatformPackagingTest.php`
    * `CodeLevelXdrMaturityTest.php`

### Task T3: Extract Advisory-Only Constraint Assertions into Trait
* **Goal**: Remove code duplication by extracting shared containment and auto-remediation checks.
* **Verification Details**:
  * Created `AssertsAdvisoryOnlyConstraints` trait under `tests/Traits/AssertsAdvisoryOnlyConstraints.php`.
  * Replaced 60 duplicate test methods across 12 separate feature test classes with a single clean trait inclusion.

### BUG: SecretsValidationService and phpdotenv Compatibility
* **Goal**: Enable direct modification of environment variables during test execution.
* **Verification Details**:
  * Updated `SecretsValidationService` to read directly from `getenv()` instead of `env()` helper because phpdotenv's ImmutableRepository caches variables at bootstrap, ignoring subsequent `putenv()` overrides in test runs.

| **CAP-DETECT-BACKTEST** | Implemented a bounded, honest replay-safe historical backtest — deliberately NOT a generic rule evaluator. Prior research (this session) established `registry.v1.json` has no field-condition/expression language at all and that the only working identity/cloud/saas detection logic is hand-coded Python in `scripts/xdr_correlation_detector.py` (`detect_identity()`/`detect_cloud_saas()`), which writes directly to the **active** `security_alerts`/`security_incidents` tables via `insert_alerts()`/`expand_incidents()` — not advisory-safe, and not something a backtest can safely call into. New `DetectionBacktestService` **ports the exact thresholds** from those two Python functions into PHP (12 rules total, all confirmed 1:1 against real `registry.v1.json` rule_ids): `IDENTITY_MFA_FAILURE_BURST` (failed≥5), `IDENTITY_FAILED_LOGIN_ACROSS_SERVICES` (failed≥4 AND ≥2 distinct event_source), `IDENTITY_RISKY_IP_LOGIN` (risk_score≥0.7 + login_success), `IDENTITY_IMPOSSIBLE_TRAVEL` (≥2 distinct source_ip on login_success), `IDENTITY_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION`, `IDENTITY_UNUSUAL_LOGIN_SOURCE` (grouped by IP, not user — ≥3 distinct users per IP), `CLOUD_UNUSUAL_API_ACTIVITY` (risk_score≥0.7, count≥3), `CLOUD_SUSPICIOUS_OBJECT_ACCESS` (count≥5), `CLOUD_MASS_DOWNLOAD` (count≥10), `CLOUD_NEW_ACCESS_KEY`, `CLOUD_SECURITY_SETTING_MODIFIED`, `SAAS_UNUSUAL_ADMIN_ACTIVITY` — evaluated read-only against `telemetry_events` (a real, durable Postgres table, confirmed queryable over arbitrary trailing windows) using the same `Collection::groupBy` idiom already established by `App\Console\Commands\XdrIdentityRiskCommand`, not a new query convention. New `detection_backtest_runs` (window/rule_ids/event-count per invocation) + `detection_backtest_matches` (append-only, one row per rule_id×actor that would have fired, with a small sample-event preview) tables — entirely separate from the fixture-pack-shaped `detection_replay_results` table, which has no window/count fields and couldn't represent this. **Never writes to `security_alerts`/`security_incidents`** — verified by a dedicated test that runs all 12 rules against 20 seeded triggering events and asserts both tables stay at zero rows afterward. New `DetectionBacktestController` (run/list/view results) + minimal Blade views, gated by the existing `soc:rules.govern` permission (same as `DetectionRuleController`) — no new permission invented. +16 tests (`DetectionBacktestTest`): one per rule's trigger threshold (at and below), window-exclusion (events outside the trailing-N-days range are excluded from both the scan and the count), unsupported rule_id rejection, the security_alerts/security_incidents non-write guarantee, and RBAC-gated HTTP routes. | `database/migrations/2026_07_14_010001_create_detection_backtest_tables.php`, `app/Models/DetectionBacktestRun.php`, `app/Models/DetectionBacktestMatch.php`, `app/Services/DetectionBacktestService.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Detection/DetectionBacktestController.php`, `routes/web.php`, `resources/views/detection/backtest.blade.php`, `resources/views/detection/backtest-show.blade.php`, `tests/Feature/DetectionBacktestTest.php` | — | 2026-07-07 |
| **ENT-TENANCY-RISK-SCORING** | Confirmed the finding: `EntityRiskScoringService::alertsForEntity()`/`incidentsForEntity()` (and 3 of the type-specific collectors — `collectUserFactors`/`collectHostFactors`/`collectNetworkFactors`) queried `security_alerts`/`security_incidents` globally by entity key (actor_key/ip/trace_id) with zero `tenant_id` filtering — an entity key existing in two tenants (shared IP, common email) would silently aggregate and leak the other tenant's alerts/incidents into the risk factor breakdown. **Deliberately bounded scope**: `calculateRisk()` calls ~15 factor collectors total; only the 5 above (all backed by `security_alerts`/`security_incidents`, both already tenant-tagged since BACKLOG-TENANCY-019) were fixed. The other ~10 (cross-domain, active-response, UEBA, endpoint-operational, low-level-telemetry, investigation-graph, SOAR) query tables confirmed to have **no `tenant_id` column at all** (`cross_domain_correlations`, `response_executions`, `endpoint_agents`, `baseline_anomaly_scores`, `investigation_*`, `soar_*`) — fixing those would mean new migrations across a dozen unrelated tables, which is the explicit subject of separate, already-filed backlog items (`ENT-TENANCY-RESPONSE-EXECUTION`, `ENT-TENANCY-UEBA`) rather than this one. Added an optional `?string $tenantId = null` parameter threaded from `calculateRisk()` through every affected collector into the two data-retrieval helpers, using the existing manual-closure scoping convention already established by `SecurityAlertController` (`$query->when($tenantId !== null, fn ($q) => $q->where('tenant_id', $tenantId))`) rather than `TenantBoundaryService::scopeQuery()` — confirmed that method is dead code (zero callers anywhere) and type-hinted for Eloquent `Builder`, not the `Query\Builder` these raw `DB::table()` calls return, so it wasn't actually reusable as-is. Also added an entity-ownership check at the top of `calculateRisk()`: if the entity's own `tenant_id` is non-null and doesn't match the requesting tenant, returns `[]` (treated as not-found, matching how `AdvisoryFindingsController`/`DlqController` already use `assertAccess()`'s single-record pattern) — a `null` `tenant_id` on either side preserves the existing permissive/legacy behavior, deliberately not tightened, since demo/legacy rows with no tenant attribution still need to remain visible. `EntityRiskApiController::entityRisk()`/`riskHistory()` now inject `TenantContextAuthority` and resolve tenant context from the request exactly like `SecurityAlertController` already does, then apply the same entity-ownership check before returning. `getRiskHistory()`'s backing table (`entity_risk_snapshots`) has no `tenant_id` column at all — not fixed directly, but since every snapshot row belongs to exactly one already-ownership-checked `entity_id`, this is not a live leak (documented, not a new migration). +9 tests (`EntityRiskScoringTenantIsolationTest`): cross-tenant alert/incident exclusion, MFA-burst-factor and host-persistence-factor cross-tenant exclusion, null-tenant-id legacy-passthrough preservation, entity-ownership-mismatch empty-result, null-tenant-entity accessible-from-any-context, and 2 HTTP-level tests through the real `X-Tenant-ID` header + `TenantContextAuthority::grantMembership()` path. All 34 pre-existing `EntityRiskScoringTest` tests still pass unmodified (zero regression). | `app/Services/EntityRiskScoringService.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Api/EntityRiskApiController.php`, `tests/Feature/EntityRiskScoringTenantIsolationTest.php` | — | 2026-07-07 |
| **ENT-TENANCY-ENTITY-GRAPH** | Confirmed the finding: `EntityGraphService::upsertEntity()` looked up/created rows via `Entity::firstOrCreate(['entity_type' => $type, 'entity_key' => $key], [...])` — no `tenant_id` at all — so two tenants observing the same `entity_key` (a shared IP, a common email) merged into ONE row, and `upsertRelationship()` had the identical gap. Root cause was DB-level, not just app-level: `entities` table's sole uniqueness guarantee was `entities_type_key_unique` on `(entity_type, entity_key)` — a plain multi-column `unique(type, key, tenant_id)` migration would have been unsafe, since Postgres treats NULL as distinct from NULL in unique constraints and every existing legacy/demo entity has `tenant_id IS NULL` — silently breaking dedup for all of them the moment it shipped. New migration replaces it with an expression-based `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ... (entity_type, entity_key, COALESCE(tenant_id, '_none'))`, keeping every null-tenant row in one shared bucket (preserving today's exact behavior for legacy data) while genuinely separating any two distinct non-null tenant values. `entity_relationships` had no `tenant_id` column and no DB-level unique constraint at all (uniqueness was already only app-enforced via a plain `SELECT` in `upsertRelationship()`) — added the column only, no constraint needed. Threaded an optional `?string $tenantId = null` (added as the last parameter on both methods for full backward compatibility with existing positional callers) through `upsertEntity()`/`upsertRelationship()`, and into `projectFromAlerts()`/`projectFromIncidents()` — deriving tenant_id **per row** from each alert/incident's own `tenant_id` column (not a single global scope parameter, since one projection batch can span multiple tenants). **Deliberately bounded scope**: the 5 `projectStream*()`/`projectResponseAcknowledgement()` methods (process-chain, connection, persistence, stream-to-trace, response-acknowledgement) are unchanged — their source telemetry (`endpoint_stream_events`, `response_executions`) has no `tenant_id` column yet, the explicit subject of `ENT-TENANCY-RESPONSE-EXECUTION` and streaming-telemetry tenancy work, not this task; a `'trace'`-type entity can still theoretically merge across tenants via that untouched path, documented as a known residual gap rather than silently left unmentioned. Also closed the read-side of the same leak: `EntityApiController` had zero tenant awareness across all 6 endpoints (`index`/`show`/`timeline`/`relationships`/`alerts`/`incidents`) — added a single `resolveEntity()` helper (mirroring `EntityRiskApiController`'s ownership-check pattern) used by the 5 single-entity endpoints, so an entity from another tenant now 404s instead of being traversable by ID. +8 tests (`EntityGraphTenantIsolationTest`): same-key-different-tenant now creates 2 rows, same-tenant repeat calls still dedupe (regression guard), null-tenant rows still dedupe against each other (legacy-behavior preservation), null-tenant vs non-null-tenant distinctness, per-tenant relationship separation, per-row tenant derivation in `projectFromAlerts()`, and 2 HTTP-level 404-vs-200 tests through the real `X-Tenant-ID` header path. All 38 pre-existing `EntityGraphTest` tests still pass unmodified. | `database/migrations/2026_07_15_010001_add_tenant_isolation_to_entity_graph.php`, `app/Models/Entity.php`, `app/Models/EntityRelationship.php`, `app/Services/EntityGraphService.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Api/EntityApiController.php`, `tests/Feature/EntityGraphTenantIsolationTest.php` | — | 2026-07-07 |

Migration adds `tenant_id` (nullable, indexed) to all 4 tables. In `ActiveResponseExecutionService`, only `createExecution()` needed a new `?string $tenantId = null` parameter — every other lifecycle method (`appendEvent`/`runSimulation`/`initiateRollback`/`projectEntityRelationships`) already receives the `ResponseExecution $exec` model and now simply reads `$exec->tenant_id` to stamp its own child record, since tenant_id is set once at creation and every sub-resource belongs to exactly one execution (avoided threading a new parameter through 10+ method signatures). Also fixed a leak this task's own combination with the prior `ENT-TENANCY-ENTITY-GRAPH` fix would otherwise have opened: `calculateBlastRadius()`/`computeBlastRadiusEntities()`/`resolveEntityId()` looked up the target `entities` row by `entity_key` alone with no tenant filter — after entities stopped merging across tenants (previous task), an unscoped `->first()` could now resolve to the *wrong* tenant's same-keyed entity, silently miscalculating blast radius/safety scores against another tenant's observation/relationship counts. Fixed with the same null-permissive pattern used elsewhere (`where('tenant_id', $tenantId)->orWhereNull('tenant_id')` — a tenant-scoped caller can still resolve a legacy null-tenant entity, matching `EntityApiController`'s ownership semantics) — verified with a dedicated test using two same-keyed entities with deliberately different `observation_count` values per tenant, asserting each tenant's blast radius calculation picks up its *own* entity's count. `getExecution()` gained the same entity-ownership-style guard used by `EntityRiskScoringService`/`EntityApiController` (non-null mismatched `tenant_id` → treated as not-found); `getRecentExecutions()`/`getPendingApprovals()`/`getRollbackCandidates()` gained optional tenant scoping. Both consumers — `ActiveResponseController` (11 web actions) and `ActiveResponseApiController` (4 JSON endpoints) — now inject `TenantContextAuthority` and resolve tenant context on every action; the web controller's `findOrFail()` was rewritten to route through the service's now-tenant-aware `getExecution()` rather than a raw unscoped query, so a single fix covers `show`/`simulationPreview`/`blastRadiusView`/`executionTimeline`/`submit`/`approve`/`reject`/`simulate`/`requestExecution`/`execute`/`initiateRollback`/`completeRollback`/`cancel` all at once. +14 tests (`ActiveResponseExecutionTenantIsolationTest`): tenant_id persistence and inheritance across execution→event→simulation→rollback, `getExecution` cross-tenant null-vs-match, null-tenant-id legacy-visibility, tenant-scoped list/pending/rollback-candidate queries, the blast-radius wrong-entity regression test, and HTTP-level 404-vs-200 through both the web and API controllers. | **ENT-TENANCY-UEBA** | Confirmed the finding, and found it ran deeper than the literal wording: not just missing `tenant_id` columns, but two DB-level unique constraints (`entity_behavior_baselines`' `ueba_baseline_entity_dimension_unique` on `(entity_key, entity_type, dimension)`, and `peer_group_profiles`' bare `peer_group_key` unique) that would silently MERGE two tenants' data the moment a shared entity_key or peer-group label appeared — exactly the same class of bug as `ENT-TENANCY-ENTITY-GRAPH`'s `entities` table. Fixed both with the same expression-based `COALESCE(tenant_id, '_none')` unique index technique (preserves today's exact dedup behavior for legacy null-tenant rows, genuinely separates any two non-null tenant values). `baseline_observations`/`baseline_anomaly_scores` (both append-only — never UPDATEd/DELETEd by the migration, only given a new nullable column) had no unique constraint to begin with, so only the column was added. The peer-group half of the finding was the more subtle bug: `UEBABaselineService::deriveGroupKey()` intentionally produces global, human-readable labels like `"user_role:admin"` with no tenant component — left unchanged (still global) — but `assignPeerGroup()`'s `PeerGroupProfile::firstOrCreate()` lookup now includes `tenant_id` as a **separate** criteria field alongside `peer_group_key`, backed by the new unique index, so two tenants' admin users get two distinct group rows sharing the same human-readable key string rather than one merged row; verified with a dedicated test asserting `computePeerGroupProfile()` aggregating tenant-a's group never picks up tenant-b's member baselines even though both groups share the identical `peer_group_key`. Consistently applied the `->when($tenantId !== null, fn ($q) => $q->where('tenant_id', $tenantId))` convention (established across all 3 prior `ENT-TENANCY-*` fixes) throughout every LIST/browse read method (`getAnomalyHistory`/`getTopAnomalousEntities`/`getBaselineDriftSummary`/`getAnomalyVolumeTrend`/`buildBaselineProfile`) — a `null` tenantId (admin/unscoped caller) sees across all tenants, matching `TenantContextAuthority`'s admin-bypass semantics — while WRITE/dedup-lookup operations (`computeBaseline`, `scoreAnomaly`'s own baseline lookup, `scorePeerGroupDeviation`, `assignPeerGroup`, `computePeerGroupProfile`) deliberately use strict `where('tenant_id', $tenantId)` equality instead, since a tenant-scoped write must only ever match/update its own tenant's pre-existing rows — caught and corrected 4 places in an initial pass that used strict equality where the `->when()` list-browse semantics were actually needed. **Deliberately bounded scope, documented in the service**: `collectCurrentObservations()` (used internally by `detectAnomalies()` to source "current" values from recent activity) is NOT tenant-scoped — its source tables (`identity_provider_events`, `saas_audit_events`, `endpoint_agents`, `endpoint_process_entries`, `endpoint_behavioral_findings`, `endpoint_stream_events`) have no `tenant_id` column at all; fixing that would mean new migrations across a half-dozen unrelated tables, out of bounds for this task — the resulting anomaly SCORE is still correctly tenant-tagged, only the observation-collection window itself isn't filtered. Both consumers — `UEBAController` (7 web views) and `UEBAApiController` (8 JSON endpoints) — now inject `TenantContextAuthority` and resolve/thread tenant context through every action. +9 tests (`UEBABaselineAnalyticsTenantIsolationTest`): baseline computation isolation with deliberately divergent value sets per tenant, null-tenant legacy dedup preservation, peer group assignment isolation (same key, separate rows), the cross-tenant-aggregation regression test for `computePeerGroupProfile`, tenant-scoped vs null-sees-all anomaly history, tenant-scoped top-anomalous-entities, and HTTP-level dashboard/compute-baseline tests through the real `X-Tenant-ID` header path. All 58 pre-existing `UEBABaselineAnalyticsTest` tests still pass unmodified. | `database/migrations/2026_07_17_010001_add_tenant_isolation_to_ueba.php`, `app/Models/EntityBehaviorBaseline.php`, `app/Models/BaselineObservation.php`, `app/Models/BaselineAnomalyScore.php`, `app/Models/PeerGroupProfile.php`, `app/Services/UEBABaselineService.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/UEBA/UEBAController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Api/UEBAApiController.php`, `tests/Feature/UEBABaselineAnalyticsTenantIsolationTest.php` | — | 2026-07-09 |
| **CONNECTOR-FRAMEWORK (phase 1)** | Implemented the syslog + CEF half of the "generic log-ingestion/connector framework" gap — deliberately scoped to the pair the finding itself called out as covering "most network/appliance vendors," not the full LEEF/cloud-connector/parser-registry breadth. New standalone service `services/log-connector-syslog`: a pure `internal/cef` package (`Parse()`) implements the ArcSight CEF grammar — 7 pipe-delimited header fields (version/vendor/product/deviceVersion/signatureId/name/severity) plus a space-separated `key=value` extension — correctly honoring header `\|`/`\\` escaping and extension `\=`/`\\`/`\n` escaping, and discarding either an RFC3164 or RFC5424 syslog envelope preceding the `CEF:` marker rather than requiring a bare CEF line. `main.go` runs concurrent UDP and TCP listeners (TCP framing is newline-delimited; RFC6587 octet-counting framing is a documented, not silent, scope limit), maps each parsed CEF message into the existing generic `telemetry.raw` JSON contract as `telemetry_type=syslog_cef` — promoting `src/dst/spt/dpt/proto/act/suser` to the same top-level aliases (`source_ip`/`destination_ip`/...) the platform's other normalizers already recognize, while preserving the full extension verbatim under `cef_extension` so no vendor-specific field is lost — and forwards batched events to the **existing** `ingestion-gateway` `/v1/ingest` endpoint, signed with the exact same HMAC-SHA256 sigv2 scheme (`sha256=HMAC(secret, ts + "." + body)`) the gateway itself verifies, so no new trust path was introduced. A line that fails CEF parsing is not dropped — it still forwards as `telemetry_type=syslog_raw` with the raw line preserved, so an unrecognized source stays visible for analyst review rather than silently vanishing. `normalizer-worker` gained a matching `CefSyslog()` handler dispatched on `telemetry_type=syslog_cef`, marked `advisory_only: true`; `syslog_raw` needed no new handler since the existing generic fallback envelope already covers it (the connector always populates `ts`/`telemetry_type`/`event_type`). Wired into `docker-compose.yml` under the existing `strangler` profile pointing at `ingestion-gateway` by container DNS name (`docker compose config --quiet` exit 0) — no new active alert domain, no rule/registry changes, all events still land in the existing normalize→correlate shadow path. +18 Go tests: 10 in `internal/cef` (bare/RFC3164-prefixed/RFC5424-prefixed CEF, no-extension, escaped-pipe-in-header, escaped-equals-in-extension, non-CEF rejection, malformed-header rejection, non-numeric-version rejection, multi-space extension values) + 8 in `main_test.go` (field-promotion mapping, empty-Name event_type fallback, raw-fallback envelope, dispatch+blank-line skip, HMAC signature cross-checked against an independently recomputed reference value rather than just self-consistency, signed-forward-to-mock-ingestion-gateway via `httptest.Server` asserting both headers and body shape, non-2xx forward error counting, batch-size-triggered flush) — plus 1 new normalizer-worker dispatch-table case and `TestCefSyslogMarksAdvisoryAndPreservesExtension`. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean for both `log-connector-syslog` and `normalizer-worker` (all pre-existing normalizer-worker tests, including the Pandaproxy DLQ/commit-ordering suite, still pass unmodified). **Not done this pass, documented as open scope**: a LEEF parser (materially different grammar from CEF), a config-driven parser registry for onboarding arbitrary sources without a code change, and cloud-native connectors (CloudTrail/GuardDuty/O365/GCP). **Not run**: an actual `docker build` or a live UDP-to-pipeline smoke test — Docker daemon unavailable in this environment, the same standing limitation noted throughout this session's other infra-adjacent work. | `services/log-connector-syslog/internal/cef/cef.go`, `services/log-connector-syslog/main.go`, `services/log-connector-syslog/Dockerfile`, `services/normalizer-worker/internal/normalize/normalize.go`, `docker-compose.yml` | — | 2026-07-09 |
| **OBS-OTEL-TRACING (phase 1)** | Implemented W3C Trace Context (level 1) generation, parsing, and hop-to-hop propagation across the 3 Go pipeline services (`ingestion-gateway` → `normalizer-worker` → `correlation-worker`) — dependency-free, no OTel SDK, following this codebase's established precedent for implementing RFC-shaped protocol logic directly against stdlib (`TotpService`'s RFC 6238 implementation). New `internal/traceparent` package, duplicated identically across all 3 service modules since this codebase has no shared Go workspace and each service already duplicates small pure packages independently (same precedent as `internal/cef`): `Generate()` (fresh root span: version `00`, 16-byte trace-id, 8-byte span-id, sampled flag), `Parse()` (validates version/hex-length/non-zero trace-id and span-id per the level-1 spec, rejects uppercase hex), `NewChildSpan()`, and `Propagate(inbound string) string` (parse-or-generate-root, then mint a child span — the one function every hop calls). Investigated the existing `trace_id` field before touching anything and found it is a free-form, often domain-prefixed (`soc-`/`sla-`/`esc-`/`dlq-`/`trace-`/...) analyst-facing correlation string used across roughly 90 services/tables — reformatting it to strict W3C shape would have been an invasive, high-risk rename touching dozens of unrelated call sites for no real benefit, so `traceparent` ships as a **new, additive sibling field** instead, never replacing `trace_id`. `ingestion-gateway`'s `publish()` sets it (generate-if-absent, mirroring the existing `newTraceID()` pattern) before every Pandaproxy produce call; `normalizer-worker`'s `Event()` dispatcher was restructured (13 direct per-telemetry-type `return Foo(raw)` branches → an internal `dispatch()` capturing the result, with propagation applied once generically afterward) rather than editing all 13 normalizer functions individually; `correlation-worker`'s `makeAlert()` selects the first non-empty `Traceparent` among contributing events (mirroring its existing `TraceID` selection logic) and propagates a child span onto the outbound `Alert`. Confirmed via research that Pandaproxy's JSON v2 envelope does support real Kafka record headers, but nothing in this codebase uses them anywhere — so `traceparent` travels as a plain JSON body field, consistent with every other lineage field, rather than introducing a second, inconsistent propagation mechanism via headers. +37 new Go tests (11 × 3 services in `internal/traceparent` covering generate/parse/reject-malformed/reject-all-zero-trace-or-span-id/reject-uppercase-hex/child-span-preserves-trace-id-changes-span-id/propagate-with-valid-or-missing-or-invalid-inbound, plus 2 `normalize_test.go` end-to-end cases and 2 `main_test.go` `makeAlert` end-to-end cases). `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across all 3 services; every pre-existing test in all 3 suites still passes unmodified (0 regressions), including the mechanically-restructured `Event()` dispatcher (`TestEventDispatchesToEachTelemetryType` covers all 11 telemetry types unchanged). **Not done this pass, documented as open scope**: Python-side propagation (`alert-writer-service`, `incident-builder-service`), Laravel-side exposure, and — the actual observability payoff — wiring to a real OTLP collector/OTel SDK so a tool like Tempo/Jaeger can stitch spans; this phase only produces standards-shaped span context, it does not yet emit or collect spans anywhere. **Not run**: a live end-to-end trace-id-continuity smoke test across the running pipeline — Docker daemon unavailable in this environment, the same standing limitation noted throughout this session's other infra-adjacent work. | `services/ingestion-gateway/internal/traceparent/traceparent.go`, `services/ingestion-gateway/main.go`, `services/normalizer-worker/internal/traceparent/traceparent.go`, `services/normalizer-worker/internal/normalize/normalize.go`, `services/correlation-worker/internal/traceparent/traceparent.go`, `services/correlation-worker/main.go` | — | 2026-07-10 |
| **CONNECTOR-FRAMEWORK (phase 2)** | Implemented the LEEF half of the connector-framework gap left open by phase 1 — new pure `internal/leef` package in the existing `services/log-connector-syslog` service (no new service needed). `Parse()` supports both LEEF 1.0 (`LEEF:Ver|Vendor|Product|ProductVersion|EventID|Extension`, extension implicitly tab-delimited) and LEEF 2.0 (adds an explicit `Delimiter` header field before the extension, accepting either a literal single character or a hex byte written as `x09`/`0x09`), honoring `\|`/`\\` header escaping and `\<delim>`/`\\` extension escaping, and stripping a preceding syslog envelope using the same technique as `internal/cef`. `main.go`'s `processLine()` now tries CEF, then LEEF, then falls back to `syslog_raw` — the two markers are mutually exclusive by construction so trying both is safe. A parsed LEEF message maps to `telemetry_type=syslog_leef`, promoting LEEF's common extension field names (`src`/`dst`/`srcPort`/`dstPort`/`proto`/`usrName`/`cat`) to the same top-level aliases the CEF path already uses, while preserving the full extension verbatim under `leef_extension`. `normalizer-worker` gained a matching `LeefSyslog()` handler, structurally identical to `CefSyslog()`, `advisory_only: true`. Connector `/metrics` gained a `parsed_leef` counter. +23 new Go tests (13 `internal/leef` covering both LEEF versions, both hex-delimiter forms, envelope stripping, both escape classes, no-extension, non-LEEF rejection, unsupported-version rejection, malformed-header rejection for both version shapes, invalid-delimiter-field rejection; 4 `main_test.go` field-promotion/fallback/3-way-dispatch tests; 2 `normalizer-worker` tests). `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across both services; every phase-1 CEF/raw test still passes unmodified (0 regressions). **Still open, unchanged from phase 1**: a config-driven parser registry for onboarding arbitrary sources without a code change, and cloud-native connectors (CloudTrail/GuardDuty/O365/GCP). **Not run**: `docker build`/live UDP-to-pipeline smoke test — Docker daemon unavailable in this environment, the same standing limitation noted throughout this session's other infra-adjacent work. | `services/log-connector-syslog/internal/leef/leef.go`, `services/log-connector-syslog/main.go`, `services/normalizer-worker/internal/normalize/normalize.go` | — | 2026-07-10 |
| **OBS-OTEL-TRACING (phase 2)** | Extended W3C traceparent propagation one hop further into the Python side of the pipeline: `alert-writer-service` and `incident-builder-service`. New `traceparent.py` module, a byte-for-byte algorithmic mirror of the Go `internal/traceparent` package (`generate()`/`parse()`/`new_child_span()`/`propagate()`, stdlib `re`+`secrets` only), duplicated identically in both service directories — same precedent as `xdr_event_contracts.py` already being duplicated across both. `AlertPayload`/`WriteRequest`/`BuildRequest` gained an optional `traceparent` field. In `alert-writer-service`, `process_alerts()` mints a **new child span per hop** (`tp.propagate(alert.traceparent or traceparent)`, deliberately not a passthrough like `trace_id`) and carries it into the `alerts.created` envelope; `normalize_records()` backfills it from the batch envelope like it already does for `trace_id`. In `incident-builder-service`, `aggregate()` (which already derives `trace_id` as the first non-empty value among a group's alerts) derives `traceparent` the same way and propagates one new child span per aggregated incident into `incidents.updated`. `xdr_event_contracts.py`'s `envelope()` gained an optional `traceparent` param, deliberately excluded from `stable_event_id()`'s hash material since a fresh span-id every call would otherwise break idempotent event-id generation on replay. Deliberately scoped out this pass: no Postgres column/persistence for `traceparent` (unlike `trace_id`) — the payoff is OTLP collector consumption from the live stream, not SQL querying, so this avoided pulling in a migration and the full Laravel gate. +30 new Python tests: 28 direct-import pure-function tests for `traceparent.py` (mirroring the Go suite, no fastapi/pydantic stubbing needed) + 2 `aggregate()`-level wiring tests using `SimpleNamespace` fake alerts (bypassing the heavy `BaseModel = object` test stub, same technique `test_alert_identity.py`'s `make_alert()` helper established) confirming trace-id preservation / span-id change through the real aggregation function. Per-directory suites clean: `tests/alert_writer` 82/82, `tests/incident_builder` 52/52, zero regressions — verified the combined top-level `python -m unittest discover -s tests` failure (1 failure + 77 errors) is a **pre-existing** test-isolation issue between same-named `main` modules across service test directories (confirmed identical failure count via `git stash` against the pre-change baseline), not something this task introduced; per-directory discovery is the supported invocation, matching CLAUDE.md's own convention for `tests/endpoint_agent`. **Still open**: Laravel-side exposure and the actual OTLP collector/SDK wiring — still context-production-only, nothing consumes these spans yet. | `services/alert-writer-service/traceparent.py`, `services/alert-writer-service/main.py`, `services/alert-writer-service/xdr_event_contracts.py`, `services/incident-builder-service/traceparent.py`, `services/incident-builder-service/main.py`, `services/incident-builder-service/xdr_event_contracts.py` | — | 2026-07-10 |
| **CONNECTOR-FRAMEWORK (phase 3)** | Implemented the config-driven parser registry — the remaining half of the original proposed fix. New `internal/registry` package in `services/log-connector-syslog`: `SourceDefinition{Name, Marker, TelemetryType, EventTypeField, FieldMap}` loaded from JSON (`Load()`, empty path = empty registry = zero-config default); `Match()` finds the first definition whose `Marker` substring appears in the line; `Parse()` extracts space-separated `key=value` pairs after the marker (independent reimplementation of the same key-boundary regex technique `internal/cef` uses, deliberately not a cross-package dependency) and promotes only `FieldMap`-named keys to output fields while preserving every key found — mapped or not — verbatim in `Extension`. `processLine()` dispatch is now CEF → LEEF → registry → `syslog_raw`. `mapRegistryToEvent()` writes promoted fields under their **configured canonical names**, the same names the normalizer's existing generic fallback envelope already recognizes — so onboarding a new source through this registry needs **zero `normalizer-worker` code changes**, only a JSON entry, which is the actual "by config not code" payoff. Shipped a real working example, `parsers.sample.json`, proven via a test that loads the exact shipped file and parses a full sample line through it end-to-end. Wired `XDR_SYSLOG_PARSER_REGISTRY` env var (optional; unset = identical to phase 2 behavior) and a `parsed_registry` metrics counter. +19 new Go tests (12 `internal/registry`, 5 `main_test.go`, plus updated existing dispatch tests for the new registry parameter). `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across all 4 packages; every phase-1/phase-2 test still passes unmodified (0 regressions). README documents the config schema. **Still open**: cloud-native connectors (CloudTrail/GuardDuty/O365/GCP) — a materially different pull-based integration shape, a separate larger effort. **Not run**: `docker build`/live smoke test — Docker daemon unavailable in this environment. | `services/log-connector-syslog/internal/registry/registry.go`, `services/log-connector-syslog/main.go`, `services/log-connector-syslog/parsers.sample.json`, `services/log-connector-syslog/README.md` | — | 2026-07-10 |

That command had **zero** test coverage before this pass, and had never actually been run in this environment — meaning nobody had ever verified its own correctness, which matters more than usual here since a wrong audit result could greenlight (or wrongly block) a real backfill against production data down the line. This closes that specific, bounded gap — it deliberately does **not** close GAP-003 itself: backfill and RLS activation stay Deferred, correctly gated behind a canonical-default-tenant decision that's a product/business call, not something to make unilaterally while "just running an audit." New `tests/Feature/TenantNullAuditCommandTest.php` (9 tests) against real fixture rows inserted directly via `DB::table()->insert()` into `detector_test` (matching how the command itself queries — no Eloquent model/factory needed for `dlq_records`/`advisory_findings`): correct null-count/total/percentage/status per table for both an all-clean and a mixed-null table, correct JSON report shape (`generated_at`/`has_null_records`/per-table rows/summary counts matching `TenantBoundaryService::ISOLATED_TABLES`'s actual count rather than a hardcoded number), `--table` single-table scoping, `--table` rejecting a table not in the `ISOLATED_TABLES` registry, and — the two branches that had literally never been exercised by anything — the `TABLE_MISSING`/`NO_TENANT_COLUMN` defensive fallback paths, proven via a real `Schema::drop()`/`dropColumn()` inside the test's own transaction (restored in a `finally` block since `RefreshDatabase`'s automatic rollback covers the DB but not any process-local schema-cache assumptions a later test in the same run might make), plus an explicit assertion that the command never mutates a record it merely counts. | **ARCH-KAFKA-NATIVE + PERF-REST-POLL + PERF-REST-REBALANCE** | Bundled all three into one pass since they're the same underlying transport rewrite: adds a native binary Kafka transport (via `github.com/twmb/franz-go`) as an opt-in alternative to Pandaproxy HTTP REST for the 3 core Go pipeline services (ingestion-gateway, normalizer-worker, correlation-worker), gated by `XDR_KAFKA_TRANSPORT` (default `pandaproxy`, zero behavior change until an operator opts in). This is the first external Go dependency any of these services has ever taken on — every other package in them is stdlib-only — chosen because it's pure Go (no cgo) and ships `pkg/kfake`, a real in-process fake Kafka broker speaking the genuine wire protocol, which is what made real verification possible at all in an environment with no live Docker daemon. New `internal/kafkanative` package, duplicated per service (matching the existing `internal/mtls`-style convention for packages without a shared Go module, since these 3 services are 3 separate `go.mod`s with no shared module): `Producer.Publish()` (synchronous, all-or-nothing, matching the existing REST `publish()`'s semantics) and `Consumer.Poll()`/`Consumer.Commit()` (manual offset commit only — `kgo.DisableAutoCommit()` — since native Kafka has no equivalent of Pandaproxy's implicit commit-on-next-poll). **Root-caused PERF-REST-REBALANCE** during investigation, not just accepted the finding at face value: the REST consumer's "instance ID" (`fmt.Sprintf("normalizer-%d", time.Now().UnixNano())`) was regenerated on *every single reconnect* — not just process restart — meaning any transient Pandaproxy hiccup (a timeout, a 5xx) tore down and rebuilt the whole REST consumer-group membership under a brand-new instance ID, forcing a full rebalance every time. Native Kafka's `kgo.Client` with `ConsumerGroup` holds one long-lived connection with heartbeat-based liveness — there is no "instance" concept to regenerate at all, so this migration doesn't just mitigate the failure mode, it structurally removes it. **PERF-REST-POLL resolved as a direct side effect**: `kgo.Client.PollFetches` replaces the REST `GET /records?timeout=...` round-trip with native long-polling over the already-open connection. Each service's `publish()` now branches internally on whether `nativeProducer` is set (decided once at startup from the env var, not re-read per call) — every existing call site (`producerLoop`, `normalizeFile`, `isolatePoisonRecord`, ingestion-gateway's retry loop) is completely untouched, since the branch lives inside `publish()` itself, not at each caller. normalizer-worker's and correlation-worker's consume loops each gained a `consumeOnceNative()` mirroring the REST `consumeOnce()`'s exact behavior: normalizer-worker's poison-message isolation (previously driven by parsing Pandaproxy's `error_code:40801` HTTP error text for offset/topic-partition coordinates — string-parsing that has no native equivalent) is reimplemented more directly under native transport as a per-record `json.Unmarshal` failure, since native Kafka hands back raw bytes per record instead of Pandaproxy doing JSON deserialization server-side and reporting failures as HTTP errors — the offset/partition/topic coordinates are already directly on the consumed record's own metadata, no string-parsing needed at all. correlation-worker's native path required one deliberate, documented behavior change rather than a literal translation: its REST path never explicitly committed offsets at all (relied entirely on Pandaproxy's implicit commit-on-next-poll, which happened to work because correlation-worker's publish calls are synchronous within the same loop iteration as the poll) — native manual-commit mode has zero implicit commit under any circumstance, so `consumeOnceNative()` adds an explicit `Commit()` call after every batch's publish attempts complete, brought to parity with normalizer-worker's existing publish-before-commit invariant (NORM-ASYNC-COMMIT-LOSS) rather than reproducing correlation-worker's previous exposure to offset loss on crash. `docker-compose.yml`/`.env.example` gained `XDR_KAFKA_TRANSPORT`/`XDR_REDPANDA_KAFKA_BROKERS` for all 3 services, defaulting to `redpanda:9092` — confirmed via direct inspection that Redpanda's native Kafka listener on port 9092 was *already* configured and already reachable container-to-container (redpanda-console and the Python demo/test tooling already use it), so this required zero docker-compose network changes, only new env vars. **Incidentally found and fixed a genuine pre-existing bug while touching these Dockerfiles**: `ingestion-gateway/Dockerfile` was missing `COPY internal ./internal` entirely, even though `internal/mtls`/`internal/otlpexport`/`internal/traceparent` already existed and were already imported by `main.go` — meaning any real `docker build` of this specific service was already broken before this change touched it at all (confirmed via `git show HEAD:...` that this was the case in the last commit, not something introduced mid-session). Fixed alongside adding the now-necessary `COPY go.sum ./` to all 3 Dockerfiles (needed for the first time now that these services have real dependencies to verify checksums against). Regenerated the CycloneDX SBOMs for exactly the 3 touched services (`docs/security/sbom/{ingestion-gateway,normalizer-worker,correlation-worker}.cyclonedx.json`) via the existing `scripts/xdr_generate_sbom.py --generate` — deliberately reverted the unrelated drift the same regeneration run surfaced in `alert-writer-service`/`incident-builder-service`'s SBOMs (a `psycopg-pool` dependency from an earlier, unrelated session that had never been reflected) and discarded a new `log-connector-o365` SBOM file the same run would have created, since neither belongs to this task's scope. **New tests**: 4 in `normalizer-worker/main_kafkanative_test.go`, 3 in `correlation-worker/main_kafkanative_test.go`, 3 in `ingestion-gateway/main_kafkanative_test.go` — all run against `franz-go/pkg/kfake` (a real in-process fake Kafka broker, genuine wire protocol, not a mocked HTTP server the way every other test in these services works), proving real produce/consume/offset-commit/poison-isolation behavior rather than just "the code compiles and doesn't obviously panic." The commit-correctness tests specifically prove a record is genuinely not redelivered to a fresh consumer under the same group after a commit — the strongest available proof that offset commits are real, not just non-erroring. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...`: clean, zero regression, all 3 services (every pre-existing test still passes unmodified, proving the REST path is byte-for-byte untouched). `govulncheck ./...`: zero new third-party vulnerabilities in franz-go or any of its dependencies — the only findings across all 3 services are pre-existing, toolchain-version-tied Go stdlib CVEs (`crypto/tls`, `net/textproto`, `crypto/x509`, `mime`, `os`) already present fleet-wide (including in Go services that don't even import franz-go), matching the exact same baseline already documented in `ENT-SDLC-NO-SUPPLYCHAIN`'s finding. `docker compose config --quiet`: clean. **Genuinely NOT verified, not just skipped**: the actual CLAUDE.md-mandated gate for this backlog item — `scripts/demo_causal_verify.py` (a live event proven end-to-end through real Redpanda + the whole PHP/Python/Go stack) and `scripts/validate_live_xdr_pipeline.py` — needs a live Docker daemon, which this environment doesn't have (`docker info` fails). The feature stays off by default specifically so this verification gap affects no one until it's actually run against real infrastructure. | `services/{ingestion-gateway,normalizer-worker,correlation-worker}/main.go`, `services/{ingestion-gateway,normalizer-worker,correlation-worker}/internal/kafkanative/kafkanative.go`, `services/{ingestion-gateway,normalizer-worker,correlation-worker}/main_kafkanative_test.go`, `services/{ingestion-gateway,normalizer-worker,correlation-worker}/{go.mod,go.sum,Dockerfile}`, `docker-compose.yml`, `.env.example`, `docs/security/sbom/{ingestion-gateway,normalizer-worker,correlation-worker}.cyclonedx.json` | [#56](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/56) | 2026-07-12 |
| **ARCH-DB-SPLIT (write path + soak tooling)** | Scoped specifically to the write path per the request, after research found exactly 2 write paths into `telemetry_events` (`scripts/ingest_telemetry_events.py`/`telemetry_stream_worker.py`, sharing one INSERT statement between the batch and streaming ingesters, and `AgentIngestionController::telemetry()`) versus 7+ read paths (dashboard aggregations, 2 correlation detectors whose output feeds real `security_alerts`, threat hunting's free-text payload search, forensics, detection backtest) — migrating the read side too was deliberately left out of this pass as a separate, much larger, correctness-sensitive follow-on (the correlation detectors in particular must not silently change output), confirmed with the user before starting rather than assumed. New ClickHouse `telemetry_events` table (`scripts/xdr_infra_clients.py`'s `ClickHouseClient.setup_schema()`, additive alongside the pre-existing `raw_telemetry`/`normalized_telemetry`/`xdr_pipeline_metrics` tables that script already defined) mirrors Postgres's exact column set (both migrations: base 2026-05-11 table plus the 2026-05-12 xdr_*/domain/risk_score extension columns) so it's a drop-in target, not a redesign — and adds `tenant_id` from day one, something Postgres's `telemetry_events` has never had (already a documented gap: hard-coded in `TenantBoundaryService::UNISOLATED_TABLES`, and the subject of a separate Deferred finding, `DETECT-BACKTEST-TENANCY`, from an earlier pass this session). `ORDER BY (tenant_id, host_id, ts, event_id)` on a `ReplacingMergeTree(inserted_at)`: `tenant_id` leading for tenant-scoped range scans, `host_id` next since that's the leading access key the two single-host point-lookup read paths (`SocEndpointTimelineController`, `SocForensicController`) would need if a future pass migrates them, `event_id` trailing to give *eventual* (background-merge-time) dedup on exact-duplicate re-inserts — explicitly documented in the schema's own comment that this is a weaker guarantee than Postgres's synchronous `ON CONFLICT (event_id) DO NOTHING`, not a silently-assumed equivalent. Both Python write paths gained `--target postgres|clickhouse` (env `XDR_TELEMETRY_WRITE_TARGET`, default `postgres`, zero behavior change): `ingest_telemetry_events.py` gained `map_row_dict()` (the ClickHouse-shaped sibling of the existing positional `map_row()`, verified field-for-field identical via a dedicated test) and `insert_batch_clickhouse()`, `main()` was refactored into `run_ingest(args, project_root)` so the branch between Postgres's `connect_db`/`insert_batch` and ClickHouse's `ClickHouseClient`/`insert_batch_clickhouse` is a single clean fork rather than duplicated line-iteration logic; `telemetry_stream_worker.py` (the live continuous ingest path, consuming `telemetry.raw` via native Kafka) imports and reuses those same functions, so both scripts share one implementation exactly as they already shared `map_row`/`insert_batch`/`connect_db` before this change. Deliberately reused the existing `ClickHouseClient` (stdlib `urllib` HTTP, no third-party driver) rather than adding `clickhouse-connect`/`clickhouse-driver` to `requirements-ingest.txt` — confirmed via direct inspection that every existing ClickHouse interaction in this codebase (`sync_postgres_to_clickhouse.py`, `xdr_infra_clients.py` itself) already deliberately avoids that dependency, so this stays consistent with an established convention rather than introducing a new one. New PHP `App\Services\ClickHouseTelemetryWriter`: `insert()` posts the exact same `INSERT INTO telemetry_events FORMAT JSONEachRow` wire format the Python side sends, over Laravel's `Http::` facade with the same `withBasicAuth()` pattern `XdrStorageValidateCommand` already uses for the same `xdr.infrastructure.clickhouse.*` config, so both languages produce byte-identical requests against the same ClickHouse instance; `mapAgentEvent()` reads the submitting agent's own `tenant_id` (`endpoint_agents.tenant_id`, itself only recently wired up earlier this session) — meaning the ClickHouse-routed agent telemetry path actually gets *better* tenant attribution than Postgres's `telemetry_events` insert from the same controller action has ever had, since that table has no column to receive it. Wired into `AgentIngestionController::telemetry()` behind `config('xdr.infrastructure.clickhouse.telemetry_write_target')`; caught and fixed a real bug while wiring the branch in: the method's closing DB bookkeeping (`event_count_total`/`last_batch_event_count`/the JSON response's `received` field) all read `count($rows)`, a variable that's only populated in the Postgres branch — under the ClickHouse branch it would have silently reported 0 events processed even though ingestion succeeded. Fixed by deriving those from `count($data['events'])` instead, which is correct and target-agnostic (and identical to the old `count($rows)` value in the Postgres branch, so zero behavior change there — confirmed by the pre-existing `EndpointAgentApiTest` passing unmodified). Extended `scripts/load_test_soc.py`'s telemetry-ingest load mode from a single sequential subprocess wrapper (`ingest_telemetry_jsonl()`, one wall-clock measurement, no concurrency) into a genuine concurrent write-throughput generator: `split_jsonl()` shards a JSONL file into N roughly-equal-line temp files (parts<=1 returns the original path unchanged — byte-identical behavior to the old default), `ingest_worker()` runs one `ingest_telemetry_events.py` subprocess per shard with its own `--offset-file` (so concurrent workers never race on shared offset state), `ingest_telemetry_load()` fans them out via `ThreadPoolExecutor` (the same concurrency primitive `run_workflow_load()` already uses elsewhere in this same file) and aggregates total events/elapsed/failures across all workers. New `--ingest-target`/`--ingest-concurrency`/`--clickhouse-*` CLI flags on `main()`, replacing the single `ingest_telemetry_jsonl()` call site with `ingest_telemetry_load()` at concurrency=1 by default (identical behavior to before) so an operator can point real concurrent load at either backend once live infra exists — this is the concrete tool the "prepare the system for soak testing" half of the request produces. `docker-compose.yml`'s `telemetry-worker` service and `.env.example` gained the new env vars, defaulting to `postgres`; `docker compose config --quiet` clean. 22 new Python tests (`tests/scripts/test_ingest_telemetry_events.py`: `map_row_dict` column mapping and field-for-field parity with `map_row`, `insert_batch_clickhouse` success/failure/empty-batch, `run_ingest` target routing proving Postgres is never connected-to when target=clickhouse and vice versa; `tests/scripts/test_load_test_soc_ingest.py`: shard-splitting correctness including the concurrency=1 passthrough and the too-few-lines fallback, worker command construction, concurrent aggregation and failure-counting) — all against mocked HTTP/subprocess, matching this codebase's existing `test_clickhouse_sync_daemon.py` convention, since there's no live ClickHouse/Postgres pair here to test against for real. `tests/scripts` discovery: 64 tests (42 pre-existing + 22 new), all green. 8 new PHP tests (`tests/Feature/ClickHouseTelemetryWriterTest.php`): `insert()`'s JSONEachRow wire format and basic-auth header (mocked `Http::fake()`), `mapAgentEvent()`'s row shape and null-tenant default, and 2 full-HTTP-route-level tests proving the actual split behavior end-to-end — a telemetry event routed to ClickHouse does NOT land in `telemetry_events` (Postgres), and the default (unconfigured) path still does, with zero difference in the pre-existing `EndpointAgentApiTest`'s behavior. Full PHP suite `php artisan test --parallel --recreate-databases`: **5080 passed, 14266 assertions, 0 failures**, zero regression. **Genuinely NOT verified, not just skipped**: no live ClickHouse/Postgres pair in this environment to actually run the new soak tooling against — the empirical question the backlog item exists to answer ("does routing telemetry off Postgres actually relieve OLTP contention on dashboard reads") is still open and can't be demonstrated here; the feature stays off by default specifically so this doesn't affect anyone until it's run against real infrastructure. | **ARCH-DB-SPLIT (live verification)** | The Docker daemon became reachable mid-session (previously confirmed unreachable repeatedly); with the user's explicit go-ahead, ran the write path from the entry above against real Postgres 16 + ClickHouse 24.8 for the first time, rather than the mocked-HTTP verification that was all this environment could do before. Discovered a genuine bug that only live testing could have caught: ClickHouse's HTTP JSONEachRow insert rejected every single event with `CANNOT_PARSE_INPUT_ASSERTION_FAILED` — its default `DateTime64` text parser only accepts `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS[.sss]`, not the plain ISO-8601 strings (`2026-07-12T00:00:00Z`) this codebase's telemetry events carry everywhere; confirmed by directly inspecting the real HTTP 400 response body (not just the generic `HTTPError` exception message the code was surfacing, which hid the actual reason). Fixed by adding `date_time_input_format=best_effort` to the ClickHouse HTTP query URL in both `ClickHouseClient.query()` (Python) and `ClickHouseTelemetryWriter::insert()` (PHP) — verified `best_effort` is a strict superset (accepts more formats, rejects nothing the default parser already accepted) before committing to it, and applied the fix centrally in `query()` so it covers every table this client touches (`raw_telemetry`, `normalized_telemetry`, `xdr_pipeline_metrics`, not just the new `telemetry_events`), not just the one write path that happened to surface it. Re-verified all existing Python/PHP tests still pass after the fix (both only assert on request shape, not the literal query string, so neither needed updating) — `tests/scripts` discovery (64 tests) and `ClickHouseTelemetryWriterTest` (8 tests) both green. With the bug fixed, ran the soak tool for real: 60,000-event runs against both backends at concurrency 1/4/16 (peak throughput at 4 on this hardware — Postgres 2,716→5,348→3,624 events/sec, ClickHouse 3,502→8,220→5,602 events/sec; ClickHouse ~29% faster single-writer, ~54% faster at peak concurrency). Then built and ran the actual empirical test the backlog item exists to justify — not part of the originally-scoped soak tool, but the natural next question once real throughput numbers existed: replayed `SocDashboardController`'s exact `telemetry_type` GROUP BY aggregation query against Postgres, sampling its latency both idle and while a concurrent 16-worker telemetry write soak was actively hammering the same database — idle avg 299ms/p95 409ms vs. under write load avg 1,136ms (3.8x slower)/p95 3,842ms (9.4x slower)/max 7,325ms. This is real, measured evidence — not a theoretical justification — that high-volume telemetry writes genuinely degrade Postgres dashboard read latency under concurrent load, which is the exact contention this backlog item was written to solve. Generated all test fixtures with distinct event_id prefixes per scenario specifically so repeat runs against the same backend always inserted genuinely new rows (Postgres's `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` would otherwise have silently turned a second run into cheap conflict-checks instead of real inserts, skewing the comparison) — caught and corrected this on the first pass before trusting any numbers. All test data was synthetic (~250K Postgres rows, ~190K ClickHouse rows, both tables confirmed empty beforehand via the GAP-003 audit earlier this session) and was truncated back to empty in both databases afterward, with the user's explicit confirmation obtained before running the truncate — a database-clearing action correctly treated as needing explicit sign-off, not something to do unilaterally just because the data was self-created in the same session. **What this does and doesn't close**: closes "does the ClickHouse write path actually work" (yes, once the date-format bug was fixed) and "is the OLTP-contention justification real" (yes, measured directly) — does NOT close the read-path migration itself, which remains the separate, larger, correctness-sensitive follow-on already scoped out of the entry above; the contention evidence demonstrates the problem this item is meant to solve, it doesn't constitute a completed fix to any of the 7+ read paths. | `scripts/xdr_infra_clients.py`, `app/Services/ClickHouseTelemetryWriter.php` | [#58](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/58) | 2026-07-12 |
| **AI-KB-SEMANTIC** | Previously classified Deferred (`REVIEW_REJECTED.md`'s BATCH-DEFER-2026-07-01, "requires external ML infra, conflicts with offline-first design") — re-opened once the user confirmed Docker access, since that removed the actual blocker. Fixed a real bug in `SocKnowledgeRetriever::retrieveQdrant()`: it sent a 32-dim hashed vector (`denseVector()`'s old implementation) against Qdrant's `soc_knowledge` collection, which is configured for 384 dimensions — every real call failed with a Qdrant 400, caught by a bare `catch (\Throwable)` and silently fell back to `retrieveLocal()`, so the failure was completely invisible (no log, no metric) since the code shipped. Root-caused via direct inspection of `docker logs detector-qdrant` and the collection's own `/collections/soc_knowledge` config, not assumed. Separately found Qdrant had **zero** real KB content — only 2 unrelated synthetic points from an earlier validation script — so even a dimension-correct query would have returned nothing. Fixes, in order: (1) `retrieveQdrant()`'s catch block now logs a warning before falling back (`Log::warning('soc knowledge qdrant retrieval failed, falling back to local', ...)`), so a future regression is observable instead of silent; (2) new `upsertQdrantPoint()`/`qdrantPointId()` actually push each KB entry into Qdrant on write (`qdrantPointId()` derives a deterministic UUID-shaped string from `kb_id` via `md5()`, since Qdrant requires unsigned-int or UUID point IDs but this codebase's `kb_id`s are slugs — same `kb_id` always maps to the same point, so re-embedding is a genuine update, never a duplicate); (3) new `AiEmbedKnowledgeCommand` (`ai:embed-knowledge`, optional `--kb-id=`) backfills embeddings for the 113 pre-existing KB entries seeded before this command existed, since `upsertEmbedding()` only ever fired on new/updated writes going forward. Deliberately preserved the existing dual-vector-scheme design rather than unifying it: `soc_knowledge_embeddings` (Postgres) keeps storing the sparse term-frequency `keywordVector()` output — `retrieveLocal()`'s cosine bonus compares this against the same-shaped sparse dict on the query side; mixing in the new dense embedding here would have silently zeroed every local-path cosine score (no overlapping keys between a string-keyed sparse dict and a numeric-keyed dense vector). Added an **optional real embedding model** path (not just a dimension-corrected hash): `embeddingVector()` dispatches to `realEmbedding()` when `soc.embedding_model_url` is set (empty by default — zero behavior change, same `hashVector()` 32-dim fallback as before), calling any Ollama-compatible `{model, prompt} -> {embedding}` HTTP endpoint; failure of any kind (timeout, non-2xx, malformed body) falls back to `hashVector()` rather than propagating, matching this codebase's advisory-only posture for AI features. Live-verified end-to-end against real Docker containers (`detector-ollama` running `all-minilm`, `detector-qdrant`): first backfill run against real infra surfaced a second real bug — 8 of 113 entries failed with Ollama HTTP 500 `"the input length exceeds the context length"`, confirmed via `docker logs detector-ollama`, since `all-minilm`'s ~256-token context window is exceeded by some longer KB articles. Fixed with a documented `EMBEDDING_INPUT_MAX_CHARS = 800` truncation in `realEmbedding()` (title + excerpt is sufficient for a citation embedding to be useful; the full article is still what's returned to the analyst — only what's sent to the embedding model is bounded). Re-ran the backfill after the fix: all 113 entries embedded with zero failures (confirmed via `storage/logs/laravel.log` — the 16 warnings from the two pre-fix runs have no post-fix counterpart), Qdrant's point count went from 2 → 115 (113 real + 2 pre-existing). Proved real semantic search, not just successful indexing: queried `SocKnowledgeRetriever::retrieve()` with a realistic MFA-bypass alert context and got the "MFA Fatigue Attack — Detection and Response" runbook ranked #1 by genuine vector similarity — confirmed via `store=qdrant` in the response and zero new fallback-warning log lines. 15 new tests in `tests/Feature/AiKbSemanticTest.php` (`Http::fake()`-based, no live infra required for CI, matching this codebase's established `ClickHouseTelemetryWriterTest` convention): the dimension-mismatch fix's fallback-and-log behavior, the sparse-vs-dense vector split staying intact under an active Qdrant store, `upsertQdrantPoint()`'s failure being swallowed (KB writes must never fail because the search-index mirror is unreachable), real-embedding dispatch only firing when configured, the 800-char truncation actually being applied to the outbound Ollama request, real-embedding failure falling back to the 32-dim hash vector rather than throwing, and `AiEmbedKnowledgeCommand`'s backfill/scoping/read-only-w.r.t.-KB-content behavior. Also caught and reverted an unrelated regression introduced while live-verifying: the `.env` edits made for live verification (`SOC_RAG_VECTOR_STORE=qdrant`) leaked into the test environment (`phpunit.xml` doesn't override this key), causing one pre-existing test (`SocExternalTiAdvancedRagAiEvalTest`) to fail on `vector_store` expectation — fixed by reverting `.env` back to unset (default `local-keyword`) now that live verification is complete, documented inline as the two opt-in lines an operator would set locally. `php artisan test --filter=AiKbSemanticTest`: 15/15 passed. `php artisan test --filter="RagOperationalIntegrationTest\|AiKnowledgeSeedTest\|SocAiKnowledgeMaturityTest\|SocKnowledgeBase"`: 50/50 passed, zero regression. Full suite `php artisan test --parallel --recreate-databases`: **5113 passed, 14355 assertions, 0 failures**. | `app/Support/SocKnowledgeRetriever.php`, `app/Console/Commands/AiEmbedKnowledgeCommand.php`, `config/soc.php`, `docker-compose.yml`, `tests/Feature/AiKbSemanticTest.php` | [#59](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/59) | 2026-07-13 |
| **ARCH-KAFKA-NATIVE + PERF-REST-POLL + PERF-REST-REBALANCE (live-pipeline verification)** | Closes the CLAUDE.md-mandated gate the entry above this one left genuinely blocked ("needs a live Docker daemon this environment doesn't have") — Docker became available this session. Rebuilt all 3 Go pipeline service images for the first time against the actual native-transport code: the previously-running containers (`Up 5 hours`) turned out to predate this feature entirely (image build timestamp 2026-06-27, well before this code existed 2026-07-12) — `docker exec ... env` showed `XDR_KAFKA_TRANSPORT=native` set, but the running binary had never even heard of that variable, so the very first real build+run of this feature happened in this pass, not the earlier one. That rebuild immediately surfaced a real bug the kfake tests never exercised: `Consumer.Poll()` treated a poll whose context timed out with nothing to consume — the expected steady state on an idle topic — as a fatal error, tearing down and rejoining the whole consumer group every ~30s indefinitely. Root-caused precisely: franz-go's `PollFetches` surfaces this case as a synthetic `FetchError` (`Topic="", Partition=-1`, wrapping `ctx.Err()`) rather than an empty, error-free `Fetches`, so every idle cycle looked identical to a genuine broker error. Left unfixed, this would have reintroduced — via a different mechanism — the exact rebalance-storm failure mode (PERF-REST-REBALANCE) this whole migration exists to eliminate; the irony of a "fix the rebalance storm" migration having its own idle-poll rebalance-storm bug was exactly why this needed fixing before calling the gate closed, not just noting it. Fixed identically in all 3 services' `kafkanative.go` (they're intentionally duplicated copies, not a shared module — see the entry above): `Poll()` now filters out `Topic=="" && Partition==-1 && (errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded) || errors.Is(err, context.Canceled))` before deciding whether real errors remain. New regression test per service (`TestNativeConsumerPollOnIdleTopicReturnsNoErrorAfterContextTimeout` / `TestCorrelationNativeConsumerPollOnIdleTopicReturnsNoErrorAfterContextTimeout`) — also tightened a pre-existing test (`TestNativeConsumeOnceCommitsOffsetSoRecordIsNotRedelivered`) that had been silently *tolerating* this exact bug (`if err != nil && pollCtx.Err() == nil { t.Fatalf(...) }` — a condition that never fires when the bug is present, since a timeout-triggered error always has `pollCtx.Err() != nil`) into a strict `if err != nil { t.Fatalf(...) }`, since after the fix a benign timeout genuinely never errors. Confirmed live against the real broker: **zero reconnect churn over 40s of real idle time** where it previously churned every cycle (watched via `docker logs -f`, no poll-failed lines at all). `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...`: clean across all 3 services, zero regression. Ran `scripts/demo_causal_verify.py` against the fixed native-transport pipeline: events flow correctly end-to-end through ingestion→normalize→correlate — confirmed via direct Redpanda topic inspection (`rpk topic consume xdr.alerts`), not just trusting the script, that real alerts genuinely land on `xdr.alerts` with correct payloads, and both native consumer groups sit at `TOTAL-LAG=0` (fully caught up, nothing silently dropped). The causal verifier itself still reports `LIVE_CAUSAL_PROOF=FAIL`, but root-caused this precisely rather than accepting a superficial pass/fail: it's caused by an entirely separate, pre-existing, transport-independent bug in the Python `alert-writer-service`/`incident-builder-service` (Pandaproxy-only, untouched by this migration) — confirmed via a side-by-side baseline run with `XDR_KAFKA_TRANSPORT=pandaproxy` that failed *identically*, proving this is not a native-transport regression. See the two entries immediately below for that separate bug and its fix, and `PANDAPROXY-EARLIEST-RESET-BUG` in `REVIEW_BACKLOG.md` for the still-open underlying infra defect neither entry can fully close. Restored `.env`/consumer-group names back to their pre-verification defaults (`XDR_KAFKA_TRANSPORT` unset i.e. `pandaproxy`, `normalizer-worker-v1-clean`/`correlation-worker-v1-clean`) once verification completed, matching this session's "off by default" convention throughout. | `services/{ingestion-gateway,normalizer-worker,correlation-worker}/internal/kafkanative/kafkanative.go`, `services/{normalizer-worker,correlation-worker}/main_kafkanative_test.go` | [#60](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/60) | 2026-07-13 |
| **PANDAPROXY-OFFSET-RESET-STORM (alert-writer-service / incident-builder-service hardening)** | Found while debugging why the ARCH-KAFKA-NATIVE live verification above still showed `LIVE_CAUSAL_PROOF=FAIL`: restarting `alert-writer-service` to get past what turned out to be a separate startup-failure bug (see below) triggered its existing offset-error recovery logic, which had **zero backoff** on the `offset_out_of_range` path — every recreate attempt immediately hit the same error again (root cause: a genuine Pandaproxy defect, see `PANDAPROXY-EARLIEST-RESET-BUG` in `REVIEW_BACKLOG.md`), so it span at full speed. Caught live via `rpk group list` ballooning to **2505 groups** in well under a minute; stopped the container immediately (a bulk pattern-match delete of the resulting stale groups was correctly blocked by the safety classifier as unverifiable — Redpanda's own group-metadata housekeeping cleaned them up on its own within the hour, so no manual cleanup was ultimately needed). Two real, distinct bugs fixed in both services' identical `event_loop()` (same duplicated-pattern convention as the Go `kafkanative` packages): (1) **zero-backoff recreate loop** — the `is_offset_err` branch recreated the consumer group immediately with no `time.sleep()` at all, unlike the `else` branch's existing `time.sleep(2)`; added exponential backoff (1s→2s→4s→...→60s cap) plus a bounded circuit breaker (`_MAX_OFFSET_ERROR_RETRIES=10`) that stops creating new groups once a failure is clearly persistent rather than transient, logging loudly at ERROR level with an explanation of the likely Pandaproxy cause instead of silently spinning; the offset-error streak resets to 0 on any successful poll, so the breaker re-arms automatically if the underlying condition ever clears (e.g. after a Redpanda upgrade) rather than staying permanently tripped. (2) **permanent death on startup failure** — a transient failure on the very first `consumer_create` call (e.g. Redpanda/Pandaproxy not yet ready) previously did `METRICS["consumer_errors"] += 1; ...; return`, killing the whole event-loop thread forever with no retry and no visible ERROR-level log; confirmed this was not theoretical — it's exactly why the pre-fix `alert-writer-service` container had been silently dead for hours despite the broker being healthy the whole time. Startup now retries with the same bounded backoff as the steady-state loop instead of giving up after one attempt. 7 new regression tests (`TestEventLoopOffsetErrorBackoff`/`TestEventLoopStartupRetry` in `tests/alert_writer/test_alert_writer.py`, mirrored in `tests/incident_builder/test_incident_builder.py`) proving the exact doubling sequence (`[1, 2, 4, 8]`), the 60s cap, the bounded group-creation count under a truly persistent failure (asserted `<=12` creates across 30 simulated polls, versus the old code's unbounded growth), and the startup-retry behavior with a DLQ record per attempt. All pre-existing tests needed zero changes (`time.sleep` was already mocked in every relevant test via `patch.object(aw.time, "sleep", ...)`, so the new real sleep calls are no-ops under test exactly like the old ones). `alert_writer` suite: 51/51 passed (47 pre-existing + 4 new... 7 counting both classes, some shared coverage). `incident_builder` suite: 42/42 passed. Both suites run together: 93/93 passed, no cross-module collision. Confirmed live against the real, still-broken Pandaproxy: backoff now visibly paces at 1s/2s/4s/8s/16s/32s in the real container logs exactly as designed, instead of the previous unbounded spin. | `services/alert-writer-service/main.py`, `services/incident-builder-service/main.py`, `tests/alert_writer/test_alert_writer.py`, `tests/incident_builder/test_incident_builder.py` | [#60](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/60) | 2026-07-13 |

Both `Dockerfile`s were missing `COPY` steps for `otlp_export.py` (OBS-OTEL-TRACING phase 5) and `pg_pool.py` (PERF-DB-CONN-LEAK, an earlier commit this session per `git log`) — both files exist in the service directories and are `import`ed by `main.py`, but were never added to either `Dockerfile`'s explicit file list when those features shipped. Fixed by adding `COPY --chown=app:app otlp_export.py ./` and `COPY --chown=app:app pg_pool.py ./` to both Dockerfiles.
