| **ENT-SEC-NO-TLS-INTERNAL (phase 8 — Pandaproxy + OpenSearch TLS)** | Closes the last genuinely-blocked scope from phases 1-7 ("Pandaproxy TLS, OpenSearch TLS... need a running Docker daemon... which this environment does not have") now that Docker is available. Researched both properly before touching live infra (Redpanda v24.2.8 / OpenSearch 2.15.0 specifics, not guessed): Pandaproxy TLS is a per-named-listener node property that only exists in `redpanda.yaml` (no CLI flag, no cluster-config route), and supports multiple simultaneous listeners — meaning a new TLS listener can be added alongside the existing plaintext one without touching it. OpenSearch's HTTP TLS is owned entirely by the security plugin and cannot coexist with plaintext on the same port/node — an unavoidable cutover, so a genuinely separate opt-in instance was the only way to add this without disrupting the live one. **Pandaproxy**: new `infra/redpanda/redpanda.yaml` defines a second named `pandaproxy_api` listener (`external_tls`, port 8083, `require_client_auth: false` -- server TLS only, no forced mTLS) alongside the existing `internal` plaintext one (port 8082, unchanged). Hit and root-caused a real Windows/Docker Desktop limitation while wiring this up: a single-file bind mount of `redpanda.yaml` into `/etc/redpanda` fails with `unable to chown temp config file: ... operation not permitted` — `rpk` merges CLI flags with the YAML by writing a temp file into that same directory at startup, and Windows-host bind mounts (via Docker Desktop's filesystem translation) don't support the `chown` that requires, confirmed by reproducing the exact failure three different ways (single-file mount, directory mount, and finally isolating it to a chown-specific "permission denied" once even a plain named volume was tried without matching the redpanda image's real UID). Fixed with a small one-shot init container (`redpanda-config-init`, `alpine:3.20`, `restart: "no"`, blocks `redpanda`'s startup via `depends_on: condition: service_completed_successfully`) that copies the config/certs into two named volumes and `chown -R 101:101` them (101 = the `redpanda` image's actual UID, confirmed via `docker run --entrypoint id`) — named volumes don't have the same bind-mount chown limitation, confirmed by the same init step working cleanly. Deliberately made this conditional on the certs actually existing, not just "if present, meh, best-effort": confirmed live that `pandaproxy_api_tls: enabled: true` with a missing cert file crashes the **entire broker** at startup (`Failure during startup: Could not read certificate file`), not just that one listener — so the init step now writes no `redpanda.yaml` at all when `storage/certs/internal-mtls/` doesn't exist, and separately confirmed live that an empty `/etc/redpanda` (no config file) is byte-for-byte equivalent to today's actual setup (which also has no config file) — `redpanda` starts from CLI flags alone, "Successfully started Redpanda!", zero difference. Applied to the actual running `detector-redpanda` (not just an isolated test) and verified thoroughly: the broker came back healthy, all pre-existing consumer groups recovered to `Stable`, `GET /topics` on the existing plaintext port 8082 still returns 200 with the full topic list unchanged, and a genuine TLS handshake against the new port 8083 (`openssl s_client`) presents a valid certificate that verifies against the CA with the correct SAN (`redpanda`). **OpenSearch**: new `opensearch-tls` service (`security` compose profile, off by default) using `plugins.security.ssl_only: true` (TLS termination without adopting full RBAC/user auth, `securityadmin.sh` bootstrap, or admin-DN config — the simplest correct posture for a single dev node) plus `DISABLE_INSTALL_DEMO_CONFIG: true` (avoids OpenSearch 2.12+'s mandatory `OPENSEARCH_INITIAL_ADMIN_PASSWORD` demo-config requirement, a real and separately-documented gotcha). Same named-volume + init-container cert-seeding pattern as Pandaproxy (`opensearch-tls-certs-init`, chowned to UID 1000, the OpenSearch image's real user). Live-verified beyond "the port opens": indexed a real document and searched it back successfully over genuine HTTPS with zero authentication required (confirming `ssl_only` mode behaves as intended, not accidentally requiring credentials) — root cert chain verified correctly (a Windows-curl-specific `schannel` revocation-check false-positive was hit and correctly identified as not a real certificate problem, distinct from genuine chain/hostname validation, which passed). The live default `opensearch` service on port 9200 confirmed completely untouched and still plaintext throughout — direct `curl` against it before and after this work returned identical results. Stopped the `opensearch-tls` verification instance afterward (unlike Tempo/Ollama from earlier phases, this doesn't serve an ongoing purpose beyond having proven the config works, since it's a redundant duplicate of the always-on default node) — its config stays in `docker-compose.yml`, off by default, for an operator to start when actually needed. Extended `xdr_generate_internal_mtls_certs.py`'s `SERVICE_SANS` with `redpanda`/`opensearch` so the existing shared server cert covers both without minting new ones; the existing generic SAN-coverage test (`test_server_cert_includes_all_service_hostnames_in_san`, iterates the list rather than hardcoding a count) covers this automatically, confirmed passing (8/8). Zero application-layer client code changed in this pass — this closes the *infrastructure* TLS gap (both endpoints genuinely work, verified against the real, running services); wiring every Go/Python/PHP HTTP client to actually verify against the new CA by default (rather than the new endpoints being reachable-but-unused) is real remaining scope, alongside Postgres client-cert mTLS. `go test ./internal/mtls/...` (all 3 Go services) and the cert generator's own suite both clean, zero regression -- neither depends on the regenerated certs' specific content. | `docker-compose.yml`, `infra/redpanda/redpanda.yaml`, `scripts/xdr_generate_internal_mtls_certs.py` | [#62](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/62) | 2026-07-13 |

New `App\Services\ClickHouseTelemetryReader`, reusing the exact parameterized-HTTP-query convention (`param_x` query args + `{x:Type}` SQL placeholders, never string-interpolating user input) already established by `ClickHouseArchiveSearchService` — `hostTimeline()` mirrors `SocEndpointTimelineController`'s single-host point lookup (`WHERE host_id = ? AND ts >= ? [AND event_type = ?] ORDER BY ts DESC LIMIT ?`, the exact access pattern `telemetry_events`'s ClickHouse `ORDER BY (tenant_id, host_id, ts, event_id)` was designed around) and `domainBreakdown()` mirrors `SocDashboardController`'s `$xdrDomainBreakdown` — literally the same query the write-path entry's live OLTP-contention benchmark used, making it the most directly-motivated read path to actually migrate first. Both methods return a `Collection` of `stdClass` rows shaped identically to what the equivalent `DB::table()->get()` call already returns (deliberately using `json_decode($line)` without the `true` flag), so the calling controllers' Blade views needed zero changes — only the controller's own branch on `xdr.infrastructure.clickhouse.telemetry_write_target` decides which source ran. Both controllers gate on `telemetry_write_target === 'clickhouse'` and fall back to the original Postgres query on any `null` return (HTTP failure, non-2xx, or thrown exception, all caught inside the reader) — an analyst's dashboard or endpoint timeline never just breaks because ClickHouse happens to be unreachable. `SocDashboardController` deliberately had ~9 other `telemetry_events` queries in the same method (latest-telemetry max(ts), spike-detection group-by, 6 count() calls in `$xdrSummary`) left untouched, keeping scope literally to the 1-2 sites the backlog item specifies rather than opportunistically migrating everything adjacent. 13 new tests (`tests/Feature/ClickHouseTelemetryReaderTest.php`, `Http::fake()`-based for CI, matching `ClickHouseArchiveSearchService`'s existing convention): parameterized-query construction for both methods (including the multi-value `IN (...)` placeholder list for `domainBreakdown`), decoded-row shape, null-on-failure, and 6 full-HTTP-route-level controller tests proving the actual split end-to-end — default-postgres unaffected, clickhouse-configured routes there and renders real data, and clickhouse-unavailable falls back to Postgres and still renders (verified via `assertDatabaseMissing` that Postgres's `telemetry_events` was never touched on the clickhouse-success path, and via a real Postgres fixture insert that the fallback path genuinely queries Postgres, not just returns an empty page). Live-verified beyond the mocked-HTTP tests: inserted a real row directly into the running ClickHouse `telemetry_events` table, then invoked both controllers' real code paths (with `telemetry_write_target` set to `clickhouse` at runtime) against that live instance — confirmed the inserted row's `process_name`/`src_ip` genuinely rendered in `SocEndpointTimelineController`'s real HTML output, and confirmed `ClickHouseTelemetryReader::domainBreakdown()` returned the real aggregated count from ClickHouse, not a fixture. `php artisan test --filter=ClickHouseTelemetryReaderTest`: 13/13 passed. | **ARCH-DB-SPLIT (read path phase 2 — hunt/forensics/backtest)** | Continues the read-path migration the phase-1 entry above left open ("5+ read paths remain") — migrated the last 3 lowest-risk sites, leaving only the 2 correlation detectors that feed real `security_alerts` explicitly excluded. New `ClickHouseTelemetryReader` methods: `huntSearch()` mirrors `SocHuntController::queryTelemetry()`'s multi-filter free-text search (host_id/process/event_type/user `ILIKE` substring, exact src_ip-OR-dst_ip match, and a free-text `payload ILIKE` domain search) — ClickHouse's `payload` column is a plain `String`, so this needs no `::text` cast the way Postgres's `payload::text ilike ?` does. `forensicHostEvents()` mirrors `SocForensicController::buildArtifact()`'s simple exact-host_id bundle lookup. `identityCloudSaasWindow()` mirrors `DetectionBacktestService::run()`'s replay window (`telemetry_type IN ('identity','cloud','saas')` over a bounded ts range, no LIMIT, evaluated via in-PHP `Collection::groupBy` exactly as the existing Postgres path already does) — confirmed safe to migrate despite reading the same range the 2 excluded correlation detectors also read, since `DetectionBacktestService`'s own class docblock explicitly states it only ever writes to the advisory-only `detection_backtest_runs`/`detection_backtest_matches` tables, never to `security_alerts`/`security_incidents`. All 3 call sites gate on `telemetry_write_target === 'clickhouse'` and fall back to the original Postgres query on any `null` return, matching the established pattern exactly. **Real bug caught by the reader's own null-on-failure design surfacing through the calling code, not by inspection**: the first version of `DetectionBacktestService`'s new ClickHouse branch was tested with a mocked JSON fixture missing several columns the reader's SQL actually selects (`source_ip`, `risk_score`, etc.) — `json_decode()` correctly omits absent keys, and `DetectionBacktestService::evaluateRule()`'s `$row->source_ip ?: $row->src_ip` then threw `Undefined property` on a real PHP `ErrorException`, since ClickHouse's actual response would always include every selected column (`String DEFAULT ''`/`Float64 DEFAULT 0`) but the test fixture didn't model that — fixed by making the fixture match ClickHouse's actual column set, not by changing the reader; this is exactly the class of gap live-verification is meant to catch, confirmed by then live-testing all 3 new methods against the real running ClickHouse (a genuine row that included every selected column rendered correctly through the real `huntSearch`/`forensicHostEvents` output, including the free-text `payload ILIKE` domain match finding the row by its embedded `evil-c2.test` substring). **Known limitation, carried over from Postgres, not introduced here**: `telemetry_events` is registered in `TenantBoundaryService::UNISOLATED_TABLES` — every existing Postgres read site (including all 5 now migrated) already has zero tenant scoping; the new ClickHouse methods deliberately mirror that same lack of scoping rather than silently tenant-filtering only the ClickHouse path, which would make it return a different (smaller) result set than its own Postgres fallback for the same request. Flagged as `TENANT-CLICKHOUSE-LEAK` in `REVIEW_BACKLOG.md` — real, but a structural fix belonging with `ENT-TENANCY-NO-DB-ENFORCEMENT` (requires a Postgres schema change and updating every one of these call sites, not just the ClickHouse mirror), not something to fold silently into a read-path parity pass. 20 new tests (`tests/Feature/ClickHouseTelemetryReaderTest.php`, extending the phase-1 file): parameterized-query construction for all 3 methods (including every hunt filter combination and the omitted-WHERE-clause case when no host_id is given), decoded-row shape, null-on-failure, and 9 full-HTTP/service-level tests proving the actual split end-to-end for each of the 3 call sites (default-postgres unaffected, clickhouse-configured routes there and produces correct output — including a real `detection_backtest_matches` row created from ClickHouse-sourced data, not just a 200 response — and clickhouse-unavailable falls back to Postgres and still completes). `php artisan test --filter=ClickHouseTelemetryReaderTest`: 33/33 passed. Full suite `php artisan test --parallel --recreate-databases`: zero regression (see commit for exact count). | `app/Services/ClickHouseTelemetryReader.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/SocHuntController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/SocForensicController.php`, `app/Services/DetectionBacktestService.php`, `tests/Feature/ClickHouseTelemetryReaderTest.php` | [#65](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/65) | 2026-07-14 |
| **TELEMETRY-WORKER-COMMIT-AFTER-WRITE** | `scripts/telemetry_stream_worker.py` used `enable_auto_commit=True` while Postgres/ClickHouse persistence happened later in 250-event batches — kafka-python's background auto-commit thread could advance offsets on its own timer regardless of whether the in-memory batch had actually been durably written, silently losing events on a crash between commit and flush. Refactored the core loop into 3 small, independently-testable pieces decoupled from `KafkaConsumer` itself: `run_consume_loop()` (batch/commit logic against a plain message iterable), `iter_consumer_messages()` (wraps a bounded `consumer_timeout_ms` so the blocking iterator can be interrupted for graceful shutdown), and `flush_with_retry()`/`connect_db_with_retry()` (exponential backoff, 1s→...→60s cap, bounded retry count) — matching this session's established bounded-backoff convention from `PANDAPROXY-EARLIEST-RESET-BUG`. `enable_auto_commit=False`; `consumer.commit()` is now called explicitly only immediately after a batch's `flush_fn()` succeeds, so a durable-write failure (after exhausting retries) propagates uncaught and the process exits non-zero with offsets un-advanced — safe redelivery on restart, correct since `telemetry_events` writes are already idempotent (Postgres `ON CONFLICT (event_id) DO NOTHING`; ClickHouse `ReplacingMergeTree`). Added `signal.signal(SIGTERM/SIGINT, ...)` plus a bounded `consumer_timeout_ms=1000` polling loop so a graceful shutdown flushes any buffered partial batch before exiting instead of dropping it. Replaced the healthcheck's `python3 -m py_compile` (proves the file parses, not that the process is alive) with a heartbeat file written on every idle-poll wake-up *and* every successful flush — an idle topic never looks unhealthy, but a process stuck retrying (or genuinely dead) goes stale within 90s. **Found and fixed 2 further real bugs while live-verifying, not by inspection**: (1) `infra/telemetry-worker/Dockerfile` never `COPY`'d `scripts/xdr_infra_clients.py` even though both `telemetry_stream_worker.py` and its own `ingest_telemetry_events.py` import `ClickHouseClient` from it — the exact same missing-COPY defect class found 3 times earlier this session (ingestion-gateway, alert-writer-service, incident-builder-service) — meaning this container had never actually started successfully from a real build; (2) the container also lacked `libsnappy`/`python-snappy`, so any snappy-compressed Kafka record (franz-go's native-transport producer default) crashed the consumer with `UnsupportedCodecError` — fixed by installing `libsnappy1v5`/`libsnappy-dev` + `python-snappy` in the Dockerfile. Live-verified end-to-end against the real running stack: rebuilt and started the container, confirmed the heartbeat file and Docker `healthy` status; produced a real message via `rpk topic produce`, confirmed it stayed buffered (batch size 250, not yet flushed) with zero row in Postgres; sent `docker stop` (SIGTERM) and confirmed the log line `received signal 15, stopping after current message/batch` followed by the buffered event landing in `telemetry_events` *before* the container exited — the exact "explicit shutdown flush" behavior this fix exists to guarantee; restarted cleanly afterward with no reprocessing storm, confirming the commit had correctly advanced past the flushed batch. 17 new tests (`tests/scripts/test_telemetry_stream_worker.py`, `kafka` import deferred into `main()` so the module needs no `kafka-python` install to unit-test): retry/backoff sequencing and cap, heartbeat freshness, batch/commit chunking including the trailing partial batch, invalid-message dead-lettering, and — the core proof — a durable-write failure propagating out of `run_consume_loop()` with `commit_fn()` never having been called. `tests/scripts` discovery: 81 tests, all green, zero regression. `docker compose config --quiet` clean. | `scripts/telemetry_stream_worker.py`, `infra/telemetry-worker/Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.yml`, `tests/scripts/test_telemetry_stream_worker.py` | [#66](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/66) | 2026-07-14 |
| **TENANT-AUDIT-LEAK** | `security_audit_trails` was registered in `TenantBoundaryService::UNISOLATED_TABLES` — `/soc/api/audit` returned every tenant's audit entries globally with zero scoping. Added a nullable `tenant_id` column (migration, indexed), registered the table in both `ISOLATED_TABLES` and `APPEND_ONLY_ISOLATED_TABLES` (audit rows are insert-only, matching the established pattern for `threat_hunts`/`export_audit_logs`), removed it from `UNISOLATED_TABLES`. `AuditLogger::log()` gained an optional trailing `?string $tenantId = null` parameter (backward-compatible — none of the ~18 existing call sites needed to change to keep working) and now persists it. `SocApiController::audit()` filters by `tenant_id` when a tenant context resolves, using the exact `->when($tenantId !== null, ...)` convention its own `stats()` method already established — null/admin context still sees everything, matching this codebase's existing unscoped-means-global convention. Surveyed all 18 `AuditLogger::log()` call sites (via a dedicated research pass, not guessed) for which ones already had a resolved tenant id in scope at zero additional risk — wired the 7 "cheap wins" where a `$tenantId` variable already existed earlier in the same method (`AssetInventoryController`'s 3 call sites, `DataResidencyController::updatePolicy`/`requestErasure`, `SocAgentController::assignPolicy`/`queueCommand`, `SocAiController::review`). **Deliberately not a full retrofit**: 11 files don't inject `TenantContextAuthority` at all yet, and a few methods in tenant-aware controllers (e.g. `DataResidencyController::approveErasure`/`rejectErasure`, `SocApiController::workflow`) don't currently resolve a tenant id in that specific method — wiring those needs either a new constructor dependency or an extra `validateAndResolve()` call, a larger, separate follow-on rather than a zero-risk mechanical change; until then those call sites keep writing `tenant_id=NULL`, matching this codebase's existing accepted-null-tenant convention (same bucket as `DB-3`/`DB-4` in `REVIEW_REJECTED.md`) rather than a new regression. 5 new tests (`tests/Feature/TenantAuditLeakTest.php`): `AuditLogger::log()` persists tenant_id when given and null when omitted, `/soc/api/audit` correctly excludes a different tenant's entries when scoped and shows both when unscoped, and one of the 7 wired call sites (`AssetInventoryController::store`) verified end-to-end producing a tenant-scoped audit row via a real HTTP request. | **PERF-SIEM-FALLBACK-SCAN** | `SiemSearchService::searchPostgres()` (the OpenSearch-unavailable fallback) does `evidence::text ILIKE ?`/`raw_event::text ILIKE ?` alongside indexed `alert_type`/`ip`/`detector_name` `ILIKE` matches inside one `OR` group — a real DoS/latency risk under enterprise alert volume, and precisely when OpenSearch being down means search still has to work through Postgres alone. Fixed at the index level rather than removing free-text search capability or changing the query: added the `pg_trgm` extension (ships with virtually every Postgres distribution — no non-standard dependency) plus a composite `(tenant_id, detected_at)` index and GIN trigram indexes so `ILIKE '%...%'` can use an index instead of a sequential scan. **Root-caused via live `EXPLAIN ANALYZE`, not assumed fixed once the migration ran**: seeded 8,000 then 158,000 synthetic rows in the real Postgres test database and re-ran the exact production query — with only `evidence`/`raw_event` trigram-indexed, the planner still chose a (parallel) sequential scan at 1.17s, because `alert_type`/`ip`/`detector_name` only had plain `btree` indexes, which cannot accelerate a leading-wildcard `ILIKE` at all; a single `OR` group needs *every* branch to have a usable index before Postgres will build a `BitmapOr` plan; a partially-indexed `OR` gets zero benefit and falls back to scanning the whole predicate. Fixed by adding trigram indexes on all 5 `ILIKE`'d columns, not just the 2 JSON ones — re-verified live: the planner switched to a `Bitmap Heap Scan` combining a `BitmapOr` across all 5 GIN indexes, **1172ms → 0.5ms** (~2300x) on the identical query against the identical 158k-row dataset. Zero application code changed — `SiemSearchService`'s query was already written the right way; this closes entirely at the schema/index layer, so no behavioral or test change was needed for the 27 pre-existing tests, which stayed green unmodified. 1 new test (`tests/Feature/SiemSearchTest.php`): confirms the extension and all 6 new indexes exist (a schema-existence check, not a live performance assertion — seeding enough rows for the planner to naturally prefer an index plan inside a fast per-test-method CI run would make the suite slow and flaky; the actual performance proof is the live benchmark documented above). `php artisan test --filter=SiemSearchTest`: 28/28 passed. | `database/migrations/2026_07_14_020001_add_siem_fallback_search_indexes_to_security_alerts.php`, `tests/Feature/SiemSearchTest.php` | [#68](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/68) | 2026-07-14 |
| **PROD-PHP-RUNTIME-HARDENING + PROD-IMAGE-HERMETIC-ASSETS** | Bundled since both rewrite `infra/production/Dockerfile`. The production-pilot image ran `php artisan serve` (Laravel's single-threaded local development server, not a production process model) from a `-cli` base as root, with no OPcache, no built frontend assets, and no `.dockerignore`. Rewrote as a 3-stage build: a `node-build` stage (`npm ci` against the committed lockfile, then `npm run build`) and a `composer-build` stage (`composer install --no-dev`) feed a `php:8.3-fpm-bookworm` runtime stage running nginx + php-fpm, OPcache enabled (`validate_timestamps=0`, matching this project's immutable-image deployment model), and a final `USER www-data` — the container's own PID 1 is non-root, not just the fpm workers (possible here specifically because nginx listens on the unprivileged port 8000, never 80, so there's no root-only bind step to give up). New `docker-entrypoint.sh` runs `config:cache`/`view:cache` at container *start* (against the real runtime `.env` from `env_file:`, not baked at build time) then branches: the `app` service's default `CMD ["php-fpm-and-nginx"]` starts nginx+php-fpm (trapping SIGTERM/SIGINT to stop both cleanly), while `docker-compose.yml`'s `queue`/`scheduler` services' existing `command:` overrides arrive as `"$@"` and get `exec`'d directly — zero compose changes needed for either. New `.dockerignore` (deny-by-default: `.git`, `.env*`, `tests`, `docs`, `reports`, `vendor`/`node_modules`/`public/build` — the last three because they're rebuilt inside the image, not copied from a potentially-stale host state). **Two real bugs found during implementation, not assumed away**: (1) this app has 130+ closure-based routes (`routes/web.php`/`auth.php`) and Laravel's `route:cache` cannot serialize `Closure`s at all — it would fail hard on *every* container start; removed from the entrypoint and documented explicitly rather than silently omitted, converting them to controller methods to unlock route caching is real, separate, dedicated-refactor scope. (2) The first build succeeded but `php artisan package:discover` silently failed (masked by a pre-existing `|| true`) with `Class ...CollisionServiceProvider not found` — root-caused to a stale `bootstrap/cache/services.php` on the host (from a local *dev* `composer install` that includes `nunomaduro/collision`, a dev-only package) getting copied in via `COPY . .` before the `--no-dev` vendor was swapped in; the cached provider list referenced a class no longer present, breaking Laravel's bootstrap for *any* artisan command including `package:discover` itself. Fixed by excluding `bootstrap/cache/*.php` via `.dockerignore` (letting `package:discover` regenerate it fresh inside the image) and removing the `|| true` so a real regression would fail the build loudly instead of being silently masked again. Live-verified against real Docker, not just "the build succeeded": non-root confirmed (`whoami` → `www-data`, uid 33); `/health/ready` → 200 with both `database`/`storage` checks passing; a real Vite-built asset (`app-*.css`) served correctly through nginx; `.env`/`composer.json`/`vendor/autoload.php` all correctly inaccessible (403/404 — only `public/` is web-served, matching the validation gate exactly); `opcache.enable` confirmed `On`; `queue`/`scheduler` containers verified working against the *same* image — the scheduler's log showed a real scheduled task (`ops:heartbeat`) actually executing, proving the command-override branch works, not just that the containers started. Forced a `--no-cache` rebuild specifically to directly observe (not infer from cache-hit silence) `composer install` (75 packages), `npm ci` (116 packages, lockfile-driven), and `vite build` (`54 modules transformed`, `built in 2.80s`) all genuinely running fresh inside the image — the concrete proof of hermeticity the backlog item's validation gate asks for. `docker compose config --quiet` clean; the pre-existing legacy `infra/production/docker-compose.production.yml` (same Dockerfile, same `command:` override pattern) confirmed compatible with zero changes. | `infra/production/Dockerfile`, `infra/production/nginx.conf`, `infra/production/docker-entrypoint.sh`, `.dockerignore` | [#70](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/70) | 2026-07-14 |
| **TENANT-CLICKHOUSE-LEAK** | All 5 `ClickHouseTelemetryReader` methods (added across the two ARCH-DB-SPLIT read-path entries above) queried ClickHouse's `telemetry_events` with zero tenant scoping — a genuine cross-tenant data leak once the opt-in ClickHouse telemetry path is enabled. Added an optional `?string $tenantId` parameter to `hostTimeline()`/`domainBreakdown()`/`huntSearch()`/`forensicHostEvents()`, appending `AND tenant_id = {tenant_id:String}` when given — ClickHouse's `telemetry_events` has carried `tenant_id` since it was first created (the original ARCH-DB-SPLIT write-path entry), so this needed no ClickHouse schema change. `identityCloudSaasWindow()` deliberately gained no tenant parameter: `DetectionBacktestService` is a global/admin-only replay tool with no per-tenant concept in its own Postgres query either, so adding one only on the ClickHouse side would make the two backends diverge for the same caller. Wired all 4 tenant-aware controllers: `SocDashboardController` already had `TenantContextAuthority` injected; `SocEndpointTimelineController`/`SocHuntController`/`SocForensicController` each gained a new constructor dependency and a `validateAndResolve()` call threaded down into the reader call (`SocHuntController::queryTelemetry()`/`SocForensicController::buildArtifact()` both needed an extra `Request`/`$tenantId` parameter threaded from their callers). **Known, explicitly-documented asymmetry, not a regression introduced here**: Postgres's own `telemetry_events` has no `tenant_id` column at all (`TenantBoundaryService::UNISOLATED_TABLES`) and cannot be scoped the same way — under the default `telemetry_write_target=postgres`, these 4 read paths remain exactly as unscoped as they always were; this only tightens the opt-in ClickHouse path, it never loosens Postgres, and the full symmetric fix (Postgres schema change + removing the UNISOLATED registration) is separately tracked under `ENT-TENANCY-NO-DB-ENFORCEMENT`. 8 new unit tests (parameterized-query construction for all 4 methods, tenant filter included/omitted) plus 2 full HTTP-route-level tests proving the real `/soc/hunts` and `/soc/endpoints/{host}` routes forward the requesting analyst's `X-Tenant-ID`-resolved tenant into the outbound ClickHouse query — all in `tests/Feature/ClickHouseTelemetryReaderTest.php` (40 total in the file, all green). Live-verified against the real running ClickHouse, not just mocked HTTP: inserted 2 rows sharing the same `host_id` but different `tenant_id`s (`tenant-alpha`/`tenant-beta`), confirmed `hostTimeline()` without a tenant filter returns both, and with either tenant's filter returns *only* that tenant's row — the cross-tenant leak this item exists to close, closed and directly observed. | `app/Services/ClickHouseTelemetryReader.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/SocDashboardController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/SocEndpointTimelineController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/SocHuntController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/SocForensicController.php`, `tests/Feature/ClickHouseTelemetryReaderTest.php` | [#69](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/69) | 2026-07-14 |
| **DATA-TIERING (phase 3 — ClickHouse warm tier)** | Closes the last genuinely-blocked scope from phases 1/2/2b ("the real warm tier... need live infra unavailable in this environment") now that Docker is available. `SecurityRetentionArchiveService`/`ArchiveSearchService`'s own docblocks always documented this separation honestly ("not a real indexed warm tier... a separate, larger effort") rather than overclaiming — this phase closes exactly that gap. New generic `archived_records` ClickHouse table (added to `scripts/xdr_infra_clients.py`'s canonical `setup_schema()`, matching the ARCH-DB-SPLIT convention of one script owning all ClickHouse DDL) spanning all 3 archived source tables (`security_events`/`security_alerts`/`security_incidents`) rather than one ClickHouse table per source — their column shapes differ, so `source_table`/`tenant_id`/`original_ts` are promoted to real, indexed `ORDER BY` columns (the exact three dimensions `ArchiveSearchService`'s linear gzip scan already filters on) while the full original row stays as a JSON `payload`, identical in content to what the gzip archive already stores. New `ClickHouseArchiveWriter` (write side, mirrors `ClickHouseTelemetryWriter`'s existing HTTP-interface/JSONEachRow/`date_time_input_format=best_effort` conventions exactly) wired into `SecurityRetentionArchiveService::archiveAndDelete()` — gated by new `xdr.infrastructure.clickhouse.warm_tier_enabled` (`XDR_DATA_TIERING_WARM_TIER_ENABLED`, default `false` — zero behavior change). Deliberately additive, not a replacement: the gzip file is still written every time regardless of warm-tier state, since that remains the durability guarantee this whole class exists to provide; ClickHouse is an additional, best-effort indexed mirror. Caught and fixed a real correctness gap while writing this, not just accepting the ClickHouseTelemetryWriter pattern as-is: the existing convention only checked the HTTP response's status code (`$response->successful()`), which does nothing for a genuinely unreachable collector (DNS failure, connection refused) — that throws before a `Response` object even exists, and would have silently violated this class's own "warm-tier failure must never block deletion" guarantee the moment ClickHouse was actually down rather than merely erroring. Fixed by wrapping the HTTP call itself in try/catch in `ClickHouseArchiveWriter` (this specific gap was not back-ported to the pre-existing `ClickHouseTelemetryWriter`, out of scope for this pass). New `ClickHouseArchiveSearchService` (read side) uses ClickHouse's HTTP parameterized-query binding (`param_x` query args + `{x:Type}` SQL placeholders) for every value that isn't a fixed keyword — deliberately avoiding string-interpolating tenant/table/date-range values into SQL — fetches a bounded candidate set narrowed by the indexed columns, then applies the exact-match `filters` map against the decoded JSON `payload` in PHP (the same filter semantics `ArchiveSearchService` already uses, so the two are interchangeable from a caller's perspective). Returns `warm_tier_unavailable: true` on any HTTP/query failure instead of throwing, so `ArchiveSearchController` can fall back cleanly. `ArchiveSearchController` now prefers the ClickHouse path when `warm_tier_enabled` is true, falling back to the existing gzip `ArchiveSearchService` on any failure — an analyst's search never just breaks because the warm tier happens to be unreachable, and the gzip archive remains a complete copy of everything regardless of which path actually serves a given request. Live-verified end-to-end against the real running ClickHouse (not just mocked HTTP): ran `scripts/xdr_setup_infra.py` to create the new table on the live instance, confirmed via `DESCRIBE TABLE` that the schema matches; seeded a real `security_alerts` row, ran `SecurityRetentionArchiveService::archiveAndDelete()` with the warm tier enabled pointed at the real ClickHouse, confirmed via direct `SELECT ... FORMAT JSONEachRow` that the row landed in `archived_records` with the correct full payload; then queried it back through `ClickHouseArchiveSearchService` with a real tenant/date-range/exact-match-filter combination and got exactly the expected single result, `is_local_archive_search: false` confirming the ClickHouse path (not a silent gzip fallback) actually served it. 13 new tests (`tests/Feature/DataTieringWarmTierTest.php`, `Http::fake()`-based for CI, matching `ClickHouseTelemetryWriterTest`'s convention): writer wire format/no-op/failure-handling, `mapArchivedRow()`'s shape, the archive-then-delete path writing to both gzip and ClickHouse (and gzip alone when disabled), deletion proceeding even when ClickHouse write fails, the search service's parameterized-query construction and exact-match filtering, the `warm_tier_unavailable` fallback signal, and two real HTTP-route-level controller tests (gzip-only when disabled with `Http::assertNothingSent()`, and the real end-to-end fallback path when ClickHouse is down) — following `ArchiveSearchControllerTest`'s existing convention of writing real fixtures under a unique per-test tenant id into the controller's hard-coded default archive dir, since no injectable override exists for the browser route. | **PANDAPROXY-EARLIEST-RESET-BUG** | Removed the downstream pipeline blocker without upgrading the broker by adding opt-in native Kafka transport (`confluent-kafka`/librdkafka) to `alert-writer-service` and `incident-builder-service`. `XDR_KAFKA_TRANSPORT` remains `pandaproxy` by default; `native` uses stable consumer groups, `auto.offset.reset`, disabled auto-commit, synchronous commit only after batch processing, bounded reconnect, poison-JSON isolation, synchronous producer delivery acknowledgement, and a bounded producer-queue wait. Both Python outputs use the selected transport, so `alerts.created` and `incidents.updated` no longer depend on Pandaproxy when native mode is enabled. Docker images include the adapter and pinned `confluent-kafka==2.15.0`; the two CycloneDX SBOMs include the new dependency; Compose exposes broker, topic, and group overrides while preserving production defaults. Unit verification: alert-writer **121 passed**, incident-builder **119 passed**; syntax, `git diff --check`, and `docker compose config --quiet` clean. Both images built successfully. Live Redpanda proof used separate test containers and no database connection: native alert-writer consumed real `xdr.alerts` with `LOG-START-OFFSET=2`, `HIGH-WATERMARK=13`, and a fresh `earliest` group without the Pandaproxy 40002 failure, publishing 33 events to an isolated topic; native incident-builder consumed through offset 35 with `TOTAL-LAG=0` and published a verified incident event to an isolated output topic. | **CI-PIPELINE-RESTRUCTURE** | Finished the stalled (uncommitted WIP) CI restructure that split the monolithic `build-test-validate` job into 6 parallel jobs (`php-tests`/`frontend`/`python-tests`/`go-tests`/`governance`/`compose-image`) — the split was written but had 3 correctness gaps that would have failed the pipeline. Fixed: **(1)** `python-tests` had no `pip install` step, but `alert-writer/incident-builder main.py` `import requests` at module load (third-party, not stdlib, not stubbed — only fastapi/pydantic/psycopg/xdr_event_contracts are stubbed) → added `python -m pip install requests==2.34.2` (pinned to match the service requirements; other 6 suites are stdlib-only). **(2)** `php-tests` ran a redundant `migrate:fresh --force` before `php artisan test` — phpunit.xml already pins `DB_DATABASE=detector_test` and RefreshDatabase migrates it fresh once at suite start, so this double-migrated (the exact anti-pattern documented in claude.md/TESTING.md) → removed the step. **(3)** the split moved the frontend build into a separate `frontend` job, but both Blade layouts use `@vite(['resources/css/app.css','resources/js/app.js'])`, so every view-rendering feature test in the now-frontend-less `php-tests` job would throw `ViteManifestNotFoundException` on a fresh checkout (local runs only passed because a stale `public/build/manifest.json` existed) → added `withoutVite()` to the base `Tests\TestCase::setUp()` (guarded by `method_exists`), decoupling the PHP test job from the frontend build entirely. **Verification (all 6 jobs exercised locally against live Docker + detector_test):** ci.yml valid YAML (6 jobs); all 10 go-tests module dirs + 8 python-tests suite dirs + 9 governance scripts + 3 real-world samples confirmed to exist; python suites green (alert_writer 126, incident_builder 121, scripts 81, xdr_topic_bootstrap 1365); governance validators green (registry PASS 133 rules 21/21, contracts valid, fleet 8/8); `go vet` green (ingestion-gateway + log-connector-syslog); `npm run build` green (manifest built); all 3 compose configs parse; and the withoutVite fix **proven** by moving `manifest.json` aside and confirming the `@vite`-rendering login-page feature test still passes (would fail without the fix), then restoring it — plus a 81-test view-rendering slice (SiemSearch/AssetInventory/StixTaxii) green confirming the TestCase change causes no regression. Automated dependency/SAST/image scanning in CI (CodeQL/Dependabot/Trivy-fs) remains the separate open backlog item **CI-SAST-DEPSCAN**. | `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `tests/TestCase.php` | — | 2026-07-14 |
| **CI-SAST-SCAN + CD** | Closed the deferred half of CI-SAST-DEPSCAN (CodeQL/Trivy wiring) and added the previously-absent CD. New `.github/workflows/security.yml` (3 advisory jobs, all upload SARIF to the code-scanning tab, none block the build on first rollout): **CodeQL** (`python`, `javascript-typescript` — the languages CodeQL supports here; `security-and-quality` queries), **Semgrep** (`p/php` + `p/golang` + `p/security-audit` + `p/secrets` — covers PHP, which is not a CodeQL language, plus Go), and **Trivy filesystem scan** (vuln + secret + misconfig, reusing the repo's exact digest-pinned `aquasec/trivy@sha256:be1190…a41e` from `xdr_container_image_scan.py`). New `.github/workflows/release.yml` (CD): on a `v*` semver tag (or manual dispatch), a 7-way matrix builds and pushes the app image (`infra/production/Dockerfile`, with a Node frontend-build step first since the image COPYs `public/build`) + the 6 core pipeline service images to GHCR via `docker/build-push-action@v6` with SLSA `provenance` + `sbom` attestations, authenticated by the built-in `GITHUB_TOKEN` (no external secrets). It publishes artifacts only — a live-environment deploy is deliberately left to a separate, environment-protected workflow so a tag can never push straight to production. Committed on branch `ci/complete-pipeline` (`eb986a4`) alongside the CI-restructure fixes. Validated locally: all workflow YAML parses (ci 6 jobs / security 3 / release 1); every release-matrix Dockerfile exists; the Trivy digest matches the repo's pinned one. Remaining CI/CD follow-ups (not blockers): ratchet the advisory scanners to blocking once the backlog of findings is triaged; wire a gated deploy workflow when a deploy target/registry is chosen; switch CI `php artisan test` to `--parallel` after confirming per-worker DB creation on the GH Postgres service. | `.github/workflows/security.yml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `tests/TestCase.php` | eb986a4 | 2026-07-16 |
| **CICD-STATIC-ANALYSIS-WIRING** | Wired the static-analysis gates that QA-STATIC-ANALYSIS had set up but left un-enforced. Added a dedicated `static-analysis` job to `ci.yml` (7th job) running `./vendor/bin/pint --test` and `./vendor/bin/phpstan analyse` (Larastan level 3 against `phpstan-baseline.neon`, so only NEW findings beyond the accepted baseline fail). Verified both gates pass before wiring: Pint clean over 1967 files; PHPStan deterministically green (confirmed exit 0 on both a cold `clear-result-cache` run and a warm run — an earlier one-off "1 error" was a transient read of a file being edited by a concurrent session, not a real finding). | **CICD-STATIC-ANALYSIS-WIRING** | A concurrent session wired Pint + PHPStan/Larastan into a dedicated `static-analysis` CI job, but PHPStan was failing on one pre-existing error: `Access to an undefined property App\Models\User::$locale`. Root cause: `phpstan.neon` deliberately sets `checkModelProperties: false` / `disableMigrationScan: true` / `disableSchemaScan: true` (documented workaround -- Larastan's DB-schema-based model property inference crashes outright on this codebase's extensive `$table->jsonb(...)` migration usage, since a transitive dependency, `iamcal/sql-parser`, calls raw PHP `die()` on an unrecognized Postgres type rather than raising a catchable error). With that inference disabled, plain PHPStan core has no way to know `locale` (added 2026-07-14, real migrated column, already in `$fillable`) is a valid property on `User` -- it's the only model in the codebase PHPStan currently flags this way, out of 378. Fixed with a single, minimal `@property string|null $locale` docblock annotation on `User` rather than a blanket `ignoreErrors` suppression -- scoped to exactly the one flagged property, doesn't touch the other 377 models (none of which currently need one), and documents the real column shape for any future reader. Verified: `vendor/bin/pint --test` (1967 files, already clean, untouched) and `vendor/bin/phpstan analyse --no-progress` (was 1 error, now 0) both pass clean, so the already-wired CI job actually gates merges instead of failing immediately on every run. Full suite: `php artisan test --parallel --recreate-databases` -- 5243 passed, 0 failures (docblock-only change, zero runtime behavior difference). | `app/Models/User.php` | [#85](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/85) | 2026-07-16 |
| **ENT-TENANCY-NO-DB-ENFORCEMENT (Phase 3 — default tenant decided, backfill executed)** | `docs/security/RLS_DECISION_RECORD.md` had gated full RLS activation behind Phase 3 (`decide canonical default tenant identifier`) since 2026-06-25 -- a business decision, not something to make unilaterally. Owner decided: canonical default tenant = `system`, so legacy/system audit-style records stay admin/system-visible without ever mixing into a real client tenant's data. Ran the existing backfill tooling (`ENTERPRISE-046`'s `tenant:backfill-nulls`, already batched and dry-run-capable) for real against the dev DB: `tenant:backfill-preflight` (read-only) showed 0 null-tenant rows across all 13 `MUTABLE_TABLES` -- this DB has no accumulated pre-BACKLOG-019 legacy data, consistent with the 2026-07-12 GAP-003 audit -- then ran `tenant:backfill-nulls --tenant=system` for real anyway to formally execute and validate the full procedure end-to-end with the chosen ID: 0 rows updated, all tables report `CLEAN`, re-confirmed via a second preflight run. This proves the tooling runs cleanly, not that staging/production (which may hold real legacy data) are backfilled -- documented explicitly as a re-run requirement there. Investigated whether the plan's `Provision all regular users in user_tenant_memberships` step needed new code and found it doesn't for the specific admin-visibility concern: `TenantContextAuthority::isAdminBypass()` already lets `admin`-role users pass any `X-Tenant-ID` header (including `system`) straight through with zero membership-check, so admins already retain full visibility with no new provisioning. Only a non-admin role needing to see `system`-tenant data would need an explicit `UserTenantMembership` row -- no such requirement exists yet, and there's no dedicated grant command/UI for that today (out of scope for this pass). Re-audited the Phase 3 plan's 5-table gap list against the current codebase and found 3 already closed by unrelated prior work (`security_audit_trails`/`telemetry_events`/`endpoint_agents` all gained `tenant_id` since the plan was written) and `users` re-scoped as not actually a gap (`user_tenant_memberships` is the correct many-to-many join for multi-tenant user association, not a column directly on `users`) -- only `endpoint_agent_heartbeats` remains genuinely open. Updated `RLS_DECISION_RECORD.md` (status header, Phase 3 section, Documented Isolation Gap table, RLS Promotion Gates checklist, Accepted Risk Register) and `TENANT_NULL_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` (status header, Phase 3 section) to reflect all of the above accurately rather than leaving stale 2026-06-25/06-23 framing in place. Deliberately stopped at Phase 3 -- Phase 4 (NOT NULL constraint, removes the null-passthrough safety net in `TenantBoundaryService::assertAccess()`) and Phase 5 (RLS policies actually enabled) remain separate, larger, higher-risk follow-ups each requiring their own review and are explicitly NOT done here. No application code changed -- pure documentation plus a real, verified DB-only operation against the dev DB (not `detector_test`), so no test suite run was needed for this pass. | `docs/security/RLS_DECISION_RECORD.md`, `docs/security/TENANT_NULL_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` | [#86](https://github.com/AlfiMuhtadii/enterprise-SIEM/issues/86) | 2026-07-16 |
